**Four occurrences in two verses.** **And in the forty-second verse the verb is repeated twice in the same sentence** — this is a concentration verifiable within the sūra, and it will be taken up separately there.

### The name of the sūra comes from here

***Āla ʿImrān* — "the family of ʿImrān".** The construct occurs only here in the sūra.

**A note per the method (`USLUP.md`):** the Qurʾān names *ʿImrān* in two other places — 3/35 (*imraʾatu ʿImrān*) and al-Taḥrīm 66/12 (*Maryama'bnata ʿImrān*). **The Qurʾān gives no other information about this person.** The genealogical material transmitted in the classical sources is not passed on in this commentary; **the only thing the text gives is a name.**

### ذُرِّيَّةًۢ بَعْضُهَا مِنۢ بَعْضٍ

**The construction must be recorded: "an offspring, one part of it from another."**

The root **ذ-ر-ر / ذ-ر-و** — the lexicographers differ over the origin of the word; *dhurriyya* means **progeny, generation**. **The root was treated at 13/23 in `013-rad.md` and at 36/41 in `036-yasin.md`.**

**And the work of the sentence is worth recording — I give this as my own reading:** the verse counts four separate names and then binds them into **a single chain**. **That is, the choosing is given not as four separate events but as one continuing line.**

**And this is connected with a later sentence of the sūra:** at verse 68, *inna awla'n-nāsi bi-Ibrāhīma la'lladhīna'ttabaʿūh* — "the **nearest of people to Abraham are those who followed him**". **That is, the sūra builds a chain of descent and then ties nearness in that chain to *following*.** This is a weave verifiable from the text, and it will be taken up separately at verse 68.

**وَٱللَّهُ سَمِيعٌ عَلِيمٌ** — and the verse ends with the name of **hearing**. **I record this as an observation about the word order:** in the very next verse there will come **an utterance spoken** — a vow a woman turned over within herself and then put into words. **The name prepares the scene.**

---

## 3/35-36 — إِذْ قَالَتِ ٱمْرَأَتُ عِمْرَٰنَ رَبِّ إِنِّى نَذَرْتُ لَكَ مَا فِى بَطْنِى مُحَرَّرًا · فَلَمَّا وَضَعَتْهَا قَالَتْ رَبِّ إِنِّى وَضَعْتُهَآ أُنثَىٰ وَٱللَّهُ أَعْلَمُ بِمَا وَضَعَتْ وَلَيْسَ ٱلذَّكَرُ كَٱلْأُنثَىٰ

*Idh qālati'mraʾatu ʿImrāna rabbi innī nadhartu laka mā fī baṭnī muḥarraran fa-taqabbal minnī, innaka anta's-samīʿu'l-ʿalīm · Fa-lammā waḍaʿathā qālat rabbi innī waḍaʿtuhā unthā wa'llāhu aʿlamu bimā waḍaʿat, wa laysa'dh-dhakaru ka'l-unthā, wa innī sammaytuhā Maryama wa innī uʿīdhuhā bika wa dhurriyyatahā mina'sh-shayṭāni'r-rajīm*

"When **the wife of ʿImrān** said: '**My Lord! I have vowed to You what is in my womb — set free.** Accept it from me; truly You are the hearing, the knowing.' · **And when she gave birth to her she said: 'My Lord! I have given birth to a female.'** — and God knew better what she had given birth to — '**and the male is not like the female.** I have named her **Mary**; and I place her and her offspring in Your protection from the accursed Satan.'"

---

**These two verses will be worked through with care. The sentence *wa laysa'dh-dhakaru ka'l-unthā* in particular is one of the sentences on which the most mistaken rulings have been built. Per the method (`USLUP.md`) I shall first settle the question of *whose* sentence it is, then give the classical explanations in a table.**

### مُحَرَّرًا — root: ح-ر-ر

The concrete sense of the root: **ح-ر-ر — heat; and to be free, to be released from a bond.** *Ḥurr* — free; *taḥrīr* — setting loose. **The root was treated at 90/13 in `090-beled.md` (*fakku raqaba*) and at 58/3 in `058-mucadele.md` (*taḥrīru raqaba*).**

**A passive participle: *muḥarrar* — set free, released.**

**The senses given to this word in the classical commentaries:**

| # | What *muḥarrar* means | Explanation |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Dedicated to the sanctuary** | Released from worldly affairs and set apart for the service of the sanctuary |
| 2 | **Made pure** | For God alone, with no other purpose mixed in |
| 3 | **Exempt from service** | Freed from the obligation of serving his parents |

**All three are transmitted. I do not impose a preference.**

**And there is a tension in the word itself, and I record it as an observation:** *muḥarrar* means "set free"; but in the sentence this freedom is given **by a vow**. **That is, being loosed from everything else by being bound to one thing.** The word carries the logic of a vow by itself.

### وَضَعَتْ — a verb repeated three times

The root **و-ض-ع**: **to put a thing down, to set it down.** Its use for giving birth comes from here — in Arabic *waḍaʿati'l-marʾa* means "the woman gave birth"; **the picture of the root is the setting down of a carried load.**

**And the verb occurs four times in two verses:**

| Order | Sentence | Who says it |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | *Fa-lammā **waḍaʿathā*** | **The narrator** |
| 2 | *Innī **waḍaʿtuhā** unthā* | **The woman** |
| 3 | *Wa'llāhu aʿlamu bimā **waḍaʿat*** | **The narrator — breaking in** |
| 4 | (*mā fī baṭnī*, 35 — indirectly) | The woman |

**The third occurrence must be recorded, because the whole question of the verse is knotted there.**

### Who is speaking? — the question of whose sentence it is

**There is an *interposed clause* (*jumla muʿtariḍa*) in the verse, and this is plainly recorded in the classical commentaries:**

> *"…qālat rabbi innī waḍaʿtuhā unthā — **wa'llāhu aʿlamu bimā waḍaʿat** — wa laysa'dh-dhakaru ka'l-unthā…"*

**The middle sentence (*wa'llāhu aʿlamu bimā waḍaʿat* — "and God knew better what she had given birth to") is not the woman's words; it is the narrator breaking in.** On this there is broad agreement among the sources.

**And the real disagreement is about the sentence after it: *wa laysa'dh-dhakaru ka'l-unthā.* Whose speech does this sentence continue?**

| Reading | Whose the sentence is | The direction of the sentence |
|---|---|---|
| **A** | **A continuation of the woman's words** — she returns to her speech after the interposed clause | The woman is voicing her own expectation: "I had wanted a male; he would not have been like this female" — **a human being's own reckoning** |
| **B** | **God's words** — a continuation of the interposed clause | **An answer given to the woman's reckoning**: "the male is not like this female" — that is, what was expected is not the equal of what was given |

**Both readings are transmitted in the classical sources. I do not impose a preference.**

**And it should be seen how much the sense changes between the two readings — this shows why the sentence before us must be worked through with care:**

| | **Reading A** | **Reading B** |
|---|---|---|
| Whose the sentence is | **The human being's** | **The narrator's / God's** |
| What the sentence does | Records an **expectation** | **Corrects** an expectation |
| What *al-dhakar* is | What was expected, **hoped for** | What was expected, **the lesser** |
| To whom it gives precedence | **To neither** — a note of a state | **To the female** — because *ka'l-unthā* is taken as the measure |

**And now, per the method (`USLUP.md`), I enter a note, because this sentence is often misused:**

**One. Whichever reading is taken, the sentence is not a sentence of *general ruling*.** It is spoken inside a scene, at the moment of a birth, in the context of a particular vow. **Every element around the sentence is particular: a woman, a vow, a birth, a name.**

**Two. The word order of the sentence does not *support* a reading of disparagement; it blocks it.** In Arabic, in the pattern *laysa A ka-B*, **the measure is B** — A is what is likened, B is what it is likened to. That is, the sentence makes *unthā* the measure and *dhakar* what is measured. **This is a fact of word order that comes out of the wording itself, and it has been recorded in the classical commentaries as well.**

**Three. And the continuation of the verse confirms it.** What happens immediately after the sentence: a name is given to the girl (*Maryam*), protection is sought, and then in verse 37 it is said *"Her Lord accepted her with a good acceptance"*. **That is, the vow *is accepted* — and what was expected in the vow was a male.** **The flow of the text describes not a deficiency but a change of place.**

**Four. And the sixth verse of the sūra had laid down the ruling of this scene in advance:** *huwa'lladhī yuṣawwirukum fi'l-arḥāmi kayfa yashāʾ* — "**He it is who shapes you in the wombs as He wills.**" **Thirty verses apart, in the same sūra.** This is a link verifiable from the text.

**I record this as my own reading, and it is not binding:** what the two verses do together is to show the difference between a person's **plan** and what comes about. The woman had made a plan; the plan held, but **not in the shape she expected.** And the sūra will tell this not as a loss but as an outcome greater than the plan itself — because the child born will be settled at the centre of the family from which the sūra takes its name.

**And I record this explicitly: no disparaging ruling about women can be drawn from this sentence; none is drawn in this commentary.** What the sentence addresses is not a sex but **an expectation.**

### وَإِنِّى سَمَّيْتُهَا مَرْيَمَ

**The verb of naming is worth recording:** *sammaytuhā* — "**I** named her". The pronoun is emphatic (*innī*).

**And the name will occur six times in the second half of the sūra.** **In `019-meryem.md` a sūra bears this name**, and Mary's account was treated there in detail.

**Per the method (`USLUP.md`) I record this: differing views are transmitted in the classical sources about the meaning of the name, and there is no certain knowledge.** I make no claim about its meaning in this commentary.

**ٱلشَّيْطَٰنِ ٱلرَّجِيمِ** — *rajīm*: from the root **ر-ج-م**, **stoned, driven off.** **The root was treated at 37/7 in `037-saffat.md` and at 67/5 in `067-mulk.md` (*rujūman li'sh-shayāṭīn*).**

And the extent of the request must be recorded: *uʿīdhuhā bika **wa dhurriyyatahā*** — "her **and her offspring**". **That is, the request for protection is stretched a generation further.** And that offspring will, later in the sūra, consist of a single person.

---

## 3/37 — فَتَقَبَّلَهَا رَبُّهَا بِقَبُولٍ حَسَنٍ وَأَنۢبَتَهَا نَبَاتًا حَسَنًا وَكَفَّلَهَا زَكَرِيَّا كُلَّمَا دَخَلَ عَلَيْهَا زَكَرِيَّا ٱلْمِحْرَابَ وَجَدَ عِندَهَا رِزْقًا

*Fa-taqabbalahā rabbuhā bi-qabūlin ḥasanin wa anbatahā nabātan ḥasanan wa kaffalahā Zakariyyā, kullamā dakhala ʿalayhā Zakariyya'l-miḥrāba wajada ʿindahā rizqā, qāla yā Maryamu annā laki hādhā, qālat huwa min ʿindi'llāh, inna'llāha yarzuqu man yashāʾu bi-ghayri ḥisāb*

"**Her Lord accepted her with a good acceptance and made her grow as a good plant grows,** and **He gave Zechariah charge of her.** **Whenever Zechariah entered upon her in the sanctuary he found provision beside her.** '**Mary! Where does this come to you from?**' he said. And she said: '**It is from God.** **Truly God provides for whom He wills without reckoning.**'"

### أَنۢبَتَهَا نَبَاتًا حَسَنًا — like a plant

The root **ن-ب-ت**: **a plant's coming up out of the ground.** Form IV: *anbata* — to make grow, to raise.

**And the construction of the comparison must be recorded, and the lexicographers dwell on it:** the object of the verb is a human being, but its verbal noun is **the verbal noun of a plant** (*nabātan*). In Arabic this is called the absolute object (*mafʿūl muṭlaq*), and it reinforces the verb.

**That is, the sentence does not say "He raised her well"; it says "He made her grow as one makes a plant grow."**

**I record this as my own reading, and it is not binding:** the comparison carries the sense that the growing happened **of itself** and **unseen**. A plant is not seen in the moment of its growing; whoever looks finds it each time a little further grown. **And this picture agrees with what follows in the verse:** every time Zechariah entered he found **something new**.

### كُلَّمَا دَخَلَ عَلَيْهَا … وَجَدَ عِندَهَا رِزْقًا

**The word order must be recorded: *kullamā* — "whenever".** **This particle conveys repetition, and although the two verbs that follow it are both perfect, the sentence conveys *continuance*.**

**That is, the scene is not a one-time event; it is a repeated state.**

**ٱلْمِحْرَاب** — root **ح-ر-ب**. **The lexicographers differ over the origin of the word;** it is commonly explained as **the foremost, highest or innermost part of a place**. **The present-day sense of *miḥrāb* (the niche where the one leading the prayer stands) is a usage that settled later and cannot be read back into the verse.** I record this explicitly.

**The word occurs twice in the sūra** — at 37 (beside Mary) and at 39 (Zechariah himself there). **This is a repetition verifiable within the sūra, and it binds the two scenes to the same place.**

### Question and answer

**The scene consists of a question and an answer, and the word order of both is worth recording:**

| Speaker | Words | What it does |
|---|---|---|
| **Zechariah** | *Annā laki hādhā* — "**Where** does this come to you from?" | Asks for a **cause** |
| **Mary** | *Huwa **min ʿindi'llāh*** — "It is **from God**" | Names not a cause but a **source** |

**I record this as an observation:** the question is looking for an intermediary; the answer puts nothing in between. **And the last sentence of the answer comes as a rule: *inna'llāha yarzuqu man yashāʾu bi-ghayri ḥisāb.***

**And this sentence is word for word the same as the last sentence of verse 27 of the sūra:** *wa tarzuqu man tashāʾu bi-ghayri ḥisāb* (27) — *inna'llāha yarzuqu man yashāʾu bi-ghayri ḥisāb* (37).

| Verse | Who says it | About whom |
|---|---|---|
| **27** | **The one praying** — second person (*tarzuqu*) | Addressed to God |
| **37** | **Mary** — third person (*yarzuqu*) | About an event |

**The same sentence, one in a prayer and one in a scene.** This is a repetition verifiable within the sūra. **I record this as my own reading, and it is not binding:** what is said in the twenty-seventh verse as a **principle** is shown in the thirty-seventh as **having come about in a room**. The sūra states the principle and gives its instance ten verses later.

**And in the next verse this scene will have a consequence: Zechariah will pray.**

---

## 3/38-41 — هُنَالِكَ دَعَا زَكَرِيَّا رَبَّهُۥ قَالَ رَبِّ هَبْ لِى مِن لَّدُنكَ ذُرِّيَّةً طَيِّبَةً إِنَّكَ سَمِيعُ ٱلدُّعَآءِ

*Hunālika daʿā Zakariyyā rabbah, qāla rabbi hab lī min ladunka dhurriyyatan ṭayyibah, innaka samīʿu'd-duʿāʾ · Fa-nādathu'l-malāʾikatu wa huwa qāʾimun yuṣallī fi'l-miḥrābi anna'llāha yubashshiruka bi-Yaḥyā muṣaddiqan bi-kalimatin mina'llāhi wa sayyidan wa ḥaṣūran wa nabiyyan mina'ṣ-ṣāliḥīn · Qāla rabbi annā yakūnu lī ghulāmun wa qad balaghaniya'l-kibaru wa'mraʾatī ʿāqir, qāla kadhālika'llāhu yafʿalu mā yashāʾ · Qāla rabbi'jʿal lī āyah, qāla āyatuka allā tukallima'n-nāsa thalāthata ayyāmin illā ramzā, wa'dhkur rabbaka kathīran wa sabbiḥ bi'l-ʿashiyyi wa'l-ibkār*

"**There and then Zechariah prayed to his Lord:** '**My Lord! Grant me from Your presence a goodly offspring; truly You hear prayer.**' · **The angels called to him while he stood praying in the sanctuary:** '**God gives you good news of John** — confirming a word from God, **a lord**, **one who holds himself back**, and a prophet from among the righteous.' · He said: '**My Lord! How shall I have a boy, when old age has overtaken me and my wife is barren?**' He said: '**Even so; God does what He wills.**' · '**My Lord! Give me a sign.**' He said: '**Your sign is that for three days you shall not speak to people except by gesture.** Remember your Lord much, and glorify Him in the evening and in the early morning.'"

---

**This account was treated in detail at 19/1-15 in `019-meryem.md`.** There *nidāʾan khafiyyā*, *wahana'l-ʿaẓmu minnī*, *ishtaʿala'r-raʾsu shaybā*, *ʿitiyyā*, and the list of what was given to John were analysed. **I rely on that treatment and do not repeat it here.**

**The work I shall do here is to show the difference between the two tellings. And the difference can be verified from the text.**

### The two tellings — a comparison

| | **Maryam 19/1-15** | **Āl ʿImrān 3/38-41** |
|---|---|---|
| **Where the prayer stands** | The **opening** of the sūra — the account begins here | **After a scene** — *hunālika* ("there and then") |
| **The voice of the prayer** | *Nidāʾan **khafiyyā*** — with a hidden voice | **No** note about the voice |
| **The ground of the prayer** | *Wahana'l-ʿaẓmu… wa'shtaʿala'r-raʾs* — **a description of the body** | **No** ground — the request directly |
| **What is asked** | *Waliyyan… yarithunī* — **an heir** | *Dhurriyyatan **ṭayyibatan*** — **a goodly offspring** |
| **Who gives the good news** | *Yā Zakariyyā innā nubashshiruka* — **directly** | *Fa-nādathu'l-**malāʾika*** — **the angels** |
| **His state at the moment of the news** | Not stated | *Wa huwa **qāʾimun yuṣallī** fi'l-miḥrāb* |
| **The wording of the objection** | *Annā yakūnu lī ghulāmun wa kānati'mraʾatī **ʿāqiran** wa qad balaghtu mina'l-kibari **ʿitiyyā*** | *Annā yakūnu lī ghulāmun wa qad **balaghaniya'l-kibaru** wa'mraʾatī **ʿāqir*** |
| **The wording of the answer** | *Kadhālika qāla rabbuka **huwa ʿalayya hayyin*** + *wa qad khalaqtuka min qablu wa lam taku shayʾā* | *Kadhālika'llāhu **yafʿalu mā yashāʾ*** |
| **The length of the sign** | *Thalātha **layālin** sawiyyā* — **three nights** | *Thalāthata **ayyāmin** illā ramzā* — **three days** |
| **John's attributes** | *al-ḥukm, ḥanān, zakāt, taqiyy, barran bi-wālidayh, lam yakun jabbāran ʿaṣiyyā* | *Muṣaddiqan bi-kalimatin mina'llāh, sayyidan, ḥaṣūran, nabiyyan mina'ṣ-ṣāliḥīn* |

**Every row of this table can be verified from the two texts. I now record three observations that come out of the table.**

**One. Sūrat Maryam tells the *person*; Āl ʿImrān tells the *context*.** In Maryam there are Zechariah's bones, his hair, the failing of his voice — the state of a human being is described. **In Āl ʿImrān there is none of this; in its place stands *hunālika* — "there and then".** That is, the prayer is given as the consequence of the scene seen in the preceding verse (the finding of unlooked-for provision beside Mary).

**I record this as my own reading, and it is not binding:** in Āl ʿImrān the prayer is an **inference**. Zechariah has seen a provision that came without a cause, and has asked for his own impossibility. *Hunālika* is the one word that establishes this link, and it is not in Sūrat Maryam.

**Two. In the two sūras the number *three* is the same and the unit different: *thalātha layāl* (Maryam) / *thalāthata ayyām* (Āl ʿImrān).** **This is a verifiable difference of wording.** In the classical commentaries this is explained as two namings of the same span — in Arabic it is common for day and night to be used so as to include one another. **I transmit this and build no ruling on it.**

**Three. The lists of attributes differ, and neither of them is deficient.** What is given in Maryam bears on **the inner world** (*ḥanān*, *zakāt*, *taqiyy*); what is given in Āl ʿImrān bears on **position** (*muṣaddiq*, *sayyid*, *nabī*). **I record this as an observation:** the two sūras, telling of the same person, bring different attributes forward, and each sūra takes what suits its own axis. The axis of Sūrat Maryam is *al-Raḥmān*; the axis of Āl ʿImrān is **being chosen, and position.**

### سَيِّدًا وَحَصُورًا

**سَيِّد** — the root **س-و-د**: **to go in front, to be at the head.** *Sayyid* — the one who stands before his people. **The word occurs rarely in the Qurʾān**, and here it is used as the attribute of a prophet.

**حَصُور** — the root **ح-ص-ر**: **to encircle, to press in, to hold back.** *Ḥaṣr* — narrowing, restriction. ***Ḥaṣūr* is on the *faʿūl* pattern and conveys intensity.**

**The senses given to the word in the classical commentaries:**

| # | What *ḥaṣūr* is | Explanation |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | **One who holds himself back** | Master of his appetite, reining in his desire |
| 2 | **One who does not marry** | Keeping away from women |
| 3 | **One held back from sin** | One whose turning to evil is prevented |

**All three are transmitted. I do not impose a preference.**

**And per the method (`USLUP.md`) I enter a note:** from the second reading it cannot be concluded that **not marrying is a general excellence**; elsewhere the Qurʾān says plainly that prophets had wives and children (al-Raʿd 13/38 — **treated in `013-rad.md`**). **No legal or moral ruling is established on this matter in this commentary.**

### كَذَٰلِكَ ٱللَّهُ يَفْعَلُ مَا يَشَآءُ

**The form of the answer must be recorded, and it differs from the one in Sūrat Maryam.**

| Sūra | The answer | What it rests on |
|---|---|---|
| **Maryam 19/9** | *Huwa ʿalayya hayyin wa qad **khalaqtuka min qablu** wa lam taku shayʾā* | **A past instance** — the addressee's own creation |
| **Āl ʿImrān 3/40** | *Kadhālika'llāhu **yafʿalu mā yashāʾ*** | **The nature of the agent** — no instance at all is given |

**I record this as an observation:** in Maryam the objection is met with a **proof**; in Āl ʿImrān with a **declaration.** And the context of Āl ʿImrān explains this: in this sūra something like the same sentence will be said to Mary as well, at verse forty-seven (*kadhāliki'llāhu yakhluqu mā yashāʾ*) — **that is, the sentence is here turned into a pattern, and it binds the two annunciations together.**

| Verse | To whom | Sentence |
|---|---|---|
| **40** | Zechariah | *Kadhālika'llāhu **yafʿalu** mā yashāʾ* |
| **47** | Mary | *Kadhāliki'llāhu **yakhluqu** mā yashāʾ* |

**Two sentences on the same pattern; the one difference is in the verb: *yafʿal* (He does) / *yakhluq* (He creates).** **This is a verifiable difference of wording.**

**I record this as my own reading, and it is not binding:** what Zechariah asks for is an **exception** to an order that is working — a child from an old man. Mary's case, on the other hand, is outside the order. **And the verbs appear to have been chosen accordingly:** one is *doing*, the other *creating*. **I give this as an observation; I establish no fixed rule.**

### إِلَّا رَمْزًا

The root **ر-م-ز**: **to signal with the lip, the eye or the hand — a communication made without the voice.** **The word occurs only here in the Qurʾān.**

**And the observation recorded in `019-meryem.md` can be carried over here:** there it was said that the prayer was **hidden** (*nidāʾan khafiyyā*), and what was given as a sign also had to do with **the voice**.

**In Āl ʿImrān the prayer is not silent; but the sign is again silence.** **I record this as an observation:** in both sūras the same sign is given — not being able to speak. And in both sūras the same command follows it: *sabbiḥ* — glorify.

**That is, speech with people is cut off, and the remembrance of God goes on.** This is a thing shared that can be verified from the two texts.

---

## 3/42-44 — وَإِذْ قَالَتِ ٱلْمَلَٰٓئِكَةُ يَٰمَرْيَمُ إِنَّ ٱللَّهَ ٱصْطَفَىٰكِ وَطَهَّرَكِ وَٱصْطَفَىٰكِ عَلَىٰ نِسَآءِ ٱلْعَٰلَمِينَ

*Wa idh qālati'l-malāʾikatu yā Maryamu inna'llāha'ṣṭafāki wa ṭahharaki wa'ṣṭafāki ʿalā nisāʾi'l-ʿālamīn · Yā Maryamu'qnutī li-rabbiki wa'sjudī wa'rkaʿī maʿa'r-rākiʿīn · Dhālika min anbāʾi'l-ghaybi nūḥīhi ilayk, wa mā kunta ladayhim idh yulqūna aqlāmahum ayyuhum yakfulu Maryama wa mā kunta ladayhim idh yakhtaṣimūn*

"When the angels said: '**Mary! God has chosen you and purified you, and chosen you above the women of all beings.** · **Mary! Be devoutly obedient to your Lord, prostrate yourself, and bow with those who bow.**' · **These are among the tidings of the unseen that We reveal to you.** You were not with them when they **cast their pens** to see which of them should have charge of Mary; **nor were you with them when they contended.**"

### Twice ٱصْطَفَىٰ

**The verb occurs twice in the same verse, and the classical commentaries have dwelt on this repetition:**

| Order | Sentence | Qualification |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | *Inna'llāha'**ṣṭafāki*** | **Unqualified** — absolute |
| 2 | *Wa'**ṣṭafāki** ʿalā nisāʾi'l-ʿālamīn* | **Qualified** — "above the women of all beings" |
| Between them | *Wa **ṭahharaki*** | **Purification** |

**The classical explanations:**

| # | The difference between the two choosings |
|---|---|
| 1 | The first is **acceptance**, the second **being set above** |
| 2 | The first is **at the birth** (the acceptance of the vow), the second **at the annunciation** |
| 3 | The first is **general** (being chosen for a religious life), the second **particular** (becoming the mother of Jesus) |

**All three are transmitted. I do not impose a preference.**

**And the verb in the middle must be recorded: *ṭahharaki* — "purified you".** The root **ط-ه-ر**: **material and spiritual purity.** **The root was treated at 2/222 in `002-bakara.md` and at 74/4 in `074-muddessir.md`.**

**The word order is worth recording: purification stands *between* the two choosings.** **I record this as an observation about the word order:** the sentence proceeds choosing–purifying–choosing; that is, the second choosing is not a repetition of the first, for an operation stands between them.

### ٱقْنُتِى وَٱسْجُدِى وَٱرْكَعِى

**Three commands, and their order must be recorded, because it is the reverse of the customary order of the prayer:** bowing comes **before** prostration; here prostration is named first.

**Two explanations of this are transmitted in the classical commentaries:**

| # | Explanation |
|---|---|
| 1 | The particle *wāw* **does not convey order** (*muṭlaq al-jamʿ*); no temporal order is to be sought among the acts listed |
| 2 | The order goes by **the weight of the acts**; the most intense has been brought forward |

**Both are transmitted. I do not impose a preference and I draw no legal conclusion from this.**

**ٱقْنُتِى** — the root **ق-ن-ت**: **a continuous and silent submission, a standing in one state.** **The root was treated at 33/31 in `033-ahzab.md` and at 66/12 in `066-tahrim.md`** — and in al-Taḥrīm about this same person: *wa kānat mina'l-qānitīn.*

**And the note *maʿa'r-rākiʿīn* is worth recording:** "**together with** those who bow".

**I record this as an observation:** the first two commands are singular and solitary; to the third a note of **being together** is added. **And *al-rākiʿīn* is a masculine plural.** In the classical commentaries this is explained in the sense of "within the congregation". **I transmit this and build no ruling on it.**

### يُلْقُونَ أَقْلَٰمَهُمْ — and the silence of the text

**The forty-fourth verse names a scene but does not narrate it: *idh yulqūna aqlāmahum ayyuhum yakfulu Maryam* — "when they cast their pens to see which of them should have charge of Mary."**

**The Qurʾān says nothing else about this scene:** how many they were, how the pen was cast, who won — none of it. **And in the classical commentaries various reports are passed on that fill this gap. Per the method (`USLUP.md`) those reports are not passed on in this commentary.**

**The verse's own sentence already says why the scene is named: *dhālika min anbāʾi'l-ghaybi nūḥīhi ilayk* — "these are among the tidings of the unseen that We reveal to you."**

**And the note repeated twice: *wa mā kunta ladayhim* — "you were not with them."**

**I record this as an observation about the word order:** the sentence is repeated twice, for two separate scenes (the casting of pens, the contending). **And the work of the repetition is to show the source of the knowledge.** The detail of the scene is not given; what is given is **where that detail does not come from.**

**يَخْتَصِمُونَ** — the root **خ-ص-م**: **to contend, to dispute at law.** **The root was treated at 38/64 in `038-sad.md` (*takhāṣumu ahli'n-nār*) and at 36/49 in `036-yasin.md`.**

**And there is a point that must be recorded:** the verse describes **contending over a good thing** — over who is to look after the child. **I give this as an observation:** the sūra always uses the words *ikhtilāf* and *tafarruq* negatively (19, 103, 105); the word used here is a different one (*ikhtiṣām*), and its subject is the sharing out of a responsibility.

---

## 3/45-47 — إِذْ قَالَتِ ٱلْمَلَٰٓئِكَةُ يَٰمَرْيَمُ إِنَّ ٱللَّهَ يُبَشِّرُكِ بِكَلِمَةٍ مِّنْهُ ٱسْمُهُ ٱلْمَسِيحُ عِيسَى ٱبْنُ مَرْيَمَ وَجِيهًا فِى ٱلدُّنْيَا وَٱلْءَاخِرَةِ وَمِنَ ٱلْمُقَرَّبِينَ

*Idh qālati'l-malāʾikatu yā Maryamu inna'llāha yubashshiruki bi-kalimatin minhu'smuhu'l-Masīḥu ʿĪsa'bnu Maryama wajīhan fi'd-dunyā wa'l-ākhirati wa mina'l-muqarrabīn · Wa yukallimu'n-nāsa fi'l-mahdi wa kahlan wa mina'ṣ-ṣāliḥīn · Qālat rabbi annā yakūnu lī waladun wa lam yamsasnī bashar, qāla kadhāliki'llāhu yakhluqu mā yashāʾ, idhā qaḍā amran fa-innamā yaqūlu lahū kun fa-yakūn*

"When the angels said: '**Mary! God gives you good news of a word from Him;** his name is **the Messiah, Jesus son of Mary**; **he is honoured in this world and in the hereafter** and **is of those brought near.** · **He will speak to people in the cradle and in his maturity**, and he is of the righteous.' · She said: '**My Lord! How shall I have a child, when no man has touched me?**' He said: '**Even so; God creates what He wills.** When He decrees a thing He only says to it '**Be**', and it is.'"

---

**This scene was treated at 19/16-21 in `019-meryem.md`.** There *intabadhat*, *ḥijāb*, *aʿūdhu bi'r-Raḥmān*, *in kunta taqiyyā* and the form of the annunciation were analysed. **I rely on that treatment and do not repeat it here. Here I show the difference between the two tellings.**

### The two tellings — a comparison

| | **Maryam 19/16-21** | **Āl ʿImrān 3/42-47** |
|---|---|---|
| **The scene** | *Intabadhat… fa'ttakhadhat min dūnihim **ḥijābā*** — a withdrawing and a screen | **No scene** — no place is named |
| **The one who comes** | *Rūḥanā* — **one**, *fa-tamaththala lahā basharan sawiyyā* | *Al-**malāʾika*** — **plural**, no description of form |
| **Mary's first reaction** | *Innī **aʿūdhu** bi'r-Raḥmāni mink* — **taking refuge** | No reaction; she first hears **praise** (42) |
| **The wording of the news** | *Li-ahaba laki **ghulāman zakiyyā*** | *Bi-**kalimatin** minhu'smuhu'l-Masīḥu ʿĪsa'bnu Maryam* |
| **Is a name given** | **No** | **Yes — three names at once** |
| **Her objection** | *Annā yakūnu lī ghulāmun wa **lam yamsasnī basharun wa lam aku baghiyyā*** | *Annā yakūnu lī **waladun** wa **lam yamsasnī bashar*** |
| **The answer** | *Kadhāliki qāla rabbuki **huwa ʿalayya hayyin*** | *Kadhāliki'llāhu **yakhluqu mā yashāʾ*** + *kun fa-yakūn* |
| **The birth scene** | **Present** — the palm tree, the pangs, *yā laytanī mittu* (23-26) | **Absent** |
| **The return to her people** | **Present** — *fa-atat bihī qawmahā taḥmiluh* (27-33) | **Absent** |
| **Its axis** | **What Mary went through** | **The position of Jesus** |

**The last row of the table explains all the differences, and I record this as my own reading:** Sūrat Maryam tells **what happened to a human being** — fear, taking refuge, the pangs, facing an accusation. **Āl ʿImrān, on the other hand, is speaking inside a *dispute***, and for that reason it counts up **who** Jesus is and **what he is not**: a word, Messiah, son of Mary, honoured, brought near, righteous, speaking, a prophet. **Eight attributes, and not one of them is "son of God".**

**In Sūrat Maryam there is a birth; in Āl ʿImrān there is no birth.** This is a fact verifiable from the two texts, and it separates the work of the two sūras.

### بِكَلِمَةٍ مِّنْهُ — "a word from Him"

**The construction must be recorded, and it is one of the places the classical commentaries have dwelt on most.**

The root **ك-ل-م**: **to wound; and to utter speech.** The lexicographers establish the link thus: an utterance **leaves a mark** in the hearer. *Kalima* — a sound that leaves a mark.

**And what must be recorded is the preposition: *kalimatin minhu* — "a word from Him". *Min* conveys source.**

**The readings transmitted in the classical commentaries about what the *kalima* is:**

| # | What the *kalima* is | Its ground |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | **The command "Be"** — Jesus is called a "word" because he came to be by that command | The end of the same verse: *kun fa-yakūn* |
| 2 | **The good news itself** — the utterance announced | *Yubashshiruki bi-kalimah* — it comes with the verb of announcing |
| 3 | **A naming** — a title of Jesus | The same construction occurs at al-Nisāʾ 4/171 |

**All three are transmitted. I do not impose a preference.**

**And per the method (`USLUP.md`) I record this plainly:** the construction is bounded by the verse's own sentence. **The verse does not debate what the *kalima* is; it gives it as a *subject of good news* and immediately afterwards names it with three names.** No theological debate is entered in this commentary.

**And a point of word order must be recorded, and the classical commentaries point to it:** *bi-kalimatin* is **feminine**, but the pronoun comes **masculine**: ***ismuhū*** — "**his** name" (not *ismuhā*).

**I record this as an observation about the word order:** the sentence passes from the *kalima* to **a person**, and it makes the passage by a pronoun. That is, what is announced is not an utterance but a human being.

### ٱلْمَسِيحُ عِيسَى ٱبْنُ مَرْيَمَ — three names at once

**Three namings stand side by side, and all three will be at work in the sūra:**

| Name | What it conveys |
|---|---|
| **al-Masīḥ** | A **title** |
| **ʿĪsā** | A **personal name** |
| **Ibnu Maryam** | **Descent** — through the mother |

**ٱلْمَسِيح** — the root **م-س-ح**: **to stroke with the hand, to wipe.** More than one explanation is transmitted in the classical sources for why the word carries this name (*because he was anointed*, *because he travelled about*, *because he healed the sick by touching them*). **There is no certain knowledge, and I impose no preference.**

**And the note *ibnu Maryam* must be dwelt on:** the Qurʾān names Jesus for the most part with this construction. **This is a verifiable fact of usage.**

**I record this as my own reading, and it is not binding:** the giving of descent through the mother agrees with the later sentence of the verse (*lam yamsasnī bashar*) — that is, the naming contains the event itself. **And the same naming is also the ground of the proof that will be built at verse 59 of the sūra.**

### وَجِيهًا — and the word order of the list of names

The root **و-ج-ه**. *Wajīh* — **one who has a face**, held in esteem, whose word counts. **The same root occurred in the twentieth verse in the construction *aslamtu wajhī*.**

**And the word order of the list must be recorded:**

| Attribute | Its field |
|---|---|
| *Wajīhan fi'd-dunyā wa'l-ākhira* | **In both worlds at once** |
| *Wa mina'l-muqarrabīn* | **Nearness** |
| *Wa yukallimu'n-nāsa fi'l-mahdi wa kahlā* | **Speaking** |
| *Wa mina'ṣ-ṣāliḥīn* | **Righteousness** |

**فِى ٱلْمَهْدِ وَكَهْلًا** — "**in the cradle and in maturity**".

The root **ك-ه-ل**: **one who has come to middle age, who has ripened.** **The word occurs in the Qurʾān only in this construction (here and at 5/110).**

**And the construction must be recorded, and the classical commentaries have dwelt on it:** two states are set side by side — one the age at which speaking is **extraordinary**, the other the age at which it is **ordinary**.

**I record this as an observation:** the sentence does not content itself with either of the two ends. Had only the cradle been named, it would have been a note of a marvel; had only maturity been named, nothing would have been said. **What is said when the two are named together is *continuance*:** the speaking is not a one-time sign.

### كُن فَيَكُونُ

**It was treated at 2/117 in `002-bakara.md`, and the note there was this:**

> *"**كُن فَيَكُونُ** — 'He says: be; and it is'. The expression pictures creation not as a **process** but as an **utterance**. Material, tool, labour, time — none of them is named."*

**I rely on that treatment and do not repeat it here.**

**Here I add what belongs to Āl ʿImrān alone, and it is a link verifiable within the sūra:** the expression occurs **twice** in the sūra — at 47 and at **59.** And at its second occurrence it will be named together with the creation of Adam.

| Verse | Context | Its work |
|---|---|---|
| **47** | An **answer** to Mary's objection | The meeting of an impossibility |
| **59** | The **comparison** of Adam and Jesus | Part of a **proof** |

**The same expression, one an answer and one a proof.** This will be taken up separately at verse 59.

---

## 3/48-51 — وَيُعَلِّمُهُ ٱلْكِتَٰبَ وَٱلْحِكْمَةَ وَٱلتَّوْرَىٰةَ وَٱلْإِنجِيلَ · وَرَسُولًا إِلَىٰ بَنِىٓ إِسْرَٰٓءِيلَ

*Wa yuʿallimuhu'l-kitāba wa'l-ḥikmata wa't-Tawrāta wa'l-Injīl · Wa rasūlan ilā banī Isrāʾīla annī qad jiʾtukum bi-āyatin min rabbikum annī akhluqu lakum mina'ṭ-ṭīni ka-hayʾati'ṭ-ṭayri fa-anfukhu fīhi fa-yakūnu ṭayran bi-idhni'llāh, wa ubriʾu'l-akmaha wa'l-abraṣa wa uḥyi'l-mawtā bi-idhni'llāh, wa unabbiʾukum bimā taʾkulūna wa mā taddakhirūna fī buyūtikum, inna fī dhālika la-āyatan lakum in kuntum muʾminīn · Wa muṣaddiqan limā bayna yadayya mina't-Tawrāti wa li-uḥilla lakum baʿḍa'lladhī ḥurrima ʿalaykum, wa jiʾtukum bi-āyatin min rabbikum fa'ttaqu'llāha wa aṭīʿūn · Inna'llāha rabbī wa rabbukum fa'ʿbudūh, hādhā ṣirāṭun mustaqīm*

"**He will teach him the Book, wisdom, the Torah and the Gospel** · and (send him) **as a messenger to the Children of Israel**: '**I have come to you with a sign from your Lord.** For you I shape out of clay something in the form of a bird, and I breathe into it; **by God's leave** it becomes a bird. **By God's leave** I heal the one born blind and the leper, and I raise the dead. **I also tell you what you eat and what you store up in your houses.** If you are believers there is in this a sign for you.' · '**Confirming the Torah that was before me**, and to make lawful for you some of what was forbidden you…' · '**Truly God is my Lord and your Lord; so serve Him. This is a straight path.**'"

### بِإِذْنِ ٱللَّهِ — a note repeated twice

**This is the most important fact of word order in the block, and it is verifiable: the expression occurs twice in the forty-ninth verse in the form *bi-idhni'llāh*, and the third verb is tied to the same note.**

| Verb | The note |
|---|---|
| *Fa-yakūnu ṭayrā* | **بِإِذْنِ ٱللَّهِ** |
| *Wa ubriʾu'l-akmaha wa'l-abraṣ* | (tied to the same note) |
| *Wa uḥyi'l-mawtā* | **بِإِذْنِ ٱللَّهِ** |

**And the subjects of the verbs must be recorded: all of them are first person singular** — *akhluqu*, *anfukhu*, *ubriʾu*, *uḥyī*, *unabbiʾu*. **"I shape, I breathe, I heal, I raise."**

**And at the end of every sequence of verbs the same note comes.**

**I record this as my own reading, and it is not binding:** the word order of the sentence says two things **at once**. The acts are not denied — they really are done. But the **authority** of the acts is each time tied elsewhere. **And this is the whole question of the sūra in this block:** what Jesus did is not denied; **the conclusion drawn from what he did** is limited.

**And the last sentence of the block does this plainly: *inna'llāha rabbī wa rabbukum* — "God is my Lord and your Lord."** **The sentence puts the speaker on the same side as those he addresses.**

**This sentence occurs several times in the Qurʾān, always in the mouth of Jesus** (5/72, 5/117, 19/36, 43/64). **It was treated at 19/36 in `019-meryem.md`.** I rely on that treatment.

**And it can be compared with the sentence at 29/46 in `029-ankebut.md`:** *wa ilāhunā wa ilāhukum wāḥid.* **The two sentences do the same work, and this will be taken up separately at verse 64 of the sūra.**

### ٱلْأَكْمَهَ وَٱلْأَبْرَصَ

**أَكْمَه** — from the root **ك-م-ه**: **blind from birth.** **And أَبْرَص** — from the root **ب-ر-ص**: **stricken with the white skin disease.**

**Both words occur in the Qurʾān only in these two places (here and at 5/110).**

**I record this as an observation and I force no connection:** the two illnesses have in common that both are **visible** and **lasting**. One is from birth, the other carries its mark on the skin. **That is, both are conditions that cannot be hidden and do not change by ordinary means.**

### وَلِأُحِلَّ لَكُم بَعْضَ ٱلَّذِى حُرِّمَ عَلَيْكُمْ

**The verse's caveat matters: *baʿḍa'lladhī* — "**some of it**".** Not all.

**Per the method (`USLUP.md`) I record this:** this sentence is connected with a ruling named elsewhere in the Qurʾān (al-Nisāʾ 4/160, al-Anʿām 6/146). **No legal ruling is built on that connection in this commentary;** what is to be recorded is that the sentence speaks of a **partial** change.

**And the closing sentence of the block is worth recording: *hādhā ṣirāṭun mustaqīm.*** **The construction was treated in detail in `001-fatiha.md`.** I do not repeat it here. **And it will occur once more in the sūra, at verse 101:** *wa man yaʿtaṣim bi'llāhi fa-qad hudiya ilā ṣirāṭin mustaqīm.* **Two occurrences, one inside an account and one in an address to the community.**

---

## 3/52-54 — فَلَمَّآ أَحَسَّ عِيسَىٰ مِنْهُمُ ٱلْكُفْرَ قَالَ مَنْ أَنصَارِىٓ إِلَى ٱللَّهِ قَالَ ٱلْحَوَارِيُّونَ نَحْنُ أَنصَارُ ٱللَّهِ

*Fa-lammā aḥassa ʿĪsā minhumu'l-kufra qāla man anṣārī ila'llāh, qāla'l-ḥawāriyyūna naḥnu anṣāru'llāhi āmannā bi'llāhi wa'shhad bi-annā muslimūn · Rabbanā āmannā bimā anzalta wa'ttabaʿna'r-rasūla fa'ktubnā maʿa'sh-shāhidīn · Wa makarū wa makara'llāh, wa'llāhu khayru'l-mākirīn*

"**When Jesus sensed disbelief in them** he said: '**Who are my helpers on the way to God?**' **The disciples** said: '**We are God's helpers; we have believed in God. Bear witness that we are those who have handed themselves over.**' · '**Our Lord! We have believed in what You sent down and we have followed the messenger; write us down among those who bear witness.**' · **They laid a plot; and God laid His plan. God is the best of planners.**"

### أَحَسَّ — to sense

The root **ح-س-س**: **to perceive by the senses.** Form IV: *aḥassa* — to sense, to feel.

**The choice of the word is worth recording:** the verse does not say "he saw" or "he knew". ***Aḥassa* — he sensed.** **That is, the disbelief has not yet been spoken openly.**

**I record this as an observation:** the sentence shows that the response is given **before open hostility**. And the response given is not a defence but **a call**: *man anṣārī ila'llāh.*

**ٱلْحَوَارِيُّون** — the root **ح-و-ر**: the lexicographers connect the word with the senses of **whiteness** and of **turning to something**; on the origin of *ḥawārī* **there is no firm agreement.** I impose no preference in this commentary.

**And the last sentence of the answer must be recorded: *wa'shhad bi-annā muslimūn* — "bear witness that we are those who have handed themselves over".**

**This is directly connected with verse 19 of the sūra and confirms what I recorded there:** the word *islām* is here the self-naming of the community around Jesus. **This is a fact of usage verifiable from the text.**

### وَمَكَرُوا۟ وَمَكَرَ ٱللَّهُ

The root **م-ك-ر**: **to lay a scheme in secret.** **The root was treated at 27/50-51 in `027-neml.md` and at 13/42 in `013-rad.md`.**

**Per the method (`USLUP.md`) I enter a note, and it has been kept consistently in the series:** when the word is used of God it does not mean **deceiving or wronging**. **In Arabic this is called *mushākala* (answering with the same word):** the response is named with the wording of the first act.

**And the sentence itself shows this: *wa'llāhu khayru'l-mākirīn* — "the best of planners".** **The attribute "best" records within the sentence that the verb is not meant in a bad sense.**

**This distinction was treated at 8/30 in `008-enfal.md` (*wa yamkurūna wa yamkuru'llāh*).** I rely on that treatment and do not repeat it here.

---

## 3/55-58 — إِذْ قَالَ ٱللَّهُ يَٰعِيسَىٰٓ إِنِّى مُتَوَفِّيكَ وَرَافِعُكَ إِلَىَّ وَمُطَهِّرُكَ مِنَ ٱلَّذِينَ كَفَرُوا۟

*Idh qāla'llāhu yā ʿĪsā innī mutawaffīka wa rāfiʿuka ilayya wa muṭahhiruka mina'lladhīna kafarū wa jāʿilu'lladhīna'ttabaʿūka fawqa'lladhīna kafarū ilā yawmi'l-qiyāmah, thumma ilayya marjiʿukum fa-aḥkumu baynakum fīmā kuntum fīhi takhtalifūn*

"When God said: '**Jesus! I shall take you fully, and raise you to Myself, and purify you of those who disbelieve, and set those who follow you above those who disbelieve until the day of resurrection.** Then your return is to Me; **and I shall judge between you in what you differed over.**'"

### مُتَوَفِّيكَ — a disputed word

The root **و-ف-ي**: **to be complete, to be without deficiency.** *Wafāʾ* — completing; *īfāʾ* — giving a right in full. **Form V: *tawaffā* — to take a thing in full.**

**At the centre of the root there is no death; there is *taking in full*.** Its use for death comes from the sense of **the soul's being taken complete**. **And the Qurʾān uses the same verb of sleep as well** (al-Zumar 39/42, al-Anʿām 6/60) — **treated at 39/42 in `039-zumer.md`.** I rely on that treatment.

**The sense of this word here is disputed in the classical commentaries, and the disagreement has continued. I give it in a table and impose no preference:**

| # | What *mutawaffīka* means | Explanation |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | **I shall cause you to die** | The common use of the word; the *rafʿ* comes after it |
| 2 | **I shall take you in full** | The sense of death is not required; the root is "taking complete" |
| 3 | **I shall take you in a state resembling sleep** | Rests on the Qurʾān's use of the same verb for sleep |
| 4 | **The order is by anticipation and postponement** | "I shall raise you and (then) cause you to die" — the *wāw* does not convey order |

**All four readings are transmitted in the classical sources. I do not impose a preference.**

**And per the method (`USLUP.md`) I enter a plain note:** a wide theological and narrative literature has formed around this verse. **That debate is not entered in this commentary.** What is to be recorded is **that the wording of the verse lists four acts**: *tawaffī*, *rafʿ*, *taṭhīr*, *jaʿl*. **And all four are ascribed to God; the subject of none of them is Jesus.**

### فَوْقَ ٱلَّذِينَ كَفَرُوا۟ إِلَىٰ يَوْمِ ٱلْقِيَٰمَةِ

**Per the method (`USLUP.md`) I record this:** both sides in the sentence are named by an **attribute** — *alladhīna'ttabaʿūk* (those who followed you) and *alladhīna kafarū* (those who disbelieved). **No people, nation or religious institution is named.**

**And what the word *fawqa* (above) conveys is disputed in the classical commentaries:**

| Reading | What *fawqa* is |
|---|---|
| 1 | Superiority **in proof** |
| 2 | In respect of **number and spread** |
| 3 | In respect of **position** — the superiority of the argument |

**I transmit what is transmitted; I impose no preference. And no present-day political or social conclusion is drawn from this.**

**Verses fifty-six and fifty-seven compare two outcomes; verse fifty-eight closes the block:** *dhālika natlūhu ʿalayka mina'l-āyāti wa'dh-dhikri'l-ḥakīm* — "**this We recite to you of the signs and of the wise reminder.**"

**I record this as an observation about the word order:** before the account ends, the text turns once more to **the narrator** — just as in verse 44 (*dhālika min anbāʾi'l-ghayb*). **The sūra sets a note of the same kind at both ends of the account.**

---

## 3/59-60 — إِنَّ مَثَلَ عِيسَىٰ عِندَ ٱللَّهِ كَمَثَلِ ءَادَمَ خَلَقَهُۥ مِن تُرَابٍ ثُمَّ قَالَ لَهُۥ كُن فَيَكُونُ

*Inna mathala ʿĪsā ʿinda'llāhi ka-mathali Ādam, khalaqahū min turābin thumma qāla lahū kun fa-yakūn · Al-ḥaqqu min rabbika fa-lā takūna mina'l-mumtarīn*

"**With God the case of Jesus is like the case of Adam:** He created him from dust, then said to him '**Be**'; and he was. · **The truth is from your Lord; so do not be of those who doubt.**"

---

**This verse is a *proof*, and proofs are read by being taken apart. I shall set out its structure step by step.**

### The structure of the proof

**The objection is this** (it is not stated in the verse, but this is what the proof answers): **a human being born without a father is not an ordinary human being; therefore he is something else.**

**The answer of the proof lies in three steps:**

| Step | Sentence | What it does |
|---|---|---|
| **1** | *Ka-mathali **Ādam*** | **It brings an instance** — an instance over which there is no dispute |
| **2** | *Khalaqahū **min turāb*** | It shows that the instance is **further out** |
| **3** | *Thumma qāla lahū **kun fa-yakūn*** | It names the **shared cause** of the two cases |

**And the force of the proof lies in the second step. This needs opening out.**

**In the birth of Jesus there is a mother; there is no father.** **In the creation of Adam there is neither mother nor father.** **That is, the instance brought is *more* extraordinary than the case under dispute.**

**The logic of the proof is this, and it can be seen in a table:**

| | **Adam** | **Jesus** | **An ordinary human being** |
|---|---|---|---|
| Father | **None** | **None** | Present |
| Mother | **None** | **Present** | Present |
| How many causes lacking | **Two** | **One** | **None** |

**And the conclusion is unavoidable:** if the absence of one cause puts a person outside humanity, **the absence of two would do so all the more.** But no such claim is made about Adam by any side. **Therefore the measure is not the number of causes.**

**In classical logic this is called *qiyās bi'l-awlā*, an argument a fortiori: a ruling is voided in the weaker instance by being shown not to hold in the stronger one.**

**I record this way of taking the proof apart as my own reading; but the structure itself comes out of the wording of the verse, and it has been explained in this direction in the classical commentaries as well.**

### مَثَل — the work of the word

The root **م-ث-ل**: **to resemble, to be equal.** *Mathal* — a likeness, an instance; and the **case, the condition** of a thing.

**And the work of the word in the sentence must be recorded:** the verse does not say "Jesus is like Adam". ***Mathala ʿĪsā… ka-mathali Ādam*** — "the **case** of Jesus is like the **case** of Adam."

**I record this as an observation about the word order:** what is likened is not the persons but **the manner of their coming to be.** This also settles the extent of the proof: the verse is not comparing the attributes of two prophets.

**And the caveat must be recorded: *ʿinda'llāh* — "with God".** **That is, the measure is not people's view.**

### كُن فَيَكُونُ — the second occurrence

**It was treated at 2/117 in `002-bakara.md`; I do not repeat it here.**

**Here I add the fact within the sūra:** the expression occurs twice in the sūra, and **the two occurrences are bound to each other.**

| Verse | To whom it is said | Its work |
|---|---|---|
| **47** | **To Mary** | An **answer** to an objection — to the question "how can this be?" |
| **59** | **To the one addressed** | The last step of a **proof** |

**I record this as my own reading, and it is not binding:** in the forty-seventh verse this expression conveyed a **possibility**; in the fifty-ninth it is used as a **leveller**. The same utterance ties two human beings to the same cause and renders the difference between them — one from dust, the other from a mother — ineffective as regards the outcome.

**And a point of word order must be recorded:** the sentence begins with *khalaqahū min turābin* (perfect) and ends with *fa-yakūn* (imperfect). **The standing of perfect and imperfect side by side in the same sentence is striking in Arabic.**

**The explanation in the classical commentaries: the imperfect brings the event before the eyes (*ḥikāyat al-ḥāl*).** **I transmit this and add an observation:** the sentence narrates a past event and then passes in its last verb to the present — **that is, what is narrated is left not as a finished event but as a rule in operation.**

### ٱلْمُمْتَرِين

The root **م-ر-ي**: **to doubt; and to dispute.** Form VIII: *imtirāʾ* — to fall into doubt. **The root was treated at 19/34 in `019-meryem.md` (*yamtarūn*) and at 53/55 in `053-necm.md`.**

**And the word order of the sixtieth verse must be recorded: *al-ḥaqqu min rabbika* — "the truth is from your Lord".** The sentence states a source; a prohibition follows.

**I record this as an observation:** the sentence that comes after the proof is given does not command that the proof be **accepted**; it commands **not falling into doubt.** **And the one addressed is the side presenting the proof.** That is, the sentence is spoken not to the person who keeps up the dispute but to the one conducting it.

---

## 3/61 — فَمَنْ حَآجَّكَ فِيهِ مِنۢ بَعْدِ مَا جَآءَكَ مِنَ ٱلْعِلْمِ فَقُلْ تَعَالَوْا۟ نَدْعُ أَبْنَآءَنَا وَأَبْنَآءَكُمْ

*Fa-man ḥājjaka fīhi min baʿdi mā jāʾaka mina'l-ʿilmi fa-qul taʿālaw nadʿu abnāʾanā wa abnāʾakum wa nisāʾanā wa nisāʾakum wa anfusanā wa anfusakum thumma nabtahil fa-najʿal laʿnata'llāhi ʿala'l-kādhibīn*

"**Whoever disputes with you about this after the knowledge that has come to you**, say: '**Come, let us call our sons and your sons, our women and your women, ourselves and yourselves; then let us pray with all our hearts and call down the curse of God upon the liars.**'"

---

**Per the method (`USLUP.md`) I draw a boundary here, and I write it plainly:** a wide literature of reports and a dispute between the schools have formed around this verse. **Those reports and that dispute are not entered in this commentary; no personal names are given.** I keep within the **wording** of the verse.

### نَبْتَهِل — root: ب-ه-ل

The concrete sense of the root is worth recording, and the lexicographers dwell on it: **ب-ه-ل — to let a thing loose, to leave it unrestrained.** *Bahala'n-nāqata* — he did not keep the she-camel from her young but let her loose; he undid her halter.

**From this comes *ibtihāl*: to entreat with abandon, keeping nothing back.** **The word occurs only here in the Qurʾān.**

**I record this as an observation:** the picture of the root is an **untying** — the lifting of a restraint. And this is what is asked in the verse: that both sides turn directly to God with no reservation held back.

### The structure of the call

**Those called are counted in three pairs, and the word order must be recorded:**

| Pair | Wording |
|---|---|
| 1 | *Abnāʾanā wa **abnāʾakum*** |
| 2 | *Nisāʾanā wa **nisāʾakum*** |
| 3 | *Anfusanā wa **anfusakum*** |

**All three pairs are symmetrical: each designation is applied to both sides in the same way.** **And the order is worth recording: sons, women, selves.**

**I record this as my own reading, and it is not binding:** the order runs from what a person **guards most** towards himself. That is, the call does not mean "come, let us argue with each other"; it means **"let each side put forward what it values most."**

**And the logic of the proposal lies here:** if a man knows that he is lying, he will not call the price of that lie down upon his own child. **That is, the proposal is not a method of debate but a *measure of sincerity*.**

**And the verse does not narrate the outcome of the proposal.** **I record this as an observation:** the text does not say whether the encounter took place or not. **This silence of the text is preserved in this commentary.**

### فِيهِ — the reference of the pronoun

**There is an indeterminacy in the sentence: *ḥājjaka fīh* — "disputes with you about this". What does the pronoun refer to?**

| Reading | What the pronoun refers to |
|---|---|
| 1 | **To Jesus** — the subject of the preceding block |
| 2 | **To *al-ḥaqq*** — the "truth" of verse 60 |
| 3 | **To the whole matter related** |

**All three are transmitted. I do not impose a preference.**

**مِنۢ بَعْدِ مَا جَآءَكَ مِنَ ٱلْعِلْمِ** — **and the most repeated note of the sūra once more:** the dispute comes **after** knowledge has arrived. **The same note stood at verse 19; it will come once more at verse 105.** **This is a repetition verifiable within the sūra.**

---

## 3/62-63 — إِنَّ هَٰذَا لَهُوَ ٱلْقَصَصُ ٱلْحَقُّ وَمَا مِنْ إِلَٰهٍ إِلَّا ٱللَّهُ

*Inna hādhā la-huwa'l-qaṣaṣu'l-ḥaqq, wa mā min ilāhin illa'llāh, wa inna'llāha la-huwa'l-ʿazīzu'l-ḥakīm · Fa-in tawallaw fa-inna'llāha ʿalīmun bi'l-mufsidīn*

"**Truly this is the true account itself.** There is no god but God, and God is **al-ʿAzīz**, **al-Ḥakīm**. · **If they turn away — God knows those who work corruption.**"

### ٱلْقَصَص — root: ق-ص-ص

The concrete sense of the root was treated in `012-yusuf.md` and in `028-kasas.md`: **ق-ص-ص — to follow a track.** *Qaṣṣa'l-athara* — he followed the trail. **From this comes *qiṣṣa*: to relate an event by tracing its track.**

**And the construction must be recorded: *al-qaṣaṣu'l-ḥaqq* — "the true account".** **That is, at the end of the block the truth of what has been related is recorded separately.**

**I record this as an observation about the word order:** the block turns back to its own source three times — 44 (*min anbāʾi'l-ghayb*), 58 (*natlūhu ʿalayk*), 62 (*al-qaṣaṣu'l-ḥaqq*). **Three notes, within a block of thirty verses.** This is a verifiable fact.

**And the emphatic pattern that closes the block is worth recording: *inna hādhā la-huwa'l-qaṣaṣu'l-ḥaqq*** — *inna* + *la* + the pronoun of separation (*huwa*). **Three instruments of emphasis together.** The same pattern once more in the same verse: *wa inna'llāha **la-huwa**'l-ʿazīzu'l-ḥakīm.*

**The sixty-third verse closes the block with the pattern of the limit of conveying: *fa-in tawallaw…*** **The same pattern occurred at verse 20 as well** (*wa in tawallaw fa-innamā ʿalayka'l-balāgh*). **Two occurrences, at the end of two blocks.**

---

## 3/64 — قُلْ يَٰٓأَهْلَ ٱلْكِتَٰبِ تَعَالَوْا۟ إِلَىٰ كَلِمَةٍ سَوَآءٍۭ بَيْنَنَا وَبَيْنَكُمْ أَلَّا نَعْبُدَ إِلَّا ٱللَّهَ

*Qul yā ahla'l-kitābi taʿālaw ilā kalimatin sawāʾin baynanā wa baynakum allā naʿbuda illa'llāha wa lā nushrika bihī shayʾan wa lā yattakhidha baʿḍunā baʿḍan arbāban min dūni'llāh, fa-in tawallaw fa-qūlu'shhadū bi-annā muslimūn*

"Say: '**People of the Book! Come to a word that is common between us and you:** that we serve none but God, that we associate nothing with Him, and that **none of us take others as lords apart from God.**' If they turn away, say: '**Bear witness that we are those who have handed themselves over.**'"

---

**This verse opens the third block of the sūra and is one of its most-quoted verses. I shall treat it in detail, because both the form of the call and its content are worth recording.**

### تَعَالَوْا۟ — the word itself

The root **ع-ل-و**: **height.** The imperative form of the verb *taʿālā*: ***taʿālaw* — "come".**

**And the picture of the word must be recorded, and the lexicographers dwell on it:** in Arabic *taʿāla* is properly **the calling up of one below by one who is in a high place**. In time it took on a general sense of "come"; but the upward movement in its root has remained.

**I record this as an observation about the language, and I build no claim of superiority on it** — because the verse itself blocks that: the place called to is **the middle between the two sides**, not the caller's own side.

### كَلِمَةٍ سَوَآءٍۭ بَيْنَنَا وَبَيْنَكُمْ — the construction taken apart

**The three words must be taken separately.**

**كَلِمَة** — the root **ك-ل-م**. It was treated at verse 45. **And here it comes indefinite: *kalimatin* — "a word".**

**سَوَآء** — the root **س-و-ي**: **to be equal, to be even, to be level.** *Sawāʾ* — **equal, common, at the same distance from both sides.**

**The concrete sense of the root is worth recording:** *istawā* — it became level, it came into balance. *Sawiyy* — even. **At the centre of the root stands *the absence of tilt*:** what does not lean to one side.

**بَيْنَنَا وَبَيْنَكُمْ** — "**between us and you**". **And the repetition of the word *bayna* must be recorded:** it says *baynanā wa baynakum*, not *baynanā wa kum*. **In Arabic this repetition stresses that the two sides are counted separately.**

**That is, the construction says: an utterance standing at an equal distance from both sides, imposed by neither upon the other.**

**I record this as my own reading, and its ground is the *absence of tilt* in the root of the word:** the call is not "come over to our side". **The ground itself is named as neutral.** And this is the reverse of what is usual at the opening of a dispute — what is usual is to declare one's own position the ground.

### The content of the common word: three items

**And the content of the call is given at once. It is three items, and the word order of all three must be recorded:**

| # | Item | Its person | Whom it binds |
|---|---|---|---|
| **1** | *Allā **naʿbuda** illa'llāh* | **First person plural** — "let **us** not serve" | **Both sides at once** |
| **2** | *Wa lā **nushrika** bihī shayʾā* | **First person plural** | **Both sides at once** |
| **3** | *Wa lā **yattakhidha baʿḍunā baʿḍan** arbāban min dūni'llāh* | **Third person, reciprocal** | **Both sides at once** |

**And this is the most important fact of word order in the verse, and it is usually passed over: all three items are in the "we" person.**

**The verse does not say "do not you associate". It says "let us not serve, let us not associate, let us not take one another as lords".**

**I record this as my own reading, and its ground is the inflection of the verbs:** the call is not a correction directed at the other side; it is set up as **an obligation the two sides will take on together.** **And this is the one thing that makes the call acceptable** — no one says yes to a sentence that begins "you are in the wrong".

### The third item: وَلَا يَتَّخِذَ بَعْضُنَا بَعْضًا أَرْبَابًا

**The third item stands apart from the first two and must be dwelt on separately.**

**The first two items are *vertical*: they order the relation between a human being and God.** **The third item is *horizontal*: it orders the relation between one human being and another.**

**رَبّ** — the root **ر-ب-ب**: **the one who nurtures, who owns, who takes charge of another's affair.** **The root was treated in detail in `001-fatiha.md`.**

**And *arbāb* is a plural and comes with the caveat *min dūni'llāh*: "lords apart from God".**

**What this item describes is explained in the classical commentaries** and is generally connected with **absolute obedience to religious authorities**. **I transmit this and add the following as my own reading:**

**The wording of the item names no class.** *Baʿḍunā baʿḍan* — "some of us, others of us". **A reciprocal pronoun. That is, the item does not say who takes whom as a lord; it describes the operation.**

**And that the item comes third is worth recording.** I give this as an observation about the word order: **after the common ground is declared, how that ground will be broken is also stated.** For even if the two sides agree on the first two items, if the third is violated the partnership turns into a hierarchy set up afresh.

### A comparison with al-ʿAnkabūt 29/46

**The same line was treated at 29/46 in `029-ankebut.md`, and there the utterance to be spoken was itself given.** The table there was this:

| Part | What it does |
|---|---|
| *Āmannā bi'lladhī unzila ilaynā **wa unzila ilaykum*** | **Common ground** — the two revelations are named together |
| *Wa ilāhunā wa ilāhukum **wāḥid*** | **A common addressee** |
| *Wa naḥnu lahū muslimūn* | **Declaring one's own position** |

**I rely on that treatment. And I compare the two verses:**

| | **al-ʿAnkabūt 29/46** | **Āl ʿImrān 3/64** |
|---|---|---|
| **Context** | *Jidāl* — **the method of disputing** | *Taʿālaw* — **an invitation** |
| **To whom the command is given** | To the believers: *lā tujādilū… wa qūlū* | To the Prophet: *qul* |
| **Where the common ground is set** | **In the revelations** — *unzila ilaynā wa unzila ilaykum* | **In a word** — *kalimatin sawāʾin* |
| **The common addressee** | *Wa ilāhunā wa ilāhukum **wāḥid*** — **a statement** | *Allā naʿbuda illa'llāh* — **an obligation** |
| **The person of the verbs** | *Āmannā* — **first person plural** | *Naʿbuda, nushrika* — **first person plural** |
| **The close** | *Wa naḥnu lahū **muslimūn*** | *Fa-qūlu'shhadū bi-annā **muslimūn*** |
| **Is there a horizontal item** | **No** | **Yes** — *lā yattakhidha baʿḍunā baʿḍan arbābā* |
| **If the other side refuses** | (Not in the verse) | *Fa-in **tawallaw*** — a closing sentence is given |

**I draw three observations from the table, and all three can be verified from the two texts.**

**One. The two verses end with the same word: *muslimūn*.** In al-ʿAnkabūt *wa naḥnu lahū muslimūn*, in Āl ʿImrān *bi-annā muslimūn*. **And in both, this word is not a ruling directed at the other side but the speaker's declaration of his own position.**

**Two. Both verses state what is shared first and what divides afterwards.** In al-ʿAnkabūt the two revelations are named together, then *naḥnu lahū muslimūn* comes. In Āl ʿImrān three shared items are counted, then *fa-in tawallaw* comes. **That is, the sentence of division is spoken after the sentence of sharing, and only if there is a refusal.**

**Three. Āl ʿImrān says one item more, and that item is horizontal.** In al-ʿAnkabūt there is no note about the relation between human beings. **I record this as my own reading:** the context of the two sūras explains this difference. Al-ʿAnkabūt is a Meccan sūra and its subject is the **method** of disputing; Āl ʿImrān is Medinan, and what it addresses is not only an opinion but **an established order.** When one speaks with an order, the question of hierarchy among human beings becomes unavoidable.

### فَإِن تَوَلَّوْا۟ فَقُولُوا۟ ٱشْهَدُوا۟ بِأَنَّا مُسْلِمُونَ

**What is to be said in the case of refusal is also given. And its word order must be recorded.**

**What is commanded to be said is not a threat, not an accusation, not even an answer.** ***Ishhadū* — "bear witness".** **What is asked of the other side is not acceptance but *testimony*.**

**I record this as my own reading, and its ground is the choice of the verb:** *ishhadū* does not put the other side into a position; it gives them a **role** — the one who records what he has seen. **That is, even the side that refuses is not thrown outside the conversation.**

**And the same verb occurred earlier in the sūra, in the mouth of the disciples:** *wa'shhad bi-annā muslimūn* (52). **There the addressee was God; here the addressees are people.**

| Verse | To whom *ishhad* is said | Who says it |
|---|---|---|
| **52** | **To God** | The disciples |
| **64** | **To the other side** | The believers |

**The same verb, the same pattern, two different addressees.** This is a repetition verifiable within the sūra.

### What this says to the present

**This verse gives a method for speaking with one who differs, and that method lies in four steps. I draw the steps from the wording of the verse:**

| Step | Its counterpart in the verse | What it does |
|---|---|---|
| **1** | *Taʿālaw ilā **kalimatin sawāʾin*** | The ground is declared **common** |
| **2** | *Allā **naʿbuda**… wa lā **nushrika*** | The obligation falls on **both sides** at once |
| **3** | *Wa lā yattakhidha **baʿḍunā baʿḍan** arbābā* | The **hierarchy** between them is removed |
| **4** | *Fa-in tawallaw fa-qūlū **ishhadū*** | Even in the case of refusal **the door is not closed** |

**And the fourth step is the one most easily passed over.** The verse takes the possibility of refusal into account from the start and gives what is to be said in that case as well. **What is given is not a sentence of rupture but a sentence of record.**

**Per the method (`USLUP.md`) I record this: no blanket ruling about any community can be drawn from this verse — neither favourable nor unfavourable.** The verse sets up a **call**; who answers the call and how is not the subject of the verse. **And verse 113 of the sūra will record plainly that the answer is not of one kind.**

---

## 3/65-68 — يَٰٓأَهْلَ ٱلْكِتَٰبِ لِمَ تُحَآجُّونَ فِىٓ إِبْرَٰهِيمَ وَمَآ أُنزِلَتِ ٱلتَّوْرَىٰةُ وَٱلْإِنجِيلُ إِلَّا مِنۢ بَعْدِهِۦٓ

*Yā ahla'l-kitābi lima tuḥājjūna fī Ibrāhīma wa mā unzilati't-Tawrātu wa'l-Injīlu illā min baʿdih, a-fa-lā taʿqilūn · Hā-antum hāʾulāʾi ḥājajtum fīmā lakum bihī ʿilm, fa-lima tuḥājjūna fīmā laysa lakum bihī ʿilm, wa'llāhu yaʿlamu wa antum lā taʿlamūn · Mā kāna Ibrāhīmu yahūdiyyan wa lā naṣrāniyyan wa lākin kāna ḥanīfan musliman, wa mā kāna mina'l-mushrikīn · Inna awla'n-nāsi bi-Ibrāhīma la'lladhīna'ttabaʿūhu wa hādha'n-nabiyyu wa'lladhīna āmanū, wa'llāhu waliyyu'l-muʾminīn*

"**People of the Book! Why do you dispute about Abraham? Both the Torah and the Gospel were sent down only after him. Will you not use your reason?** · There you are: you disputed about what you had knowledge of; **why then do you dispute about what you have no knowledge of?** God knows, and you do not know. · **Abraham was neither a Jew nor a Christian; he was a *ḥanīf*, one who had handed himself over**, and he was not of the *mushrikūn*. · **The nearest of people to Abraham are those who followed him, and this prophet, and those who believe.** God is the protector of the believers."

---

**These four verses build the first proof of the third block. And the kind of proof must be recorded: this is not a proof from *history* but a proof from the *logic of time*. I shall treat it in detail, because the form of reasoning built here does not come again so plainly in the sūra.**

### The logic of time in the proof — أُنزِلَتِ … إِلَّا مِنۢ بَعْدِهِۦ

**The structure of the sentence is this, and it consists of three elements:**

| Element | Wording | What it conveys |
|---|---|---|
| **1** | *Lima **tuḥājjūna** fī Ibrāhīm* | **There is a dispute**, and its subject is a person |
| **2** | *Wa mā unzilati't-Tawrātu wa'l-Injīlu **illā min baʿdih*** | **The two books came down *after* him** |
| **3** | *A-fa-lā **taʿqilūn*** | **Let the reader draw the conclusion** |

**And the second element carries the whole sentence. Its word order must be recorded separately: *mā … illā* — negation and exception.** In Arabic this pattern sets up **restriction** (*ḥaṣr*): "not otherwise, but only thus." That is, the sentence does not say "they came down afterwards"; it says **"they came down only after him"**. **No other possibility is left.**

**I write out the logic of the proof plainly, because it looks short and is deep:**

A person cannot be assigned to an identity that arose **after** him. The two adjectives set before Abraham's name (*yahūdī*, *naṣrānī*) cannot be attached to a person who lived before the two books that gave rise to those two adjectives. **For a name cannot exist before the thing that brings it into being.**

**This is an objection that removes not the subject of the dispute but the *possibility* of the dispute.** Before saying "Abraham was such and such", the verse says "what you are putting forward cannot be set up in point of time".

**I record this as an observation about logic, and its ground is the verse's own wording:** the sentence names the error of **anachronism** (placing a thing in a time that is not its own). And it does so not by a term but by **a date**: the time when the two books came down.

**And I draw a boundary:** the verse is not disputing the **worth** of the two books. In the third verse both were named as *hudan li'n-nās* (3/3-4). **What is at issue here is not the truth of the books but the fact that a person lived before them.** The two questions must not be confused.

### أَفَلَا تَعْقِلُونَ — the conclusion left unsaid

**And the verse does not state the conclusion.** The sentence "therefore Abraham was of neither of the two" is **not** in verse 65; it is held back until verse 67.

**I record this as an observation about the word order:** verse 66 comes in between, and there the **method** of disputing is discussed. That is, the order is this — first the proof, then the method, then the conclusion. **The conclusion is put last.**

The root **ع-ق-ل**: **to bind.** *ʿIqāl* — the cord that binds a camel's knee. **The root was treated in many places in the series; I do not repeat it here.** What belongs here is this: the picture of the root is **binding**, and what is asked to be bound here are **a date and a claim**.

### 3/66 — what there is knowledge of and what there is not

*Hā-antum hāʾulāʾi ḥājajtum fīmā lakum bihī ʿilm, fa-lima tuḥājjūna fīmā laysa lakum bihī ʿilm*

**The verse divides disputing in two, and the division must be recorded:**

| Kind | Wording | Ruling |
|---|---|---|
| **Disputing that rests on knowledge** | *Fīmā lakum bihī ʿilm* | **Not forbidden** — it is recorded that it happened |
| **Disputing that does not rest on knowledge** | *Fīmā **laysa** lakum bihī ʿilm* | ***Lima* — "why?"** |

**And this is one of the most notable notes of the sūra in point of method. I give it as my own reading, and its ground is the opposition of the two sentences:** the verse does not forbid disputing in itself. What it forbids is **groundless** disputing. And the measure is not which side one is on but whether one has **knowledge** in hand.

**هَٰٓأَنتُمْ هَٰٓؤُلَآءِ** — the coming of two particles of attention (*hā*) and two demonstratives one after another conveys in Arabic a **direct confrontation**: "there you are, you of all people." **The same pattern will come once more in the sūra: at verse 119, *hā-antum ulāʾi tuḥibbūnahum*.** This is a repetition verifiable within the sūra, and in both the addressee is confronted with **a false position**.

### 3/67 — مَا كَانَ إِبْرَٰهِيمُ يَهُودِيًّا وَلَا نَصْرَانِيًّا

**The word order of the sentence must be recorded, and it has three steps:**

| Step | Wording | Its work |
|---|---|---|
| **1** | *Mā kāna… yahūdiyyan **wa lā** naṣrāniyyā* | **It removes both names** |
| **2** | *Wa lākin kāna **ḥanīfan musliman*** | **It puts two attributes in their place** |
| **3** | *Wa mā kāna **mina'l-mushrikīn*** | **It closes a third possibility too** |

**And the real point is the difference between the first and second steps: what is removed are *names*, what is set down are *attributes*.**

*Yahūdī* and *naṣrānī* are relative nouns conveying membership — they state belonging to a community. *Ḥanīf* and *muslim*, on the other hand, are each **a quality**: one conveys a turning, the other a handing over. **Neither of them points to a group, nor does the plural of either give a list of members.**

**I record this as my own reading, and its ground is the difference between the two kinds of word:** the verse does not answer a claim of membership with another claim of membership. **The kind of answer is changed** — what was asked was "of which side?", and what is given is "of what quality?".

**And per the method (`USLUP.md`) I record this plainly: no ruling about any community follows from this verse.** The verse removes two names from Abraham; **it says nothing about those who bear those names.** Verse 113 of the sūra will enter the note on this separately.

### حَنِيف — root: ح-ن-ف

**The root was analysed in detail at 30/30 in `030-rum.md` (*ḥanīfan fiṭrata'llāh*) and at 2/135 in `002-bakara.md`. I rely on that treatment and do not repeat it here.** The summary recorded there:

> **ح-ن-ف** — **to incline in one direction, to turn away from crookedness.** The lexicographers tie the word to a concrete usage: *aḥnaf* is a person whose feet turn inward. **The idea that comes out of this: a *ḥanīf* is one who parts from what is common and inclines in another direction.**

**And what belongs here is this, which I give as an observation about the word order:** *ḥanīf* conveys **a parting**; and the verse uses it in the very sentence in which two memberships are refused. **That is, the picture of the word itself coincides with the work of the sentence:** a turning that goes outside the two names.

**مُسْلِمًا** — root **س-ل-م**. **It was analysed at verse nineteen; I do not repeat it here.** The only note belonging here is this: the word comes here **indefinite and as an attribute** — *musliman*, as an adjective. **That is, the sentence points not to an institution but to a state.**

### 3/68 — أَوْلَى ٱلنَّاسِ بِإِبْرَٰهِيمَ

*Inna awla'n-nāsi bi-Ibrāhīma la'lladhīna'ttabaʿūhu wa hādha'n-nabiyyu wa'lladhīna āmanū*

The root **و-ل-ي**: **nearness, adjacency.** *Walī* — the one who is near. *Awlā* — the elative: **the one who is nearer.**

**And the verse sets the measure of nearness plainly: *alladhīna'ttabaʿūh* — "those who *followed* him".** **Not descent, not a name, not membership — *ittibāʿ*.**

**I record this as an observation:** the measure of nearness is drawn out of the dispute itself. The sides were disputing **who belonged** to Abraham; the verse turns the measure to **who followed him.** **And this measure is not something that can be claimed but something that can be observed.**

And the end of the verse must be recorded: *wa'llāhu **waliyyu'l-muʾminīn*** — a word from the same root (*و-ل-ي*), this time of God. **That is, the verse closes a dispute about nearness by naming the source of nearness.** The use of the same root twice in one verse, in two different directions, is a fact of word order.

---

## 3/69-74 — وَدَّت طَّآئِفَةٌ مِّنْ أَهْلِ ٱلْكِتَٰبِ لَوْ يُضِلُّونَكُمْ … يَٰٓأَهْلَ ٱلْكِتَٰبِ لِمَ تَلْبِسُونَ ٱلْحَقَّ بِٱلْبَٰطِلِ

*Waddat ṭāʾifatun min ahli'l-kitābi law yuḍillūnakum wa mā yuḍillūna illā anfusahum wa mā yashʿurūn · Yā ahla'l-kitābi lima takfurūna bi-āyāti'llāhi wa antum tashhadūn · Yā ahla'l-kitābi lima talbisūna'l-ḥaqqa bi'l-bāṭili wa taktumūna'l-ḥaqqa wa antum taʿlamūn*

"**A party of the People of the Book wished to lead you astray;** yet they lead none astray but themselves, and they do not perceive it. · **People of the Book! Why do you deny the signs of God — and you yourselves bearing witness?** · **People of the Book! Why do you dress truth up in falsehood and conceal the truth knowingly?**"

### طَآئِفَةٌ مِّنْ — the caveat is placed at the very start

**And this is a fact of word order to be underlined in this commentary: the sentence does not say *ahlu'l-kitāb*; it says *ṭāʾifatun min ahli'l-kitāb* — "a party of the People of the Book".**

***Min* is here the particle of *tabʿīḍ* in Arabic (indicating a part of a whole).** That is, the subject is not the whole of the set but **a part** of it.

**The same caveat is there in the next verse too: *wa qālat ṭāʾifatun min ahli'l-kitāb* (72).** **And at verse 75 it will come once more, this time in both directions.**

| Verse | Subject | Extent |
|---|---|---|
| **69** | *Ṭāʾifatun **min** ahli'l-kitāb* | **A party** |
| **72** | *Ṭāʾifatun **min** ahli'l-kitāb* | **A party** |
| **75** | *Wa **min** ahli'l-kitāb… wa **minhum*** | **Two parties, set against each other** |
| **113** | ***Min** ahli'l-kitābi ummatun qāʾima* | **A party — favourable** |
| **199** | *Wa inna **min** ahli'l-kitābi la-man yuʾmin* | **A party — favourable** |

**The same particle in all five verses: *min*.** This is a fact verifiable within the sūra, and it **settles how every statement of the sūra about the People of the Book is to be read.**

### لِمَ تَلْبِسُونَ ٱلْحَقَّ بِٱلْبَٰطِلِ — root: ل-ب-س

The root **ل-ب-س**: **to clothe, to cover over; and from this, to confuse, to make indistinct by covering.** *Libās* — a garment. *Labs* — confusion, *iltibās*.

**And the picture of the root must be recorded:** one thing is **put on** over another; what is put on does not destroy what is beneath — **it makes it invisible.**
