**I give this as an observation about the word:** the verse does not say "you destroy the truth". It says **you clothe it.** **That is, the truth stands where it is, but because something has been drawn over it, it is not recognised.**

**And the verse sets two separate verbs side by side, and the two differ:**

| Verb | The operation | What becomes of the truth |
|---|---|---|
| *Talbisūn* | **Mixing** | It stands, but **is not recognised** |
| *Taktumūn* | **Concealing** | **It is not there at all** |

**Two separate operations, in one sentence.** In the first something is added, in the second something is taken away.

And it must be recorded that both sentences end with the same note: *wa antum **tashhadūn*** (70) — *wa antum **taʿlamūn*** (71). **Both are notes of "knowingly".** That is, the subject of the verses is not ignorance; it is **what is done despite the presence of knowledge.** This is the very note set up in the nineteenth verse: *min baʿdi mā jāʾahumu'l-ʿilm*.

### 3/72-73 — believing in the morning and turning back in the evening

*Āminū bi'lladhī unzila ʿala'lladhīna āmanū **wajha'n-nahāri** wa'kfurū **ākhirah**, laʿallahum yarjiʿūn*

"'**At the beginning of the day** believe in what has been sent down to those who believe, and **at the end of it** disbelieve; perhaps they will turn back.'"

**وَجْهَ ٱلنَّهَارِ** — "the face of the day", that is, **its beginning.** In Arabic the *wajh* of a thing is the side of it that appears first.

**And the verse describes a method: belief is used as an *instrument*.** To join in the morning and to part in the evening — and the calculated result of this is that the others should **fall into indecision** (*laʿallahum yarjiʿūn*).

**I record this as an observation and I draw a boundary:** the verse describes a **tactic** and limits the speaker to one party (*ṭāʾifatun min*). **No blanket attribute of any community is drawn from this in this commentary;** what is drawn is **the naming of a form of conduct.**

**وَلَا تُؤْمِنُوٓا۟ إِلَّا لِمَن تَبِعَ دِينَكُمْ** (73) — "**believe none but him who follows your religion**". **And the work of the sentence must be recorded: the measure of truth is tied not to *what is said* but to *the affiliation of the one who says it*.**

**And the answer comes at once: *qul inna'l-hudā huda'llāh*** — "**Say: guidance is the guidance of God.**" **That is, the measure is not the side but the source.**

***Qul inna'l-faḍla bi-yadi'llāh*** (73) and *yakhtaṣṣu bi-raḥmatihī man yashāʾ* (74) — **the section closes by saying to whom the choosing belongs.** **And this does the same work as the verse *mālika'l-mulk* at verse 26:** there dominion, here *faḍl*. **Both verses close a dispute by saying in whose hand a thing is.** This is a weave verifiable within the sūra.

---

## 3/75-76 — وَمِنْ أَهْلِ ٱلْكِتَٰبِ مَنْ إِن تَأْمَنْهُ بِقِنطَارٍ يُؤَدِّهِۦٓ إِلَيْكَ وَمِنْهُم مَّنْ إِن تَأْمَنْهُ بِدِينَارٍ لَّا يُؤَدِّهِۦٓ إِلَيْكَ

*Wa min ahli'l-kitābi man in taʾmanhu bi-qinṭārin yuʾaddihī ilayk, wa minhum man in taʾmanhu bi-dīnārin lā yuʾaddihī ilayka illā mā dumta ʿalayhi qāʾimā, dhālika bi-annahum qālū laysa ʿalaynā fi'l-ummiyyīna sabīl, wa yaqūlūna ʿala'llāhi'l-kadhiba wa hum yaʿlamūn · Balā man awfā bi-ʿahdihī wa'ttaqā fa-inna'llāha yuḥibbu'l-muttaqīn*

"**Among the People of the Book there is one who, if you entrust him with a *qinṭār*, will give it back to you;** and **among them there is one who, if you entrust him with a single dinar, will not give it back to you unless you stand over him.** That is because they say, '**We have no obligation regarding the *ummiyyūn***'; and they speak a lie against God knowingly. · **No indeed! Whoever fulfils his covenant and guards himself — God loves those who guard themselves.**"

---

**This verse will be treated in detail in this commentary, because its *division* settles how the sūra's discussion of the People of the Book is to be read.**

### The division: وَمِنْ … وَمِنْهُم

**The backbone of the verse is two particles, and both are *min*:**

| Part | Wording | Who |
|---|---|---|
| **First** | ***Wa min** ahli'l-kitābi man…* | **Some of them** — the one who returns a trust |
| **Second** | ***Wa minhum** man…* | **Some of them** — the one who does not |

**And this is the most important fact of the verse and must certainly be brought forward: the sentence is a division, not a ruling.**

**The verse does not say "the People of the Book betray a trust." Neither does it say "the People of the Book are faithful to a trust."** It counts the two groups **in the same sentence**, in the same pattern, with the same particle. **And it counts the favourable one *first*.**

**I record this as an observation about the word order, and its ground is the sequence:** the sentence begins with the trustworthy one. **That is, the text's own order blocks the building of a blanket unfavourable ruling at the very first word.**

**And per the method (`USLUP.md`) I write plainly: no blanket ruling about any religious community can be built on this verse — because the verse itself does not build one.** The verse describes **two kinds of conduct** and assigns a part to each. **Whoever does that conduct, the verse speaks of him.**

### Supports within the series: 29/47 and 13/36

**The same division was treated in two separate places in the series, and I rely on them:**

| Place | Wording | What the division is about |
|---|---|---|
| **al-ʿAnkabūt 29/47** | *Fa'lladhīna ātaynāhumu'l-kitāba yuʾminūna bih, **wa min hāʾulāʾi** man yuʾminu bih* | **Believing** |
| **al-Raʿd 13/36** | *Wa'lladhīna ātaynāhumu'l-kitāba yafraḥūna bimā unzila ilayk, **wa mina'l-aḥzābi** man yunkiru baʿḍah* | **Rejoicing and denying** |
| **Āl ʿImrān 3/75** | *Wa min ahli'l-kitābi man… **wa minhum** man…* | **Returning a trust** |

**Three sūras, three separate subjects, the same structure.** This is a weave verifiable within the series, and I rely on `029-ankebut.md` 29/47 and `013-rad.md` 13/36.

**I add the following as my own reading, and it is not binding:** when the Qurʾān speaks of the People of the Book it does not build a single sentence of ruling; **it builds divided sentences.** And the axis of the division is each time a **conduct** or an **attitude**, and never a name.

### قِنطَار / دِينَار — the opposition of two amounts

**At the fourteenth verse *al-qanāṭīru'l-muqanṭara* was treated, and it was recorded there that *qinṭār* is a large amount and that the lexicographers do not agree on any definite figure.** **I rely on that treatment.**

**And the word order here must be recorded, and it is very fine:**

| | What is entrusted | Outcome |
|---|---|---|
| **The first part** | ***Qinṭār*** — **very large** | **He gives it back** |
| **The second part** | ***Dīnār*** — **a single coin** | **He does not give it back** |

**The opposition shows the conduct of the two sides at their furthest points: one gives back the largest, the other does not give back the smallest.** And the two extremes prove that the distinction **has nothing to do with the amount.** **What is being measured is not the size of the property but the attitude of the person.**

**I record this as my own reading, and its ground is the choice of the two amounts:** had the verse given the same amount to both, the difference would have been a difference of degree. **By choosing the two extremes it turns the difference into a difference of *kind*.**

### إِلَّا مَا دُمْتَ عَلَيْهِ قَآئِمًا

"**Unless you stand over him.**" **And this note describes the second attitude exactly: the return is the result not of an obligation but of *supervision*.**

**قَآئِمًا** — root **ق-و-م**: to stand. **And the same word will come in a wholly different place in the sūra, at verse 113: *ummatun qāʾima* — "a community that stands upright". The same word in two different senses: one conveys the one who stands watch, the other the one who stands upright. This is a repetition verifiable within the sūra, and the root picture of the word (*to stand*) is at work in both.**

### لَيْسَ عَلَيْنَا فِى ٱلْأُمِّيِّۦنَ سَبِيلٌ — the naming of the pretext

**And the verse gives the *pretext* of the second attitude as well. And this is the real subject of the verse.**

*Laysa ʿalaynā fi'l-ummiyyīna sabīl* — "**there is no way (no obligation) upon us regarding the *ummiyyūn*.**"

**That is, the obligation has been lifted according to who the other party is.** The moral rule has been narrowed **according to the one addressed.**

**And the answer the verse gives to this sentence must be recorded, and it has two steps:**

**One.** *Wa yaqūlūna ʿala'llāhi'l-kadhiba **wa hum yaʿlamūn*** — **the ascribing of this narrowing to God is named separately.** That is, what is criticised is not only the conduct; it is **the attributing of the conduct to religion.**

**Two.** *Balā man awfā bi-ʿahdihī wa'ttaqā* (76) — **and the counter-sentence comes.**

The particle **بَلَىٰ** in Arabic **sets up an affirmative by rejecting a negative sentence.** *Laysa ʿalaynā… sabīl* had been said; *balā* refutes it directly: **"no, it is not so."**

**And the word order of the sentence put in its place must be recorded: *man awfā bi-ʿahdihī wa'ttaqā*** — **a conditional sentence, and its subject is *man*: "whoever."** **No group name at all.**

**I record this as an observation about the word order, and it is the sūra's method:** a party had narrowed an obligation according to group membership. **The answer is given in a sentence that ties the obligation to no group at all.** The measure is *man* — whoever it may be.

### What this says to the present

**The operation the verse describes is a recognisable operation today too, and it has a name: the narrowing of a moral rule according to the one it is applied to.**

A rule is set up "for everyone"; but in practice a **circle** is drawn, and those outside the circle are taken out of the rule's reach. **And this narrowing is generally done with a pretext** — because the narrowing itself cannot be defended, but its pretext can.

**The verse's answer lies in two places, and both are in the text:** the ascribing of the pretext to religion is counted as a separate offence (*wa yaqūlūna ʿala'llāhi'l-kadhib*), and the counter-measure is set up with a *man* that recognises no circle (76).

**Per the method (`USLUP.md`) I record this: no conclusion about any religious or ethnic group is drawn from this section.** What is described is **an attitude**, and the verse itself, in its first word (*wa min*), blocked the assigning of that attitude to the whole of a group.

---

## 3/77-78 — إِنَّ ٱلَّذِينَ يَشْتَرُونَ بِعَهْدِ ٱللَّهِ وَأَيْمَٰنِهِمْ ثَمَنًا قَلِيلًا · وَإِنَّ مِنْهُمْ لَفَرِيقًا يَلْوُۥنَ أَلْسِنَتَهُم بِٱلْكِتَٰبِ

*Inna'lladhīna yashtarūna bi-ʿahdi'llāhi wa aymānihim thamanan qalīlan ulāʾika lā khalāqa lahum fi'l-ākhirati wa lā yukallimuhumu'llāhu wa lā yanẓuru ilayhim yawma'l-qiyāmati wa lā yuzakkīhim wa lahum ʿadhābun alīm · Wa inna minhum la-farīqan yalwūna alsinatahum bi'l-kitābi li-taḥsabūhu mina'l-kitābi wa mā huwa mina'l-kitāb, wa yaqūlūna huwa min ʿindi'llāhi wa mā huwa min ʿindi'llāh, wa yaqūlūna ʿala'llāhi'l-kadhiba wa hum yaʿlamūn*

"**Those who sell the covenant of God and their oaths for a small price** — they have no share in the hereafter; God will not speak to them or look at them on the day of resurrection, nor will He purify them; and theirs is a painful punishment. · **A group of them twist their tongues with the Book so that you may think it is from the Book;** yet it is not from the Book. '**It is from God**', they say; yet it is not from God. And they speak a lie against God knowingly."

### يَلْوُۥنَ أَلْسِنَتَهُم — root: ل-و-ي

The root **ل-و-ي**: **to twist, to wring, to turn.** To twist a rope, to turn a thing on its own axis.

**The root was analysed in `063-munafikun.md` and tabulated there together with the root ح-ر-ف. I rely on that treatment and do not repeat it here.** The summary recorded there was this:

> ***ل-و-ي* and *ح-ر-ف* are two names for the same operation. One describes twisting, the other pulling from the edge. Neither breaks; both bend.**

**And what belongs here is this, and it deserves to be spelled out:**

**What is twisted is not the *Book* but the *tongue*.** The verse does not say *yalwūna'l-kitāb*; it says ***yalwūna alsinatahum bi'l-kitāb*** — "**they twist their tongues with the Book**".

**I record this as an observation about the word order, and it is the finest point in the verse:** the text is not touched. **What is altered is the reciting.** The same letters are made to resemble something else by a different movement of the tongue.

**And the verse itself says this plainly: *li-taḥsabūhu mina'l-kitāb*** — "**so that you may *think* it is from the Book**". **The verb is *ḥasiba*: to think, to suppose.** What is aimed at is not a change in the text but a change in **the impression in the hearer**.

**And the root *ح-س-ب* will come four more times in the sūra after this:** *lā taḥsabanna* / *lā yaḥsabanna* (169, 178, 180, 188). **This is a repetition verifiable within the sūra and was recorded in the table in the introduction.**

**I record this as my own reading, and its ground is the object of the verb:** the sūra uses the verb "to suppose" both here and at the end. **Here it is one person making another suppose wrongly; at the end it is a person supposing wrongly of himself.** The same verb in two directions.

### The rhythm of the sentence: four negations

**The seventy-seventh verse sets four negative sentences one after another, and the word order must be recorded:**

| # | Sentence | What is removed |
|---|---|---|
| **1** | *Lā **khalāqa** lahum fi'l-ākhira* | **A share** |
| **2** | *Wa lā **yukallimuhum**u'llāh* | **Speech** |
| **3** | *Wa lā **yanẓuru** ilayhim* | **A look** |
| **4** | *Wa lā **yuzakkīhim*** | **Purification** |

**Four negations, and two of them (speech and a look) are words of *relation*.** **I record this as an observation:** the sentence of punishment leaves the punishment to the last; it first says that the relation is cut off.

**ثَمَنًا قَلِيلًا** — "**a small price**". **And the construction will come once more in the sūra, at verse 187** (*wa'shtaraw bihī thamanan qalīlā*) **and at verse 199 in the negative** (*lā yashtarūna bi-āyāti'llāhi thamanan qalīlā*). **Three occurrences, and the third is the opposite of the first two.** This is a repetition verifiable within the sūra.

**I record this as my own reading:** the adjective itself carries a ruling. *Thamanan qalīlā* — the price is named "small". **That is, while describing the operation as a transaction, the verse says in the same sentence that the transaction is at a loss.**

---

## 3/79-80 — مَا كَانَ لِبَشَرٍ أَن يُؤْتِيَهُ ٱللَّهُ ٱلْكِتَٰبَ وَٱلْحُكْمَ وَٱلنُّبُوَّةَ ثُمَّ يَقُولَ لِلنَّاسِ كُونُوا۟ عِبَادًا لِّى مِن دُونِ ٱللَّهِ

*Mā kāna li-basharin an yuʾtiyahu'llāhu'l-kitāba wa'l-ḥukma wa'n-nubuwwata thumma yaqūla li'n-nāsi kūnū ʿibādan lī min dūni'llāhi wa lākin kūnū rabbāniyyīna bimā kuntum tuʿallimūna'l-kitāba wa bimā kuntum tadrusūn · Wa lā yaʾmurakum an tattakhidhu'l-malāʾikata wa'n-nabiyyīna arbābā, a-yaʾmurukum bi'l-kufri baʿda idh antum muslimūn*

"**It is not for a human being to whom God has given the Book, judgement and prophethood then to say to people, 'Be servants to me apart from God.'** Rather: '**Be *rabbānī*s, by the Book you teach and by what you study.**' · **Nor does He command you to take the angels and the prophets as lords.** Would He command you to disbelief after you have handed yourselves over?"

---

**These two verses are the unfolding of the third item of verse 64 (*wa lā yattakhidha baʿḍunā baʿḍan arbāban min dūni'llāh*). And this is a link verifiable within the sūra: the same word (*arbāb*) and the same caveat (*min dūni'llāh*) occur twice, fifteen verses apart.**

### مَا كَانَ لِبَشَرٍ — a pattern of impossibility

The pattern ***mā kāna li-fulānin an yafʿala*** conveys in Arabic **an unfittingness and an impossibility**: "this is not a thing that can be for him."

And the choice of subject must be recorded: *li-**bashar*** — "for a **human being**". **It does not say prophet; it says human being.**

**I record this as an observation about the word order:** the sentence sets the prohibition on the widest set. **Even after the three highest things that can be given to a person have been counted, that person is named "human being".**

**And the threefold count must be recorded: *al-kitāb*, *al-ḥukm*, *al-nubuwwa*.** **The same three occurred at verse 48 of the sūra for Jesus, and at 45/16 in `045-casiye.md` for the Children of Israel.** **I rely on `045-casiye.md` 45/16-20.**

### كُونُوا۟ رَبَّٰنِيِّۦنَ — the alternative set in its place

**And the verse does not content itself with a prohibition; it sets a sentence in its place. The word order is this:**

| | What is refused | What is set down |
|---|---|---|
| The command | *Kūnū **ʿibādan lī** min dūni'llāh* | *Kūnū **rabbāniyyīn*** |
| Attachment to whom | **To the speaker** | **To the Lord** |
| Its ground | (none) | *Bimā kuntum **tuʿallimūna**'l-kitāb* |

**رَبَّانِيّ** — root **ر-ب-ب**. **The lexicographers differ over the derivation of the word, and the disagreement is not to be concealed:**

| Reading | Where *rabbānī* comes from |
|---|---|
| 1 | A relative form of *rabb* — **one attached to the Lord, belonging to the Lord** |
| 2 | From the verb *rabba/yarubbu* — **one who nurtures, who brings up** |
| 3 | A scholar occupied with knowledge and teaching |

**All three are transmitted; I do not impose a preference.** **But the verse's own note gives an indication, and I record this as an indication, not as a preference:** the continuation of the sentence is *bimā kuntum **tuʿallimūna**'l-kitāba wa bimā kuntum **tadrusūn*** — **teaching and studying.** **That is, the word is named within the verse together with learning and teaching.**

**And that the word *rabbānī* is from the same root as *rabb* is a fact of word order and must be recorded:** in saying "do not take *arbāb*", the verse gives the alternative with a word from the same root. **What is refused is the applying of the root to human beings in the plural (*arbāb*); what is set down is a relative form of the same root (*rabbānī*).**

**I record this as my own reading, and it is not binding:** the verse does not forbid attachment, **it corrects where the attachment goes.** The same root stands both in the prohibition and in the alternative; what changes is to whom the root is applied.

### 3/80 — the angels and the prophets counted too

**And the eightieth verse widens the list: *al-malāʾikata wa'n-nabiyyīn*.**

**I record this as an observation about the word order:** the prohibition names the two likeliest candidates. A person is not seated in the position of an intermediary on a claim of nearness to a stone or a tree, but **to an angel and a prophet**. **The verse builds the list with the two most revered names** — because the question is not whether the intermediary is of worth but **whether he stands in between.**

**And the verse's last note: *baʿda idh antum muslimūn*** — "**after you have handed yourselves over**". **The word is again *muslim*: the last word of verse 64, the attribute of Abraham in verse 67, and now the state of the one addressed.** **Three occurrences within sixteen verses.** This is a repetition verifiable within the sūra.

---

## 3/81-82 — وَإِذْ أَخَذَ ٱللَّهُ مِيثَٰقَ ٱلنَّبِيِّۦنَ

*Wa idh akhadha'llāhu mīthāqa'n-nabiyyīna lamā ātaytukum min kitābin wa ḥikmatin thumma jāʾakum rasūlun muṣaddiqun limā maʿakum la-tuʾminunna bihī wa la-tanṣurunnah, qāla a-aqrartum wa akhadhtum ʿalā dhālikum iṣrī, qālū aqrarnā, qāla fa'shhadū wa ana maʿakum mina'sh-shāhidīn · Fa-man tawallā baʿda dhālika fa-ulāʾika humu'l-fāsiqūn*

"**God took this covenant from the prophets:** 'I have given you a Book and wisdom; **then, when a messenger comes to you confirming what is with you, you shall surely believe in him and you shall surely help him.**' '**Do you affirm this, and do you take up My heavy charge on these terms?**' He said. '**We affirm it**', they said. '**Then bear witness; and I am with you among the witnesses**', He said. · **Whoever turns away after this — those are the ones who have gone out of the way.**"

### The subject of the covenant: a chain of confirmation

**The content of the pledge must be recorded, and it is tied to two verbs:**

| Verb | What is asked |
|---|---|
| *La-**tuʾminunna** bih* | **Believing** |
| *Wa la-**tanṣurunnah*** | **Helping** |

**Both verbs come with the *nūn* of emphasis** — in Arabic the most forceful binding form an utterance can take. **That is, the pledge is not a loose intention but an undertaking taken in the heaviest pattern.**

**And the condition itself must be recorded: *rasūlun muṣaddiqun limā maʿakum*.** **The attribute of the messenger who comes: one who *confirms* what is with you.** **The same attribute was used of the Book in the third verse of the sūra: *muṣaddiqan limā bayna yadayh*.** **Two occurrences, one for the Book, one for the messenger.** This is a repetition verifiable within the sūra.

### إِصْر — root: أ-ص-ر

The root **أ-ص-ر**: **to bind, to hold fast; and a heavy load.** *Iṣr* — both a bond and a burden hard to carry.

**And both senses of the word are at work here at once:** the pledge taken is both a **bond** and a **burden**. **The word occurred at 2/286 in `002-bakara.md` (*wa lā taḥmil ʿalaynā iṣrā*)**, and there it is the subject of a prayer. **Here it is an obligation taken up.**

| Place | *Iṣr* | Who does what |
|---|---|---|
| **al-Baqara 2/286** | *Wa lā taḥmil ʿalaynā **iṣrā*** | **It is asked that it not be laid on** |
| **Āl ʿImrān 3/81** | *Wa akhadhtum ʿalā dhālikum **iṣrī*** | **Its being taken up is affirmed** |

**Two occurrences, two directions.** This is a fact verifiable from the text.

### فَٱشْهَدُوا۟ وَأَنَا۠ مَعَكُم مِّنَ ٱلشَّٰهِدِينَ

**And the compact closes with a call to bear witness. And both sides of the witnessing are counted.**

**I record this as an observation about the word order:** the verb *ishhadū* had occurred twice earlier in the sūra (52: the disciples; 64: to the other side). **Now for a third time, and this time to the prophets.** **Three occurrences, three separate addressees:**

| Verse | Who is called to bear witness | Who calls |
|---|---|---|
| **52** | **God** | The disciples |
| **64** | **The other side** | The believers |
| **81** | **The prophets** | **God** |

**Three occurrences, in a single sūra.** This is a repetition verifiable within the sūra.

**There is a disagreement in the grammar of the verse, and it is not to be concealed:**

| Reading | How *lamā ātaytukum* is read |
|---|---|
| 1 | *Lamā* — "whatever I have given you" (the relative *mā*, with the *lām* of emphasis before it) |
| 2 | *Limā* — "because of My giving" (the *lām* of causation) |

**Both are transmitted, and different recitations are found in the variant readings; I impose no preference and give no reciter's name, because I cannot give one with certainty.**

---

## 3/83-85 — أَفَغَيْرَ دِينِ ٱللَّهِ يَبْغُونَ … وَمَن يَبْتَغِ غَيْرَ ٱلْإِسْلَٰمِ دِينًا فَلَن يُقْبَلَ مِنْهُ

*A-fa-ghayra dīni'llāhi yabghūna wa lahū aslama man fi's-samāwāti wa'l-arḍi ṭawʿan wa karhan wa ilayhi yurjaʿūn · Qul āmannā bi'llāhi wa mā unzila ʿalaynā wa mā unzila ʿalā Ibrāhīma wa Ismāʿīla wa Isḥāqa wa Yaʿqūba wa'l-asbāṭi wa mā ūtiya Mūsā wa ʿĪsā wa'n-nabiyyūna min rabbihim, lā nufarriqu bayna aḥadin minhum wa naḥnu lahū muslimūn · Wa man yabtaghi ghayra'l-islāmi dīnan fa-lan yuqbala minh, wa huwa fi'l-ākhirati mina'l-khāsirīn*

"**Is it other than the religion of God that they seek?** Yet whoever is in the heavens and the earth **has handed himself over to Him, willingly or unwillingly**, and to Him they will be returned. · Say: '**We believe in God, and in what was sent down to us, and in what was sent down to Abraham, Ishmael (Ismāʿīl), Isaac (Isḥāq), Jacob and the descendants, and in what was given to Moses, to Jesus and to the prophets from their Lord. We make no distinction between any of them, and we have handed ourselves over to Him.**' · **Whoever seeks a religion other than islām, it will never be accepted of him, and in the hereafter he will be among the losers.**"

---

**The eighty-fifth verse, together with verse 19, is one of the two most-quoted and most misused verses of the sūra. I follow here the same procedure I followed at verse 19: first the root, then where it was treated in the series, then what the verse does not say.**

### إِسْلَٰم — root: س-ل-م

**The concrete sense of the root was analysed at 49/14 in `049-hucurat.md` and was quoted at verse 19 of this sūra. I do not repeat it; I summarise:**

> **س-ل-م** — **to be sound, to be flawless, to be complete.** *Salāma*, *salām*, *silm*, *taslīm* are from the same root. **At the centre of the root there is an operation: to hand a thing over to the other side in sound condition.** *Islām* is in Form IV: **to hand oneself over.**

**And I rely on the distinction of *īmān* and *islām* at 49/14 and do not repeat it.** The summary of the table there: *īmān* conveys what is in the heart, *islām* what is outward; and there the refusal was read not as **an exclusion but as a correction of place**.

### The context of the verse: 85 cannot be read without 83 and 84

**And this is the most important note of this section. The eighty-fifth verse is the last of a sequence of three verses, and the two before it show how the word *islām* is used in this context:**

| Verse | Wording | In what sense *islām* / *aslama* is used |
|---|---|---|
| **83** | *Wa lahū **aslama** man fi's-samāwāti wa'l-arḍ* | **Of all that exists** — the state of having handed oneself over |
| **84** | *Wa naḥnu lahū **muslimūn*** | **The speaker's own position** — a declaration |
| **85** | *Wa man yabtaghi ghayra'l-**islāmi** dīnan* | **A measure** |

**The same root in three verses, on three different scales.** This is a fact verifiable from the text.

**And the eighty-fourth verse, immediately before the eighty-fifth, gives the list of those believed in — and the list contains eight separate names and a summing "the prophets".** **That is, immediately before the word "islām", the extent of that word is shown by a list of names.**

***Lā nufarriqu bayna aḥadin minhum*** — "**we make no distinction between any of them**". **And this sentence stands at 3/84, one verse before 85.**

**I record this as my own reading, and its ground is the order of the two verses:** the sentence that commands making no distinction and the sentence that says "no other will be accepted" **stand side by side.** **And this order shows that the second sentence cannot be read so as to cancel the first.**

### What the verse says and what it does not say

**Per the method (`USLUP.md`) I record this plainly — these are limits that come out of the wording of the verse, not a softening brought in from outside:**

**One. The verse sets a *measure*; it does not give a *list of persons*.** No community, people or group is named in the sentence. The subject is *man* — "whoever". **And the predicate is *yabtaghi* — "seeks": a verb.**

**Two. The choice of the verb must be recorded.** *Ibtighāʾ* (**ب-غ-ي**, Form VIII) — **to seek, to want, to go after.** **That is, the verse takes as its subject not a *state* but a *seeking*.** The difference between not knowing a thing and knowing it while seeking another lies in the verb itself.

**Three. The root sense of the word blocks any reduction of the verse to a historical-institutional name.** The preceding verse (83) uses *aslama* of **everything in the heavens and the earth**. **In the verse's own context the word points not to an institution but to a state.**

**Four. This commentary does not enter the debate about excommunication.** Who is inside and who is outside this measure is a ruling the verse itself does not make, and it will not be made here. **The verse gives a measure; applying the measure to persons is not the work of this commentary.** **This limit was set at verse 19 and is kept here exactly.**

**Five. The sūra itself enters a note on this matter, and that note is at verses 113 and 199.** Both plainly count the faith and the deeds of a part of the People of the Book, and verse 199 says *ulāʾika lahum ajruhum ʿinda rabbihim*. **In this commentary verse 85 is read together with this note the sūra itself sets down.**

### طَوْعًا وَكَرْهًا — the pair in verse 83

**ط-و-ع** — willingly. **ك-ر-ه** — unwillingly, under compulsion.

**And the construction comes as a *ḥāl*: the handing over takes place in both ways.**

**The lexicographers differ over the extent of this pair, and the disagreement is not to be concealed:**

| Reading | What is meant |
|---|---|
| 1 | **All that exists being subject to laws** — an involuntary submission that covers everyone |
| 2 | **Believers willingly, the rest by necessity** |
| 3 | Everyone's turning to Him at a moment of distress |

**All three are transmitted; I do not impose a preference.**

**And I record an observation about the word order:** before offering a human being any choice, the verse describes **the field in which there is no choice.** *Man fi's-samāwāti wa'l-arḍ* — the extent is the widest possible. **That is, the handing over itself is not a choice; what is a choice is whether it is done *knowingly*.**

**And the same idea was treated at 22/18 in `022-hac.md` with the word for prostration** (*a-lam tara anna'llāha yasjudu lahū man fi's-samāwāti wa man fi'l-arḍ*). **The two sūras state the same extent with two different verbs: one *prostration*, the other *islām*.** This is a weave verifiable within the series.

---

## 3/86-91 — كَيْفَ يَهْدِى ٱللَّهُ قَوْمًا كَفَرُوا۟ بَعْدَ إِيمَٰنِهِمْ … إِلَّا ٱلَّذِينَ تَابُوا۟ مِنۢ بَعْدِ ذَٰلِكَ وَأَصْلَحُوا۟

*Kayfa yahdi'llāhu qawman kafarū baʿda īmānihim wa shahidū anna'r-rasūla ḥaqqun wa jāʾahumu'l-bayyināt, wa'llāhu lā yahdi'l-qawma'ẓ-ẓālimīn · … · Illa'lladhīna tābū min baʿdi dhālika wa aṣlaḥū fa-inna'llāha ghafūrun raḥīm*

"**How shall God guide a people who disbelieved after they had believed, after they had borne witness that the messenger is true, and after the clear proofs had come to them?** God does not guide a wrongdoing people. · … · **Except those who turn back after that and set things right: God is much-forgiving, most merciful.**"

### The building of the sentence: three conditions, then a question

**The eighty-sixth verse does not set the ruling down directly; it opens with a question: *kayfa* — "how?"** And it is a question that carries its answer within it (in Arabic, *istifhām inkārī*).

**And three notes are set before the question, and all three convey a *prior state*:**

| # | Note | What it conveys |
|---|---|---|
| **1** | *Baʿda **īmānihim*** | **They had believed** |
| **2** | *Wa **shahidū** anna'r-rasūla ḥaqq* | **They had borne witness** |
| **3** | *Wa **jāʾahumu'l-bayyināt*** | **The proofs had come** |

**Three notes, and all three are a repetition of the sūra's main note: something happens *after knowledge has come*.** **The same note is at verses 19, 61 and 105 as well.** This is a repetition verifiable within the sūra.

### The position of the exception — verse 89

**And after a sequence of heavy rulings running three verses, verse 89 comes: *illa'lladhīna tābū min baʿdi dhālika wa aṣlaḥū*.**

**And the exception has two conditions, and the two are distinct:**

| Condition | Verb | Where |
|---|---|---|
| **1** | *Tābū* — **they turned back** | **Within** |
| **2** | *Aṣlaḥū* — **they set things right** | **Without** |

**I record this as an observation about the word order:** repentance alone is not counted sufficient; **repair** is added beside it. **And the same pair occurred at 42/40 in `042-sura.md` as well** (*fa-man ʿafā **wa aṣlaḥa***) — there for the one who forgives, here for the one who turns back. **Two sūras apply the same pair to two different subjects.**

**And the difference in the ninetieth verse must be recorded:** *thumma'zdādū kufrā* — "**then they increased in disbelief**". **What is excluded from the exception is not the one who does not turn back but the one who *increases* in the opposite direction.** **That is, the axis of the ruling is not an event but a *direction*.**

**مِلْءُ ٱلْأَرْضِ ذَهَبًا** (91) — *milʾu'l-arḍi dhahabā*: "the earth's fill of gold". **And this lies along the same line as *al-qanāṭīru'l-muqanṭara* in verse 14:** the sūra names the largest of hoarded amounts twice, and both times says that it **is not enough**.

---

## 3/92 — لَن تَنَالُوا۟ ٱلْبِرَّ حَتَّىٰ تُنفِقُوا۟ مِمَّا تُحِبُّونَ

*Lan tanālu'l-birra ḥattā tunfiqū mimmā tuḥibbūn, wa mā tunfiqū min shayʾin fa-inna'llāha bihī ʿalīm*

"**You will not attain *birr* until you spend of what you love.** And whatever you spend, God knows it."

---

**This verse is the counterpart in Āl ʿImrān of al-Baqara's definition of *birr*, and it is not fully understood unless the two sūras are read together.**

### ٱلْبِرّ — on the basis of al-Baqara

***Al-birr* was analysed in detail at 2/177 in `002-bakara.md`, and the concrete sense of the root was given at 2/44. I rely on that treatment and do not repeat it here.** The summary recorded there:

> The picture of the root **ب-ر-ر** is **breadth** — from the same root *barr*: dry land, open wide country. And 2/177 defines *birr* by a list: faith, wealth, worship, character. **Within the definition the block on wealth is the longest, and it comes before worship.**

**And within the definition in al-Baqara there was a note, and the whole of this verse is that note unfolded:**

> *Wa āta'l-māla **ʿalā ḥubbihī*** — "he gave wealth **while loving it**."

**I set the two verses side by side:**

| | **al-Baqara 2/177** | **Āl ʿImrān 3/92** |
|---|---|---|
| **Kind of sentence** | **A definition** — *laysa'l-birra… wa lākinna'l-birra man āmana* | **A condition** — *lan tanālū… ḥattā* |
| **The note of love** | *ʿAlā **ḥubbihī*** — a circumstantial note, inside the list | *Mimmā **tuḥibbūn*** — **the condition itself** |
| **Extent** | A long list: faith, wealth, prayer, alms, covenant, patience | **A single item: spending** |
| **The one addressed** | Third person (*man āmana*) | **Second person plural** (*tanālū*, *tunfiqū*) |
| **A cutting off** | None | ***Lan*** — absolute negation |

**And the difference must be recorded: al-Baqara gives a definition, Āl ʿImrān turns one item of that definition into a *threshold*.**

**I record this as my own reading, and its ground is the kind of the two sentences:** in al-Baqara "giving what you love" is **an element inside** the definition; here it is **the condition of entry** into the definition. **The same element, in one sūra a part, in the other a door.**

### لَن … حَتَّىٰ — the finality of the word order

**Both particles of the sentence are strong:**

- **لَن** — the particle that in Arabic puts the imperfect in the subjunctive and conveys **an absolute negation regarding the future**. "You will never attain."
- **حَتَّىٰ** — the particle of purpose: up to a limit. **Here it conveys a condition.**

**That is, the sentence sets not a recommendation but a *threshold*.**

**نَالَ — root ن-و-ل**: **to stretch out the hand and take, to reach, to obtain.** **The root was treated at 33/25 in `033-ahzab.md` (*lam yanālū khayrā*) and at 22/37 in `022-hac.md`. I rely on those treatments.**

**And the occurrence at 22/37 in `022-hac.md` connects directly here, and the two verses should be compared:**

| Place | Sentence | What is not reached / is reached |
|---|---|---|
| **al-Ḥajj 22/37** | *Lan **yanāla**'llāha luḥūmuhā wa lā dimāʾuhā wa lākin **yanāluhu't-taqwā** minkum* | Not the flesh of the offering but **taqwā** reaches |
| **Āl ʿImrān 3/92** | *Lan **tanālu**'l-birra ḥattā tunfiqū mimmā tuḥibbūn* | The condition of reaching **birr**: giving **what is loved** |

**The same verb, the same particle of negation (*lan*), in two different sūras.** **And in both the measure is taken out of material quantity and tied to intention or attitude.** This is a weave verifiable within the series.

### مِمَّا تُحِبُّونَ — the work of *min*

**And the particle itself sets a limit, and it is usually passed over: *min* here is the particle of *tabʿīḍ* — "of some of what you love".**

**The verse does not say "give all of what you love".**

**I record this as an observation about the word order, and its ground is the particle itself:** the measure is not the amount given but **the kind of what is given.** The whole of something unloved may be given and the condition is not met; a part of something loved is given and the condition is met.

**And `002-bakara.md` 2/267 is the other half of this verse, and I rely on it:**

> *Yā ayyuha'lladhīna āmanū anfiqū min ṭayyibāti mā kasabtum… wa lā tayammamu'l-khabītha minhu tunfiqūn* — "**Spend of the good things you have earned; and do not pick out the bad of it to give.**"

**The two verses look at the same matter from two directions, and a table can be drawn:**

| | **al-Baqara 2/267** | **Āl ʿImrān 3/92** |
|---|---|---|
| **Mood** | **A command** — *anfiqū* | **A condition** — *lan tanālū… ḥattā* |
| **The measure** | *Ṭayyibāt* — **the quality of what is given** | *Mimmā tuḥibbūn* — **the giver's bond with it** |
| **The prohibition** | *Lā tayammamu'l-khabīth* — do not **pick out** the bad | (No prohibition; a threshold) |
| **Point of view** | What **the receiver** will see | What **the giver** will feel |

**I record this as my own reading, and it is not binding:** the two verses together show that spending is measured from two separate places. Al-Baqara looks at the thing given **itself**; Āl ʿImrān at the giver's **relation** to that thing. **And the second measure is the side of the first that cannot be checked from outside** — and indeed it is for just this reason that the verse ends with *fa-inna'llāha bihī ʿalīm*.

### What this says to the present

**The measure the verse sets is one that can be applied directly today as well, and it can be reduced to a single question:** was the thing given **the thing that was easy to give**?

When a person gives away something he wanted off his hands, he does two things at once: he is rid of a burden and it counts as giving. **The verse's threshold cuts through exactly this doubleness.**

**And the verse names no amount.** The condition is not quantity but **bond**. **This makes the measure work for everyone according to his own situation: the same amount crosses the threshold for one person and not for another.**

---

## 3/93-95 — كُلُّ ٱلطَّعَامِ كَانَ حِلًّا لِّبَنِىٓ إِسْرَٰٓءِيلَ إِلَّا مَا حَرَّمَ إِسْرَٰٓءِيلُ عَلَىٰ نَفْسِهِۦ

*Kullu'ṭ-ṭaʿāmi kāna ḥillan li-banī Isrāʾīla illā mā ḥarrama Isrāʾīlu ʿalā nafsihī min qabli an tunazzala't-Tawrāt, qul fa'tū bi't-Tawrāti fa'tlūhā in kuntum ṣādiqīn · Fa-mani'ftarā ʿala'llāhi'l-kadhiba min baʿdi dhālika fa-ulāʾika humu'ẓ-ẓālimūn · Qul ṣadaqa'llāh, fa'ttabiʿū millata Ibrāhīma ḥanīfā, wa mā kāna mina'l-mushrikīn*

"**Before the Torah was sent down, all food was lawful to the Children of Israel, except what Israel forbade to himself.** Say: '**Bring the Torah and recite it, if you are truthful.**' · **Whoever invents a lie against God after this — those are the wrongdoers.** · Say: '**God has spoken truly. So follow the way of Abraham, as a *ḥanīf*; and he was not of the *mushrikūn*.**'"

### The proof again built upon time

**And this is the second application of the proof in verse 65, and it is in the same pattern:**

| Verse | The proof | The note of time |
|---|---|---|
| **65** | Abraham lived **before** the two books | *Illā **min baʿdih*** |
| **93** | The prohibition was laid down **before** the Torah | ***Min qabli** an tunazzala't-Tawrāt* |

**Two verses, within the same block, applying the same reasoning to two different subjects: when a thing came into being limits the claim ascribed to it.** This is a weave verifiable within the sūra.

### فَأْتُوا۟ بِٱلتَّوْرَىٰةِ فَٱتْلُوهَآ — the form of the call

**And the dispute is referred to a text: "bring it and recite it".**

**I record this as an observation and give it by way of the word order:** the sentence uses the other side's book not by **rejecting** it but by **calling for** it. **And this is consistent with the third verse of the sūra** — there it was recorded that the Torah and the Gospel had been sent down.

**And the note *in kuntum ṣādiqīn*: the offer of verification is left open to the other side.**

**Per the method (`USLUP.md`) I draw a boundary:** what the particular prohibition the verse discusses was is transmitted in various forms in the classical sources. **I do not go into the detail of those reports in this commentary and I make no preference**, because the verse does not name the matter — it states only **its time**.

### مِلَّة — root: م-ل-ل

The root **م-ل-ل**. The lexicographers explain *milla* as **a way kept to, a manner followed**; and they record the word's connection with **dictation** — *amlā*: to dictate. **That is, *milla* means a way dictated, a way laid out.**

**And it must be recorded that in the Qurʾān the word conveys not a community but a *way*.** *Millata Ibrāhīm* — Abraham's **way**, not Abraham's **people**. **The word was treated at 2/130-135 in `002-bakara.md`; I rely on that treatment.**

**And the Turkish word *millet* comes from this root, but its sense has drifted: in Turkish it is the name of a *community*, in Arabic it is the name of a *way*.** **I record this as a note about language, and I draw no sense of "a people" from the verse.**

---

## 3/96-97 — إِنَّ أَوَّلَ بَيْتٍ وُضِعَ لِلنَّاسِ لَلَّذِى بِبَكَّةَ مُبَارَكًا وَهُدًى لِّلْعَٰلَمِينَ

*Inna awwala baytin wuḍiʿa li'n-nāsi la'lladhī bi-Bakkata mubārakan wa hudan li'l-ʿālamīn · Fīhi āyātun bayyinātun maqāmu Ibrāhīm, wa man dakhalahū kāna āminā, wa li'llāhi ʿala'n-nāsi ḥijju'l-bayti mani'staṭāʿa ilayhi sabīlā, wa man kafara fa-inna'llāha ghaniyyun ʿani'l-ʿālamīn*

"**The first House established for people is the one at Bakka: blessed, and a guidance for all beings.** · **In it are clear signs, and the standing-place of Abraham. Whoever enters it is safe.** And **the pilgrimage to the House is a duty owed to God by those people who can find a way to it.** Whoever denies — God has no need of any of the worlds."

---

**Per the method (`USLUP.md`) I draw a boundary here and shall keep it throughout the section: the detail of the rulings of pilgrimage was treated at 2/196-203 in `002-bakara.md` and at 22/26-33 in `022-hac.md`. I rely on those treatments, do not repeat them, and *issue no legal ruling.* Only what these two verses add will be recorded here.**

### أَوَّلَ بَيْتٍ وُضِعَ لِلنَّاسِ — word order

**Every word of the sentence carries a note:**

| Word | The note |
|---|---|
| ***Awwal*** | **First** — a claim of priority |
| ***Bayt*** — indefinite | **A house** — not a particular structure but a kind |
| ***Wuḍiʿa*** — passive | **Was established** — the one who established it is not named |
| ***Li'n-nās*** | **For people** — not for a community |

**And the note *li'n-nās* is the most important word of this section. I record this as an observation about the word order:** the sentence does not say "for us" or "for such and such a community". **And this bears directly on the subject of the third block** — a dispute about belonging had been running throughout the block.

**And the same breadth comes once more at the end of the verse: *hudan li'l-ʿālamīn*.** **Two notes, in a single verse: *li'n-nās* and *li'l-ʿālamīn*.**

**I record this as my own reading, and it is not binding:** in the middle of a dispute about belonging that runs by way of a place, the verse cuts the dispute short by saying **for whom the place was set down.** **The claim of priority is accepted (*awwal*), but no claim of ownership is set up.**

### بَكَّة — the word itself

**The lexicographers differ over the word's relation to *Makka*, and the disagreement is not to be concealed:**

| Reading | What *Bakka* is |
|---|---|
| 1 | **Another name for Mecca** — the interchange of *mīm* and *bāʾ* (a known sound-change in Arabic) |
| 2 | **A particular place within Mecca** — the area where the House stands |
| 3 | From the root **ب-ك-ك**: *bakka* — to press together, a throng; the place where a crowd is packed |

**All three are transmitted; I do not impose a preference.**

**The third reading is worth recording as an analysis of the root: ب-ك-ك — to crush a thing, to press it, a throng.** **This is the concrete sense of the word; I make no claim that the verse means that sense.**

### فِيهِ ءَايَٰتٌۢ بَيِّنَٰتٌ مَّقَامُ إِبْرَٰهِيمَ

**And there is a nicety in the word order, and the lexicographers dwell on it: *āyātun* is plural, and the *maqāmu Ibrāhīm* that follows it is singular.**

| Reading | The relation |
|---|---|
| 1 | *Maqāmu Ibrāhīm* is **one instance** of the plural (a partitive substitute) |
| 2 | It is **an explanation of the whole** of the plural (explicative apposition) |
| 3 | The plural covers all the signs of the place; one of them has been named separately |

**All three are transmitted; I do not impose a preference.**

**مَقَام** — root **ق-و-م**: **the place where one stands.** **Its link with the other occurrences in the sūra must be recorded:** *qāʾiman bi'l-qisṭ* (18), *qāʾimā* (75), *ummatun qāʾima* (113). **The same root in four separate places, at four separate pieces of work.**

### وَمَن دَخَلَهُۥ كَانَ ءَامِنًا — the kind of the sentence

**And there is a divergence over whether this sentence is a statement or a performative, and it is not to be concealed:**

| Reading | What the sentence does |
|---|---|
| 1 | **A statement** — it reports a fact: that place has been made safe |
| 2 | **A performative / in the sense of a command** — "let him be safe", that is, whoever enters is not to be touched |

**Both are transmitted; I do not impose a preference and I draw no legal ruling from this.**

**And at 2/125-126 in `002-bakara.md` the note of *amn* was treated for the same place** (*wa idh jaʿalna'l-bayta mathābatan li'n-nāsi wa amnā*; *rabbi'jʿal hādhā baladan āminā*). **I rely on that treatment.**

### وَلِلَّهِ عَلَى ٱلنَّاسِ حِجُّ ٱلْبَيْتِ — the word order of the obligation

**And the building of the sentence must be recorded, because this pattern is rare in the Qurʾān:**

*Wa **li'llāhi** ʿala'n-nāsi ḥijju'l-bayt* — "**To God, upon people, is the pilgrimage to the House.**"

| Element | Wording | Its work |
|---|---|---|
| **Whose right** | *Li'llāh* — **brought forward** | The one to whom the obligation is owed |
| **Upon whom** | *ʿAla'n-nās* | **People** — again the wide set |
| **What** | *Ḥijju'l-bayt* | The obligation itself |
| **The caveat** | *Mani'staṭāʿa ilayhi sabīlā* | **The condition of ability** |

**And the coming of *li'llāh* and *ʿala'n-nās* before the noun conveys in Arabic a restriction.** **That is, the sentence sets up a relation of debt: the creditor and the debtor are named.**

**اِسْتَطَاعَ** — root **ط-و-ع** (Form X): **to be able.** **And the same root occurred at verse 83 as *ṭawʿan*.** **The same root, one conveying willing submission, the other being able.**

**Per the method (`USLUP.md`) I record this: the extent of *istiṭāʿa* (safety of the road, financial condition, bodily health, travel with a close relative) has been discussed in detail in classical law, and there are differences among the schools. That discussion is not entered in this commentary and no view is preferred.** The only thing recorded is that the verse ties the obligation **to a condition.**

**وَمَن كَفَرَ فَإِنَّ ٱللَّهَ غَنِىٌّ عَنِ ٱلْعَٰلَمِينَ** — **and the verse closes by saying that the one who lays down the obligation expects no benefit from it.** **The same idea was set up for the offering at 22/37 in `022-hac.md`** (*lan yanāla'llāha luḥūmuhā wa lā dimāʾuhā*). **The two sūras enter the same note for two different acts of worship.**

---

## 3/98-101 — قُلْ يَٰٓأَهْلَ ٱلْكِتَٰبِ لِمَ تَكْفُرُونَ بِـَٔايَٰتِ ٱللَّهِ … وَمَن يَعْتَصِم بِٱللَّهِ فَقَدْ هُدِىَ إِلَىٰ صِرَٰطٍ مُّسْتَقِيمٍ

*Qul yā ahla'l-kitābi lima takfurūna bi-āyāti'llāhi wa'llāhu shahīdun ʿalā mā taʿmalūn · Qul yā ahla'l-kitābi lima taṣuddūna ʿan sabīli'llāhi man āmana tabghūnahā ʿiwajan wa antum shuhadāʾ, wa ma'llāhu bi-ghāfilin ʿammā taʿmalūn · Yā ayyuha'lladhīna āmanū in tuṭīʿū farīqan mina'lladhīna ūtu'l-kitāba yaruddūkum baʿda īmānikum kāfirīn · Wa kayfa takfurūna wa antum tutlā ʿalaykum āyātu'llāhi wa fīkum rasūluh, wa man yaʿtaṣim bi'llāhi fa-qad hudiya ilā ṣirāṭin mustaqīm*

"Say: '**People of the Book! Why do you deny the signs of God? God is witness to what you do.**' · Say: '**People of the Book! Why do you turn the believer away from the path of God, seeking to make it crooked — and you yourselves witnesses?**' … · **You who believe! If you obey a group of those who were given the Book, they will turn you back to denial after your believing.** · **How can you deny — when the signs of God are recited to you and His messenger is among you?** Whoever holds fast to God has been guided to a straight path."

### The close of the block and the change of addressee

**The ninety-ninth verse closes the third block; with the hundredth the addressee changes: instead of *yā ahla'l-kitāb*, *yā ayyuha'lladhīna āmanū*.**

**And this transition was recorded in the structure of the sūra (the table of blocks in the introduction). Here the *form* of the transition must be recorded:**

| Verse | Address | Subject |
|---|---|---|
| **98, 99** | *Yā ahla'l-kitāb* | **Outward** — denial and obstruction |
| **100** | *Yā ayyuha'lladhīna āmanū* | **Inward** — the danger of following |
| **101** | *Wa kayfa takfurūn* | **Inward** — the reminder of one's own state |

**And in the hundredth verse the caveat is entered again: *farīqan mina'lladhīna ūtu'l-kitāb*** — "**a group**". **The same particle *min* for the fifth time.** This is a repetition verifiable within the sūra.

### تَبْغُونَهَا عِوَجًا — root: ع-و-ج

The root **ع-و-ج**: **crookedness.** The lexicographers record a distinction: ***ʿiwaj*** (with *i*) for **crookedness in abstract things** — speech, an affair, a religion; ***ʿawaj*** (with *a*) for **crookedness in visible things** — a wall, a staff.

And the form used in the verse is *ʿiwajan*: that is, what they want to bend is not a visible object but **a way** — *sabīlu'llāh*.

**I record this as an observation about the word and rely on the lexicographers' distinction; I make no claim that the choice of word was deliberate.**

**And the verb must be recorded: *tabghūnahā* — root ب-غ-ي, "to want, to seek".** **The same root occurred at verse 19 as *baghyan baynahum***, and at verse 83 as *yabghūn*, at verse 85 as *yabtaghi*. **This root in four separate places in the sūra, at four separate pieces of work.**

### وَمَن يَعْتَصِم بِٱللَّهِ — a bridge to verse 103

**And the hundred and first verse gives in advance the key verb of the next block: *yaʿtaṣim*.**

The root **ع-ص-م**: **to hold on, to cling fast; and from this, to protect.** *ʿIṣma* — being kept safe. *Maʿṣūm* — the one kept safe. **And the concrete usage the lexicographers record: *ʿiṣām* — the cord that ties the mouth of a waterskin.**

**The root was treated at 22/78 in `022-hac.md`, at 33/17 in `033-ahzab.md`, at 60/10 in `060-mumtehine.md` and at 11/43 in `011-hud.md`. I rely on those treatments.**

**And what belongs here is this, and it is a fact of word order: the same verb comes twice, two verses apart, and its objects differ:**

| Verse | Verb | What is held on to |
|---|---|---|
| **101** | *Wa man **yaʿtaṣim bi'llāh*** | ***To God*** — directly |
| **103** | *Wa'**taṣimū bi-ḥabli'llāh*** | ***To the rope of God*** — by way of something between |

**Two verses, one root, two different objects. And the first is singular (*man*), the second plural (*iʿtaṣimū*), and to the second *jamīʿan* has been added.**

**I record this as my own reading, and its ground is the word order of the two verses:** the first verse describes the holding fast of **a single person**, the second the holding fast of **a community together**. **And in the second an object comes in between: *ḥabl*.** **That is, one person holds on directly; a community holds on to something shared.**

---

## 3/102-103 — يَٰٓأَيُّهَا ٱلَّذِينَ ءَامَنُوا۟ ٱتَّقُوا۟ ٱللَّهَ حَقَّ تُقَاتِهِۦ · وَٱعْتَصِمُوا۟ بِحَبْلِ ٱللَّهِ جَمِيعًا وَلَا تَفَرَّقُوا۟

*Yā ayyuha'lladhīna āmanu'ttaqu'llāha ḥaqqa tuqātihī wa lā tamūtunna illā wa antum muslimūn · Wa'taṣimū bi-ḥabli'llāhi jamīʿan wa lā tafarraqū, wa'dhkurū niʿmata'llāhi ʿalaykum idh kuntum aʿdāʾan fa-allafa bayna qulūbikum fa-aṣbaḥtum bi-niʿmatihī ikhwānā, wa kuntum ʿalā shafā ḥufratin mina'n-nāri fa-anqadhakum minhā*

"**You who believe! Guard yourselves before God as guarding is due, and do not die except as those who have handed themselves over.** · **Hold fast, all of you together, to the rope of God, and do not scatter.** Remember the favour of God upon you: **you were enemies, and He knit your hearts together, and by His favour you became brothers.** **You were on the edge of a pit of fire, and He rescued you from it.**"

---

**These two verses open the fourth block, and the subject of the block is single: the inside of the community. I shall treat it in detail.**

### حَقَّ تُقَاتِهِۦ — the construction taken apart

***Taqwā* was analysed in `002-bakara.md` and I do not repeat it here.** What belongs here is the construction before the word: ***ḥaqqa tuqātih*.**

**The pattern حَقّ + a genitive** conveys in Arabic the doing of a thing **to the measure it deserves**. **And the same pattern was treated in the series at 22/74 and 22/78 in `022-hac.md`:**

| Place | Construction | About what |
|---|---|---|
| **al-Ḥajj 22/74** | *Mā qadaru'llāha **ḥaqqa qadrih*** | **Estimation** — it conveys a falling short |
| **al-Ḥajj 22/78** | *Wa jāhidū fi'llāhi **ḥaqqa jihādih*** | **Striving** — a command |
| **Āl ʿImrān 3/102** | *Ittaqu'llāha **ḥaqqa tuqātih*** | **Taqwā** — a command |

**Three occurrences, and I rely on `022-hac.md` 22/74-78.**

**And it is recorded in the classical sources that the construction gives rise to a difficulty, and the disagreement is not to be concealed:** is it possible for a human being "to guard himself as guarding is due"?

| Reading | How the construction is understood |
|---|---|
| 1 | **To the utmost of one's power** — according to a person's capacity |
| 2 | **An absolute measure** — and `064-tegabun.md` 64/16 (*fa'ttaqu'llāha ma'staṭaʿtum*) ties this measure to the limit of ability |
| 3 | The construction conveys a **direction**, not a point to be reached |

**All three are transmitted; I do not impose a preference.**

### وَلَا تَمُوتُنَّ إِلَّا وَأَنتُم مُّسْلِمُونَ — a point of word order

**The wording of the sentence carries a difficulty, and the lexicographers dwell on it: death is not a thing that can be commanded or forbidden.**

**And the solution lies in the structure of the sentence: the prohibition is laid not on dying but on the *state* of the dying.** In Arabic this is a prohibition falling on a circumstantial note: what is forbidden is not *to die* but **to die in a state of not having handed oneself over**.

**I record this as an observation about the word order:** the sentence thereby commands a continuance. **Since the time of death is not known, to say "die in that state" is to say "be in that state at every moment".**

### وَٱعْتَصِمُوا۟ بِحَبْلِ ٱللَّهِ — two words

**ٱعْتَصِمُوا۟** — root **ع-ص-م**, Form VIII. **The root was analysed at verse 101; I do not repeat those notes.** What belongs here: **Form VIII (*iftiʿāl*) conveys in Arabic that the act is done *with care and by one's own effort*.** *Iʿtiṣām* — to fasten oneself, to cling.

**حَبْل** — the root **ح-ب-ل**: **rope, bond.** **The concrete sense of the word is a rope, and its derivatives keep this:** *ḥabl* — a rope; *ḥibāla* — a snare-cord; and *ḥubl* — pregnancy (the most concrete form of a bond).

**And the word *ḥabl* will come once more nine verses later in the sūra, this time not in the plural but in a paired construction: *illā bi-ḥablin mina'llāhi wa ḥablin mina'n-nās* (112).** **Two occurrences, in a single sūra.**

| Verse | *Ḥabl* | Whose rope |
|---|---|---|
| **103** | *Ḥabl**i'llāh*** — **singular, definite** | **The rope of God** — the one to be held |
| **112** | *Ḥablin mina'llāh* **and** *ḥablin mina'n-nās* | **Two ropes** — bonds that afford protection |

**This is a repetition verifiable within the sūra, and both occurrences of the word carry the idea of a *bond*.**

**And there is a divergence in the classical commentaries over what *ḥablu'llāh* is, and it is not to be concealed:**

| Reading | What *ḥablu'llāh* is |
|---|---|
| 1 | **The Qurʾān** |
| 2 | **The covenant of God** |
| 3 | **Religion, the unity of the community** |
| 4 | **Obedience** |

**All four are transmitted; I do not impose a preference.**

**But the verse's own word order gives an indication, and I record this as an indication, not as a preference:** immediately after the command comes *wa lā tafarraqū*. **That is, the holding fast stands in the same sentence as the not scattering.** And the note *jamīʿan* makes the subject of the command plural. **Whatever the thing held on to may be, the verse names it as something that holds people together.**

### A comparison with 2/256: ٱلْعُرْوَةِ ٱلْوُثْقَىٰ

**At 2/256 in `002-bakara.md` *al-ʿurwa al-wuthqā* was treated** (*fa-qadi'stamsaka bi'l-**ʿurwati'l-wuthqā** lā-nfiṣāma lahā*) **and the same family of verbs was there too: *istamsaka* — he took hold.** **I rely on that treatment and compare the two verses:**

| | **al-Baqara 2/256** | **Āl ʿImrān 3/103** |
|---|---|---|
| **What is held** | ***Al-ʿurwa al-wuthqā*** — the firm handle | ***Ḥablu'llāh*** — the rope of God |
| **The picture of the object** | **A handle** — the grip of a vessel or a load | **A rope** — something that extends, held out from a distance |
| **The verb** | *Istamsaka* (**م-س-ك**) — to hold, to keep in hand | *Iʿtaṣama* (**ع-ص-م**) — to be protected by holding on |
| **The subject** | **Singular** — *man yakfur bi'ṭ-ṭāghūti wa yuʾmin bi'llāh* | **Plural** — *iʿtaṣimū… **jamīʿan*** |
| **The added note** | *Lā-nfiṣāma **lahā*** — **it does not break** (a quality of the object) | *Wa lā **tafarraqū*** — **do not scatter** (an obligation on the subject) |
| **Context** | **There is no compulsion in religion** — a person's choice | **The unity of the community** |

**I draw three observations from the table, and all three can be verified from the two texts.**

**One. The pictures of the two objects differ, and the difference does work.** An *ʿurwa* is a **handle**: it is joined to the object, it is close, it is held with one hand. A *ḥabl* is a **rope**: it extends, it is held out from a distance, and **more than one hand can hold the same rope.**

**I record this as my own reading, and its ground is the concrete sense of the two words:** in al-Baqara the one holding on is singular and the object is chosen to fit one hand; in Āl ʿImrān the ones holding on are plural and the object is chosen to fit many hands. **The two verses give the same idea on two different scales.**

**Two. Firmness is placed in two different places in the two verses.** In al-Baqara firmness is a quality of **the object**: *lā-nfiṣāma lahā* — it does not break. In Āl ʿImrān nothing is said about the object; **the obligation is laid on the subject:** *wa lā tafarraqū*. **That is, the rope does not break; what breaks is those who let go of it.**

**Three. Both verses set the holding fast after a *choice*.** In al-Baqara there is the condition *man yakfur bi'ṭ-ṭāghūti wa yuʾmin bi'llāh*; in Āl ʿImrān the command is directed to those who believe (*yā ayyuha'lladhīna āmanū*).

### إِذْ كُنتُمْ أَعْدَآءً فَأَلَّفَ بَيْنَ قُلُوبِكُمْ

**ألف** — root **أ-ل-ف**: **to unite, to be knit together, to grow accustomed.** *Ulfa* — cohesion. *Alf* — a thousand (a gathered multitude). **And the core the lexicographers record: separate things coming together and *growing used* to one another.**

And the subject of the verb must be recorded: *fa-**allafa*** — the subject is God. **The uniting is not told as an achievement of the parties.**

**The same verb and the same note occur at 8/63 in `008-enfal.md`** (*law anfaqta mā fi'l-arḍi jamīʿan mā **allafta** bayna qulūbihim wa lākinna'llāha **allafa** baynahum*) **and there it is said plainly: it is a work that cannot be done by spending wealth.** **I rely on that treatment.**

| Place | Sentence | What it adds |
|---|---|---|
| **al-Anfāl 8/63** | *Mā allafta bayna qulūbihim* | It is recorded as **what a human being cannot do** |
| **Āl ʿImrān 3/103** | *Fa-allafa bayna qulūbikum* | **What has happened** is recalled |

**The two verses use the same verb at two different pieces of work: one an impossibility, the other something accomplished.** This is a weave verifiable within the series.

### عَلَىٰ شَفَا حُفْرَةٍ مِّنَ ٱلنَّارِ — the description itself

**شَفَا** — root **ش-ف-و/ي**: **the edge of a thing, its brink, its threshold.** *Shafa* — lip (the edge of the mouth) is from the same root. **And the lexicographers explain *shafā* as "the point nearest to falling".**

**حُفْرَة** — root **ح-ف-ر**: **a dug pit.**

**And the word order of the description must be recorded, and it has three elements:** *ʿalā **shafā** **ḥufratin** mina'n-**nār*** — edge, pit, fire. **Three words set up a measure of distance: the person is not inside the fire but on the edge of the pit.**

**I record this as an observation:** the description tells of a **moment** — the moment at which the fall has not happened but a single step's distance remains. **And the verb of rescue is chosen accordingly: *fa-anqadhakum* (ن-ق-ذ) — to pull out, to rescue. The verb conveys not the taking out of one who has fallen but the pulling back of one who is about to fall.**

**And the standing of the two descriptions in the same verse is a fact of word order:** from enmity to brotherhood, and a moving away from the edge of the pit. **Two separate pictures, two faces of a single favour.**

---

## 3/104-105 — وَلْتَكُن مِّنكُمْ أُمَّةٌ يَدْعُونَ إِلَى ٱلْخَيْرِ · وَلَا تَكُونُوا۟ كَٱلَّذِينَ تَفَرَّقُوا۟ وَٱخْتَلَفُوا۟

*Wa'ltakun minkum ummatun yadʿūna ila'l-khayri wa yaʾmurūna bi'l-maʿrūfi wa yanhawna ʿani'l-munkari wa ulāʾika humu'l-mufliḥūn · Wa lā takūnū ka'lladhīna tafarraqū wa'khtalafū min baʿdi mā jāʾahumu'l-bayyināt, wa ulāʾika lahum ʿadhābun ʿaẓīm*

"**Let there be among you a community calling to the good, enjoining what is right and forbidding what is wrong.** Those are the ones who prosper. · **Do not be like those who split apart and fell into division after the clear proofs had come to them.** For them there is a great punishment."

### مِنكُمْ — the disagreement over the reach of the particle

**The lexicographers differ over the function of the particle *min* in the verse, and the disagreement is not to be concealed:**

| Reading | What *minkum* means | Consequence |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | ***Tabʿīḍ*** — "some of you" | The duty lies on **a group** (read as a collective obligation, *farḍ kifāya*) |
| 2 | ***Bayāniyya*** — "be so, that community being you" | The duty lies on **all of them** |

**Both are transmitted; I do not impose a preference and I draw no legal ruling from this.**

**But one datum of the text can be recorded, and I record it:** the same three attributes will be laid six verses later, at verse 110, on **the whole community** (*taʾmurūna bi'l-maʿrūfi wa tanhawna ʿani'l-munkar*) — and there the particle *min* is absent. **This is the difference of word order between the two verses, and they can also be read as completing each other.**

### The order of the three verbs

| # | Verb | Direction |
|---|---|---|
| **1** | *Yadʿūna ila'l-**khayr*** | **Calling** — positive, the widest |
| **2** | *Yaʾmurūna bi'l-**maʿrūf*** | **Enjoining** — the good that is known |
| **3** | *Yanhawna ʿani'l-**munkar*** | **Forbidding** — what is rejected |

**And the order must be recorded: calling first, forbidding last.**

**I record this as my own reading, and its ground is the order of the verbs:** the list opens with the positive and closes with the negative. **And the first verb (*daʿwa*) carries no compulsion at all — it only calls.**

**مَعْرُوف / مُنكَر** — both words are built on the same logic, and their formation must be recorded:

- **مَعْرُوف** — root **ع-ر-ف**: **known, recognised.** A passive participle.
- **مُنكَر** — root **ن-ك-ر**: **not recognised, found strange.** A passive participle.

**Both are built on the axis of *recognition*, and both are in the passive pattern.** **That is, the good is named "what is known" and the evil "what is found strange".**

**And the three occur three times in the sūra: 104, 110, 114. And the subjects of the three differ:**

| Verse | Subject | Who |
|---|---|---|
| **104** | *Ummatun **minkum*** | **A community from among you** |
| **110** | *Kuntum khayra ummatin* | **The whole of the community addressed** |
| **114** | *Ummatun qāʾima **min ahli'l-kitāb*** | **A community standing upright from among the People of the Book** |

**Three occurrences, three separate subjects — and the third is from the People of the Book.** **This is a fact verifiable within the sūra and settles directly how verse 110 is to be read.** We shall return to it below.

### مِنۢ بَعْدِ مَا جَآءَهُمُ ٱلْبَيِّنَٰتُ — the third occurrence of the note

**This construction was treated at verse 19, and a table of five places was drawn up there.** **I rely on `002-bakara.md` 2/213, `042-sura.md` 42/14 and `045-casiye.md` 45/17 and do not repeat them.**

**There are two notes that belong here:**

**One. The word that comes here is *al-bayyināt*, not *al-ʿilm*.** At verse 19 of the sūra *al-ʿilm* came. **That is, the sūra uses the same pattern twice with two different words.**

| Verse | Pattern | What comes |
|---|---|---|
| **19** | *Min baʿdi mā jāʾahumu'l-**ʿilm*** | **Knowledge** |
| **105** | *Min baʿdi mā jāʾahumu'l-**bayyināt*** | **The clear proofs** |

**Two. In the other occurrences this sentence is a *statement*; here it is *the ground of a prohibition*.** *Wa lā takūnū ka'lladhīna…* — "**do not be like them**". **That is, a sentence said about others is turned into an example for the one addressed.**

**I record this as my own reading, and its ground is the mood of the sentence:** the sūra tells this here not as a piece of history but **as a possibility.** It is assumed that the same thing may happen to the one addressed. **And indeed the sūra will record at verse 152 that something like it did happen.**

**The two occurrences of the root ف-ر-ق in the sūra must be recorded:** *wa lā **tafarraqū*** (103) — *ka'lladhīna **tafarraqū*** (105). **One a prohibition, the other an instance of its breach; two verses apart.** This was recorded in the table in the introduction.

---

## 3/106-109 — يَوْمَ تَبْيَضُّ وُجُوهٌ وَتَسْوَدُّ وُجُوهٌ

*Yawma tabyaḍḍu wujūhun wa taswaddu wujūh, fa-amma'lladhīna'swaddat wujūhuhum a-kafartum baʿda īmānikum fa-dhūqu'l-ʿadhāba bimā kuntum takfurūn · Wa amma'lladhīna'byaḍḍat wujūhuhum fa-fī raḥmati'llāhi hum fīhā khālidūn · Tilka āyātu'llāhi natlūhā ʿalayka bi'l-ḥaqq, wa ma'llāhu yurīdu ẓulman li'l-ʿālamīn · Wa li'llāhi mā fi's-samāwāti wa mā fi'l-arḍ, wa ila'llāhi turjaʿu'l-umūr*

"**On a day when some faces will grow white and some faces will grow black.** … · **These are the signs of God; We recite them to you with the truth. God wills no wrong to any of the worlds.** · Whatever is in the heavens and the earth belongs to God; **and to God all affairs are returned.**"

### Word order: أَمَّا … وَأَمَّا and the reversal of the sequence

**And there is a fact in the word order that must be recorded: in counting the two groups, the verse opens with the faces that grow *black*.**

**Yet in the first half of verse 106 the order is the reverse:** *tabyaḍḍu* (grow white) first, *taswaddu* (grow black) after. **In the second half, on the other hand, *fa-amma'lladhīna'swaddat* comes first.**

| Order | First half (106a) | Second half (106b-107) |
|---|---|---|
| **1** | *Tabyaḍḍu* — **growing white** | *Iswaddat* — **grown black** |
| **2** | *Taswaddu* — **growing black** | *Ibyaḍḍat* — **grown white** |

**This is a chiastic arrangement (*laff wa nashr mushawwash*). And it has a consequence worth recording:** the block ends with **mercy** (*fa-fī raḥmati'llāhi hum fīhā khālidūn*). **That is, the reversal of the sequence settles which sentence will be the section's last.**

**I record this as an observation about the word order; I make no claim about reckonings or numbers.**

**And the sentence *a-kafartum baʿda īmānikum* must be recorded: the same construction occurs at 106 as a question, at 90 as a statement, at 100 as a warning.** **Three occurrences, in a single sūra:**

| Verse | Wording | Mood |
|---|---|---|
| **90** | *Kafarū **baʿda īmānihim*** | **A statement** |
| **100** | *Yaruddūkum **baʿda īmānikum** kāfirīn* | **A warning** |
| **106** | *A-kafartum **baʿda īmānikum*** | **A question** |

**This is a repetition verifiable within the sūra.**

---

## 3/110 — كُنتُمْ خَيْرَ أُمَّةٍ أُخْرِجَتْ لِلنَّاسِ تَأْمُرُونَ بِٱلْمَعْرُوفِ وَتَنْهَوْنَ عَنِ ٱلْمُنكَرِ وَتُؤْمِنُونَ بِٱللَّهِ

*Kuntum khayra ummatin ukhrijat li'n-nāsi taʾmurūna bi'l-maʿrūfi wa tanhawna ʿani'l-munkari wa tuʾminūna bi'llāh, wa law āmana ahlu'l-kitābi la-kāna khayran lahum, minhumu'l-muʾminūna wa aktharuhumu'l-fāsiqūn*

"**You have been the best community brought forth for people: you enjoin what is right, forbid what is wrong, and believe in God.** Had the People of the Book believed, it would have been better for them. **Among them are believers; but most of them have gone out of the way.**"

---

**Per the method (`USLUP.md`) this verse will be treated in this commentary with great care. The reason is plain: it is one of the verses most used to set up a claim of superiority for a community. And the rule of this commentary was laid down at the head of the sūra — no blanket ruling is made about any group. What I shall show below is that this rule is not a sensitivity brought in from outside but the verse's own word order.**

### The structure of the sentence: where in the sentence the attributes stand

**The word order of the verse is this:**

| Element | Wording | Kind |
|---|---|---|
| **1** | *Kuntum **khayra ummatin*** | The ruling |
| **2** | *Ukhrijat **li'n-nās*** | A qualifying clause |
| **3** | *Taʾmurūna bi'l-maʿrūf* | **A verbal sentence** |
| **4** | *Wa tanhawna ʿani'l-munkar* | **A verbal sentence** |
| **5** | *Wa tuʾminūna bi'llāh* | **A verbal sentence** |

**And the real question is the *position in the sentence* of the third, fourth and fifth elements.**

**In Arabic these three verbal sentences following a nominal sentence are analysed in two ways, and the lexicographers divide here. The disagreement is not to be concealed:**

| Reading | What the three verbal sentences are | Consequence |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | ***Ḥāl*** — the **state** in which the ruling is laid down | "You have been the best community — **while being thus**" |
| 2 | **The ground of the ruling** (an explanatory resumption, *istiʾnāf bayānī*) | "You are the best, **because** you do these things" |
| 3 | **An explanation** of *khayra ummatin* | "The best community **is this**: …" |

**All three are transmitted; I do not impose a preference.**

**And what must now be recorded is this, and it is the same on *all three* readings:** on none of the three readings are the attributes **separable** from the ruling. **The ruling is tied to the three verbs** — whether as state, as ground, or as explanation. **That is, the phrase "best community" does not stand alone in the sentence without the three verbs.**

**I record this as an observation about the word order, and it can be verified from the wording of the verse:** there is no reading of the sentence in which the three verbs can be taken out.

### The three attributes themselves

| # | Attribute | Direction |
|---|---|---|
| **1** | *Taʾmurūna bi'l-**maʿrūf*** | **Outward** — to another |
| **2** | *Wa tanhawna ʿani'l-**munkar*** | **Outward** — to another |
| **3** | *Wa tuʾminūna **bi'llāh*** | **Inward** — to oneself |

**And that two of the attributes are *turned towards people* joins with the preceding note (*ukhrijat li'n-nās*).**

**أُخْرِجَتْ لِلنَّاسِ** — "**brought forth for people**". **The verb is passive: the one who brings forth is not named.** And the note *li'n-nās* states **by reference to whom** the community is described.

**I record this as my own reading, and its ground is the two notes (*li'n-nās* and the two outward-facing verbs):** the verse describes not a **position** but a **work**. The community is named as "brought forth" not for itself but for others. **And its quality is measured by two verbs turned towards others.**

### A comparison with 2/143: أُمَّةً وَسَطًا

**At 2/143 in `002-bakara.md` *ummatan wasaṭan* was treated, and I rely on that treatment. What was recorded there:**

> The root **و-س-ط** carries two senses at once: **middle** (between two extremes) and **most valuable, choicest** (*wasaṭu'l-qawm*, the best of a people). **And the task was defined there not as superiority but as *witnessing*:** *shuhadāʾa ʿala'n-nās*. **And this was recorded: "A community's witness is borne not by what it says but by the state in which it is seen."**

**I set the two verses side by side:**

| | **al-Baqara 2/143** | **Āl ʿImrān 3/110** |
|---|---|---|
| **The construction** | *Ummatan **wasaṭan*** | ***Khayra** ummatin* |
| **The verb** | *Jaʿalnākum* — **"We made you"**, the agent stated | *Ukhrijat* — **passive**, the agent not stated |
| **By reference to whom** | *Shuhadāʾa **ʿala'n-nās*** | *Ukhrijat **li'n-nās*** |
| **The kind of task** | **Witnessing** — being seen | **Enjoining and forbidding** — speaking |
| **The note** | *Wa yakūna'r-rasūlu ʿalaykum shahīdā* — **they too are under scrutiny** | The three verbs — **the attributes are inside the sentence** |
| **The tense of the sentence** | *Jaʿalnā* — **perfect**, a thing done | *Kuntum* — **perfect**, and the three verbs **imperfect** |

**And the last row must be opened out separately, because it is the most disputed point of the verse.**

### كُنتُمْ — the work of the perfect verb

***Kuntum* is in the perfect: "you have been / you were". And the three verbs that follow are in the imperfect: *taʾmurūna*, *tanhawna*, *tuʾminūna* — they convey continuance.**

**The lexicographers differ over what the tense conveys, and the disagreement is not to be concealed:**

| Reading | What *kuntum* conveys |
|---|---|
| 1 | ***Kāna* redundant / complete** — in the sense of "you are"; it conveys no time |
| 2 | **An attribute realised in the past** — "you became so" |
| 3 | It has been so decreed **in God's knowledge / in the Preserved Tablet** |
| 4 | **A conditioned ruling** — valid so long as the attributes continue |

**All four are transmitted; I do not impose a preference.**

**But one datum of the text can be recorded, and I record it:** the predicate of the sentence is perfect, and all three of the attributes are imperfect. **In Arabic the imperfect conveys *continuance and renewal*.** That is, the attributes are set up not as a completed event but as **an ongoing work**. **This gives an indication in favour of the fourth reading; but I record it as an indication, not as a preference.**

### Per the method (`USLUP.md`): what the verse does not say

**I now record this plainly. These are limits that come out of the *wording* of the verse:**

**One. No people, race, language or geography is named in the verse.** What is named is a **community**, and the community is described by three verbs. **No superiority for any people or group can be drawn from this verse** — because the verse names no people.

**Two. The ruling cannot be separated from the attributes.** It was shown above on all three readings: the three verbs are load-bearing in the sentence. **To take the ruling without carrying the attributes requires cutting the sentence in two; and the word order does not allow it.**

**Three. The verse's own second half prevents its first half from turning into an instrument of blanket ruling.** The second half is this: *minhumu'l-**muʾminūna** wa aktharuhumu'l-**fāsiqūn***. **That is, a division is made even for the other side** — "among them are believers."

**I record this as an observation about the word order:** even while passing a ruling about a group, the verse divides that group in two. **A blanket ruling is not in the verse's own sentence.**

**Four. And the sūra will enter this note again and plainly three verses later: *laysū sawāʾan* (113).** **In this commentary verse 110 is read together with that note the sūra itself sets down.**

**Five. Present-day politics and claims of superiority are not entered.** This is the rule of `USLUP.md`, and it is recalled here again, because this verse has been used most in that field.

### What this says to the present

**What this verse says to the present runs in exactly the opposite direction from the claim drawn out of it, and this can be shown from the wording of the verse:**

The verse looks like a sentence of **praise**; but every element in it is a **burden**. *Ukhrijat li'n-nās* — to be brought forth for others is not a privilege but a duty. *Taʾmurūna* and *tanhawna* — both verbs cost labour, and both carry risk. *Tuʾminūna bi'llāh* — the third is the support of the other two.

**And a sentence does not stand when all the conditions that carry it have fallen away.**

**I record this as my own reading, and it is not binding:** to read this verse as a sentence of identity requires cutting the sentence right through the middle. **Read whole, the verse is not an attribute but a *description of a work*.**

---

## 3/111-112 — لَن يَضُرُّوكُمْ إِلَّآ أَذًى · ضُرِبَتْ عَلَيْهِمُ ٱلذِّلَّةُ أَيْنَ مَا ثُقِفُوٓا۟ إِلَّا بِحَبْلٍ مِّنَ ٱللَّهِ وَحَبْلٍ مِّنَ ٱلنَّاسِ

*Lan yaḍurrūkum illā adhā, wa in yuqātilūkum yuwallūkumu'l-adbāra thumma lā yunṣarūn · Ḍuribat ʿalayhimu'dh-dhillatu ayna mā thuqifū illā bi-ḥablin mina'llāhi wa ḥablin mina'n-nāsi wa bāʾū bi-ghaḍabin mina'llāhi wa ḍuribat ʿalayhimu'l-maskana, dhālika bi-annahum kānū yakfurūna bi-āyāti'llāhi wa yaqtulūna'l-anbiyāʾa bi-ghayri ḥaqq, dhālika bimā ʿaṣaw wa kānū yaʿtadūn*

"**They will do you no harm beyond annoyance**… · **Wherever they are found, abasement is stamped upon them — except by a rope from God and a rope from people.** … This is because **they denied the signs of God and killed the prophets without right**; this is because **they disobeyed and transgressed.**"

### The place of the exception: إِلَّا بِحَبْلٍ

**And the most important element of the verse is the exception in its middle, and it is usually passed over: *illā bi-ḥablin mina'llāhi wa ḥablin mina'n-nās*.**

**That is, the ruling is not absolute; it has a caveat, and the caveat is inside the sentence.** **Two ropes: one from God, one from people.**

**And the word *ḥabl* had occurred nine verses earlier, at verse 103.** **The comparison of the two occurrences was recorded above.**

**ذِلَّة** — root **ذ-ل-ل**: **lowness, submission.** *Dhalūl* — a tamed animal, one brought under. **And the opposite of the root is *ʿizza*.**

**مَسْكَنَة** — root **س-ك-ن**: **stillness, immobility.** *Miskīn* — one who cannot move, who is helpless.
