**And it must be recorded that both words come with the same verb: *ḍuribat* — "was struck".** In Arabic the verb *ḍaraba* is used also for the pitching of a tent and the stamping of a seal. **And the verb is passive: the one who strikes is not named.**

**The same two words and the same verb occur together at 2/61 in `002-bakara.md`** (*wa ḍuribat ʿalayhimu'dh-dhillatu wa'l-maskanatu wa bāʾū bi-ghaḍabin mina'llāh*). **I rely on that treatment.** **The difference between the two verses must be recorded, and it is the point this commentary draws attention to:**

| | **al-Baqara 2/61** | **Āl ʿImrān 3/112** |
|---|---|---|
| The two words | *Al-dhillatu **wa**'l-maskana* — **together** | *Al-dhilla*… and then separately *al-maskana* — **parted** |
| **The exception** | **None** | ***Illā bi-ḥablin mina'llāhi wa ḥablin mina'n-nās*** |
| The ground | *Bimā ʿaṣaw wa kānū yaʿtadūn* | **The same** |

**And the difference is at the most important place: in giving the same ruling, Āl ʿImrān adds an exception.**

**Per the method (`USLUP.md`) I record this plainly:** these two verses cannot be read as a blanket ruling about a community. **The reason is not brought in from outside; it is from the text itself, in three places:**

**One.** The ground of the ruling is counted at the end of the verse **by verbs**: denial, killing prophets, disobedience, transgression. **That is, the ruling is tied not to a name but to a list of conduct.**

**Two.** There is an exception in the middle of the sentence, and the exception says that the ruling can be lifted.

**Three.** And one verse later the sūra will set up the division plainly: *laysū sawāʾan* (113).

---

## 3/113-115 — لَيْسُوا۟ سَوَآءً مِّنْ أَهْلِ ٱلْكِتَٰبِ أُمَّةٌ قَآئِمَةٌ يَتْلُونَ ءَايَٰتِ ٱللَّهِ ءَانَآءَ ٱلَّيْلِ وَهُمْ يَسْجُدُونَ

*Laysū sawāʾan min ahli'l-kitābi ummatun qāʾimatun yatlūna āyāti'llāhi ānāʾa'l-layli wa hum yasjudūn · Yuʾminūna bi'llāhi wa'l-yawmi'l-ākhiri wa yaʾmurūna bi'l-maʿrūfi wa yanhawna ʿani'l-munkari wa yusāriʿūna fi'l-khayrāt, wa ulāʾika mina'ṣ-ṣāliḥīn · Wa mā yafʿalū min khayrin fa-lan yukfarūh, wa'llāhu ʿalīmun bi'l-muttaqīn*

"**They are not all alike.** Among the People of the Book there is **a community that stands upright**: **they recite the signs of God through the watches of the night, prostrating themselves.** · **They believe in God and the Last Day, enjoin what is right, forbid what is wrong, and hasten to good works. Those are among the righteous.** · **No good they do will go unrewarded; and God knows those who guard themselves.**"

---

**This verse is singled out in this commentary and is the note under which all the sūra's statements about the People of the Book are to be read. The note was entered in the introduction to the sūra; here its ground is shown from the text.**

### لَيْسُوا۟ سَوَآءً — the sentence itself

**Two words. And both are familiar from another place in the sūra.**

**سَوَآء** — root **س-و-ي**: **to be equal, to be even.** **The word was analysed at verse 64: *kalimatin sawāʾin baynanā wa baynakum*.** **What was recorded there: at the centre of the root stands *the absence of tilt* — what does not lean to one side.**

**And now the same word comes negated:**

| Verse | Wording | What it does |
|---|---|---|
| **64** | *Kalimatin **sawāʾin** baynanā wa baynakum* | **Common ground** — the two sides are equal |
| **113** | *Laysū **sawāʾan*** | **"They are not all alike"** — a set is not uniform |

**The same word, at two different pieces of work. This is a repetition verifiable within the sūra and was recorded in the table in the introduction.**

**I record this as my own reading, and its ground is the two occurrences of the word:** the sūra settles two separate questions of equality with the same word. **In one place it says that the ground between two sides is equal; in the other that one side is *not* uniform within itself.** Both are the work of the same root: measuring.

### أُمَّةٌ قَآئِمَةٌ — and the counting of the attributes

***Ummatun qāʾima*** — **a community that stands upright.** The root **ق-و-م** was treated at verses 96-97 (*maqāmu Ibrāhīm*), occurred at verse 18 as *qāʾiman bi'l-qisṭ* and at verse 75 as *qāʾimā*. **The fourth occurrence is here.**

**And the attributes given to this community must be counted, because they can be compared with the attributes the sūra gives to its own community:**

| # | Attribute (113-114) | Elsewhere in the sūra |
|---|---|---|
| **1** | *Yatlūna āyāti'llāhi ānāʾa'l-layl* | Cf. 17: *al-mustaghfirīna **bi'l-asḥār*** |
| **2** | *Wa hum **yasjudūn*** | — |
| **3** | *Yuʾminūna bi'llāhi wa'l-yawmi'l-ākhir* | Cf. 110: *wa **tuʾminūna bi'llāh*** |
| **4** | *Wa **yaʾmurūna bi'l-maʿrūf*** | **The same as 110 and 104** |
| **5** | *Wa **yanhawna ʿani'l-munkar*** | **The same as 110 and 104** |
| **6** | *Wa **yusāriʿūna fi'l-khayrāt*** | Cf. 133: *wa **sāriʿū** ilā maghfiratin* |
| **The ruling** | *Wa ulāʾika **mina'ṣ-ṣāliḥīn*** | — |

**And what the table shows is a fact verifiable from the text: *all three* of the attributes given to the community addressed in verse 110 are given here to a community from among the People of the Book.**

**I record this as an observation, and it is the sūra's own method:** the attributes are named together with whoever carries them. **The same three verbs occur, for two separate communities, within the same group of verses.** That is, the sūra shows three verses apart that the attribute is not proper to one group.

### The sūra's statements about the People of the Book — a table together with the note of 113

**I now give all the sūra's statements about the People of the Book together. The purpose of the table is to show the *note of extent* of each statement. The notes of extent are taken from the wording of the verses themselves.**

| Verse | Statement | Note of extent — from the verse's own wording |
|---|---|---|
| **64** | *Taʿālaw ilā kalimatin sawāʾin* | **A call** — not a ruling; and with *fa-in tawallaw* the possibility of refusal is taken into account from the start |
| **65-68** | *Lima tuḥājjūna fī Ibrāhīm* | **To those who dispute** — and the answer is given by an attribute (*ḥanīfan musliman*) |
| **69** | *Waddat **ṭāʾifatun min** ahli'l-kitāb* | ***Min*** — a party |
| **70-71** | *Lima takfurūn / lima talbisūn* | **To those who do the act** — in the interrogative |
| **72** | *Wa qālat **ṭāʾifatun min** ahli'l-kitāb* | ***Min*** — a party |
| **75** | *Wa **min** ahli'l-kitāb… **wa minhum*** | **An open division** — and the favourable one **first** |
| **78** | *Wa inna **minhum la-farīqan*** | ***Min*** — a part |
| **98-99** | *Lima takfurūn / lima taṣuddūn* | **To those who do the act** — in the interrogative |
| **100** | *In tuṭīʿū **farīqan min**a'lladhīna ūtu'l-kitāb* | ***Min*** — a group |
| **110** | *La-kāna khayran lahum* | **A division within the sentence itself:** *minhumu'l-muʾminūn* |
| **112** | *Ḍuribat ʿalayhimu'dh-dhilla* | **An exception within the sentence:** *illā bi-ḥablin mina'llāh*; and the ground counted by verbs |
| **113-115** | *Laysū sawāʾan… **ummatun qāʾima*** | **An open note** — the attributes counted, and *mina'ṣ-ṣāliḥīn* said |
| **199** | *Wa inna **min** ahli'l-kitābi la-man yuʾmin* | ***Min*** — and *lahum ajruhum ʿinda rabbihim* |

**Two facts follow from the table and can be verified from the text:**

**One. There is not a single unfavourable sentence of ruling in the sūra built about the *whole* of the People of the Book.** Every statement carries either a particle *min*, or an exception, or a division, or the note of the interrogative mood.

**Two. The sūra builds a favourable ruling plainly in two places: 113-115 and 199.** **And both come *after* the sūra's heaviest statements** — 113 immediately after 112; 199 at the end of the sūra.

**I record this as my own reading, and it is not binding:** the placing of the note immediately after the heavy statement can be read as a method of the sūra. **The text meets the generalising of its own sentence within itself.**

**And per the method (`USLUP.md`) I record this: in this commentary no blanket ruling about any religious or ethnic group has been drawn from any statement of the sūra. This is the note the sūra itself sets down.**

### وَمَا يَفْعَلُوا۟ مِنْ خَيْرٍ فَلَن يُكْفَرُوهُ

The concrete sense of the root **ك-ف-ر** is **to cover**. **And here the verb is passive and negative: *lan yukfarūh* — "it will not be covered", that is, it will not be left unrewarded.**

**I record this as an observation about the word:** the same root had been used throughout the sūra for denial; here it is used for **a deed's not being covered up**. **That is, the root conveys denial, ingratitude and the concealing of a work alike.**

**And the subject of the sentence must be recorded: *wa mā yafʿalū min khayrin*** — a conditional sentence, and the subject of the condition is not tied to any group. **The sentence holds whoever it is that does the good.**

---

## 3/116-120 — إِنَّ ٱلَّذِينَ كَفَرُوا۟ لَن تُغْنِىَ عَنْهُمْ أَمْوَٰلُهُمْ … لَا تَتَّخِذُوا۟ بِطَانَةً مِّن دُونِكُمْ

*Inna'lladhīna kafarū lan tughniya ʿanhum amwāluhum wa lā awlāduhum mina'llāhi shayʾā, wa ulāʾika aṣḥābu'n-nāri hum fīhā khālidūn · Mathalu mā yunfiqūna fī hādhihi'l-ḥayāti'd-dunyā ka-mathali rīḥin fīhā ṣirrun aṣābat ḥartha qawmin ẓalamū anfusahum fa-ahlakat'hu… · Yā ayyuha'lladhīna āmanū lā tattakhidhū biṭānatan min dūnikum lā yaʾlūnakum khabālan waddū mā ʿanittum, qad badati'l-baghḍāʾu min afwāhihim wa mā tukhfī ṣudūruhum akbar… · Hā-antum ulāʾi tuḥibbūnahum wa lā yuḥibbūnakum… · In tamsaskum ḥasanatun tasuʾhum wa in tuṣibkum sayyiʾatun yafraḥū bihā, wa in taṣbirū wa tattaqū lā yaḍurrukum kayduhum shayʾā, inna'llāha bimā yaʿmalūna muḥīṭ*

"**Neither their wealth nor their children will avail those who disbelieve anything against God**… · **What they spend in this life of the world is like a wind carrying a searing cold:** it strikes the tilth of a people who wronged themselves and destroys it… · **You who believe! Do not take as intimates those who are not of you: they spare no effort to harm you, and they wish you distress. Hatred has spilled from their mouths; and what their breasts conceal is greater**… · **There you are: you love them, and they do not love you**… · **If good touches you it grieves them; if evil strikes you they rejoice at it. But if you are steadfast and guard yourselves, their scheming will do you no harm at all.** God encompasses what they do."

### مَثَلُ … كَمَثَلِ رِيحٍ فِيهَا صِرٌّ

**صِرّ** — root **ص-ر-ر**: the lexicographers explain the word as **severe cold** or as **a wind that makes a searing sound**; there is an idea of **pressing and of sound** in the root (*ṣarra* — it creaked, it pressed).

**And the structure of the comparison must be recorded:**

| Element | Its counterpart in the comparison |
|---|---|
| *Mā yunfiqūn* | **The tilth** — laboured over, grown |
| *Rīḥun fīhā ṣirr* | **The wind** — coming from outside |
| *Fa-ahlakat'hu* | **Destruction** |

**I record this as an observation:** what is destroyed in the comparison is not the wealth spent but **the return on the spending.** The tilth had already grown — what is lost is the crop.

**And the word *rīḥ* had occurred in another idiom at 8/46 in `008-enfal.md`** (*wa tadhhaba **rīḥukum***) **and there the wind meant *strength*.** **The same word in two sūras, at two separate pieces of work: one the going of strength, the other the destroying of tilth.** This is a fact verifiable within the series.

### بِطَانَة — root: ب-ط-ن

The root **ب-ط-ن**: **the inside, the belly, what is hidden.** *Baṭn* — belly. *Bāṭin* — what is within. **And *biṭāna*, for the lexicographers, is *the lining of a garment*** — the face that does not show outwardly and touches the body.

**And its opposite is recorded by the lexicographers too: *ẓihāra* — the outer face of a garment.**

**I record this as an observation about the word:** the word does not say "companion" or "friend". It says **lining** — that is, **the innermost, what touches the skin, what is not seen from outside.** **And the prohibition falls not on the relation but on this *degree* of the relation.**

**ولا يَأْلُونَكُمْ خَبَالًا** — *lā yaʾlūnakum khabālā*: root **أ-ل-و**, "**to fall short of nothing, to hold back from nothing**". **And *khabāl* (**خ-ب-ل**): a derangement of mind and body, corruption.**

**And the verse's notes must be recorded, because the ground of the prohibition is inside the verse and is tied not to a name but to an attitude:**

| # | The ground | Wording |
|---|---|---|
| **1** | That they hold back from nothing in harming you | *Lā yaʾlūnakum khabālā* |
| **2** | That they wish you distress | *Waddū mā ʿanittum* |
| **3** | That the hatred shows in their words | *Qad badati'l-baghḍāʾu min afwāhihim* |
| **4** | That what is concealed is greater still | *Wa mā tukhfī ṣudūruhum akbar* |

**Per the method (`USLUP.md`) I record this: all four grounds describe an *attitude*; none of them gives a group's name.** **The verse says *min dūnikum*, and the measure of that is the four attitudes counted below it.** **No ruling about any community is built on this verse in this commentary** — and the sūra itself has already entered this note at verse 113.

### وَإِن تَصْبِرُوا۟ وَتَتَّقُوا۟ — the close of the block

**And the fourth block closes with a condition: *wa in taṣbirū wa tattaqū lā yaḍurrukum kayduhum shayʾā*.**

**And the pair (*ṣabr* + *taqwā*) occurs three times in the sūra:**

| Verse | Wording | Context |
|---|---|---|
| **120** | *In **taṣbirū wa tattaqū*** | **The close of the block** — enmity |
| **125** | *Balā in **taṣbirū wa tattaqū*** | **A promise of help** |
| **186** | *Wa in **taṣbirū wa tattaqū** fa-inna dhālika min ʿazmi'l-umūr* | **Testing** |

**Three occurrences, the same two verbs.** This is a repetition verifiable within the sūra.

**I record this as an observation:** in all three places the pair changes **the outcome of something the other side will do.** **That is, the verse does not remove the outward pressure; it ties its effect to an inward attitude.**

---

## 3/121-122 — وَإِذْ غَدَوْتَ مِنْ أَهْلِكَ تُبَوِّئُ ٱلْمُؤْمِنِينَ مَقَٰعِدَ لِلْقِتَالِ

*Wa idh ghadawta min ahlika tubawwiʾu'l-muʾminīna maqāʿida li'l-qitāl, wa'llāhu samīʿun ʿalīm · Idh hammat ṭāʾifatāni minkum an tafshalā wa'llāhu waliyyuhumā, wa ʿala'llāhi fa'l-yatawakkali'l-muʾminūn*

"**When you set out early from your family to station the believers in their posts for the fighting.** God is hearing, knowing. · **When two parties among you were inclined to falter; yet God was the protector of both.** Let the believers rely on God alone."

---

**This verse opens the fifth and longest block of the sūra. Per the method (`USLUP.md`) I draw a boundary and shall keep it throughout the block: in the discussion of Uḥud I do not go into the detail of the reports or into personal names. The verses themselves name no one anywhere — no commander, no tribe, no unit. This silence of the text is preserved in this commentary.**

**The only thing to be recorded is this: in the classical sources this block is identified as Uḥud, and this identification is widespread. I transmit it in language that plainly marks the uncertainty; I do not ascribe it word for word to any particular report.**

### غَدَوْتَ — root: غ-د-و

The root **غ-د-و**: **the early hour of morning; and setting out at that hour.** *Ghadāt* — morning. *Ghuduww* — the morning departure. **And the opposite of the root is *rawāḥ*** — the evening return.

**And the choice of the verb must be recorded: the verse does not say "you set out", it says "you set out *early in the morning*".** **The hour is inside the verb.**

**مِنْ أَهْلِكَ** — "**from your family**". **And this note has been chosen for the opening of the block.**

**I record this as an observation about the word order:** the block opens not with a scene of battle but with **a leaving of the house.** **And two words stand together: *ahl* (family) and *al-qitāl* (fighting).** In one sentence the nearest thing and the harshest thing stand side by side.

### تُبَوِّئُ ٱلْمُؤْمِنِينَ مَقَٰعِدَ — root: ب-و-أ

The root **ب-و-أ**: **to settle in a place, to lodge; and to settle someone in a place.** *Mubawwaʾ* — the place one is settled in. *Bāʾa* — he returned, he came to a place.

**And the same root occurred at 22/26 in `022-hac.md`: *wa idh bawwaʾnā li-Ibrāhīma makāna'l-bayt*** — "when We **assigned** to Abraham the place of the House". **I rely on that treatment.**

| Place | Verb | What is settled |
|---|---|---|
| **al-Ḥajj 22/26** | *Bawwaʾnā li-Ibrāhīma **makāna'l-bayt*** | **A place of worship** |
| **Āl ʿImrān 3/121** | *Tubawwiʾu'l-muʾminīna **maqāʿida li'l-qitāl*** | **Battle posts** |

**The same root, two different settlings.** This is a fact verifiable within the series.

**مَقَاعِد** — the plural of *maqʿad*; root **ق-ع-د**: **to sit.** **That is, "places to sit in" — posts.**

**I record this as an observation about the word:** the place appointed for battle is named with a word from a root of **staying still**, not of movement. **And as the block goes on this word will take on its sense** — because what is described at verse 152 is exactly the abandoning of the place held.

### أَن تَفْشَلَا — the first occurrence of the root

The root **ف-ش-ل**: **to slacken, to come undone, to be unable to act for fear.** The lexicographers explain the root as **the coming undone of courage and strength**; it is a state between fear and exhaustion.

**And the root occurs twice in the sūra: here (*an tafshalā*) and at verse 152 (*fashiltum*).**

| Verse | Wording | Mood | What it conveys |
|---|---|---|---|
| **122** | *Hammat ṭāʾifatāni minkum an **tafshalā*** | **An approach** — *hamma*: to be minded to | **It did not happen** |
| **152** | *Ḥattā idhā **fashiltum*** | **Accomplished** | **It happened** |

**This is a repetition verifiable within the sūra and binds the two ends of the block.** **This link will be treated in detail below at verse 152.**

**And the end of verse 122 must be recorded: *wa'llāhu waliyyuhumā*.** **That is, about the two parties inclined to falter the verse does not pass a ruling of loss; it says that the protection continues.**

**I record this as an observation about the word order:** while naming a failing, the sentence writes immediately afterwards that the bond has not broken. **And this structure will be repeated throughout the sūra's Uḥud block** — at 152 *wa la-qad ʿafā ʿankum*, at 155 *wa la-qad ʿafa'llāhu ʿanhum*.

---

## 3/123-127 — وَلَقَدْ نَصَرَكُمُ ٱللَّهُ بِبَدْرٍ وَأَنتُمْ أَذِلَّةٌ

*Wa la-qad naṣarakumu'llāhu bi-Badrin wa antum adhilla, fa'ttaqu'llāha laʿallakum tashkurūn · Idh taqūlu li'l-muʾminīna a-lan yakfiyakum an yumiddakum rabbukum bi-thalāthati ālāfin mina'l-malāʾikati munzalīn · Balā in taṣbirū wa tattaqū wa yaʾtūkum min fawrihim hādhā yumdidkum rabbukum bi-khamsati ālāfin mina'l-malāʾikati musawwimīn · Wa mā jaʿalahu'llāhu illā bushrā lakum wa li-taṭmaʾinna qulūbukum bih, wa ma'n-naṣru illā min ʿindi'llāhi'l-ʿazīzi'l-ḥakīm · Li-yaqṭaʿa ṭarafan mina'lladhīna kafarū aw yakbitahum fa-yanqalibū khāʾibīn*

"**God helped you at Badr when you were weak;** so guard yourselves before God, that you may be thankful. · **When you were saying to the believers: 'Is it not enough for you that your Lord should reinforce you with three thousand angels sent down?'** · **Yes: if you are steadfast and guard yourselves, and they come upon you at once, your Lord will reinforce you with five thousand angels marked out.** · **God made this only as good news for you and so that your hearts might be set at rest by it. Help comes only from God, *al-ʿAzīz* and *al-Ḥakīm*.**"

### A return to an earlier one: بِبَدْرٍ

**And the block, in its second verse, turns back to an earlier one. In the sūra this is the second occurrence:**

| Verse | Expression | What it does |
|---|---|---|
| **13** | *Qad kāna lakum āyatun fī **fiʾatayni'ltaqatā*** | **Unnamed** — "two companies that met" |
| **123** | *Naṣarakumu'llāhu **bi-Badr*** | **Named** |

**Two occurrences, and the first is unnamed.** This is a fact verifiable within the sūra.

**وَأَنتُمْ أَذِلَّةٌ** — "**when you were weak**". **The root ذ-ل-ل was treated at verse 112 (*al-dhilla*).** **The same root in two separate places, eleven verses apart: one a ruling, the other a note of a state.**

**I record this as an observation:** the reminder names not the victory but **the condition in which the victory began.** The weight of the sentence lies not in *naṣarakum* but in *wa antum adhilla*.

### وَمَا ٱلنَّصْرُ إِلَّا مِنْ عِندِ ٱللَّهِ — the place of the note

**And verse 126 comes immediately after the two verses in which the number of the angels is named, and it enters a note.**

**The word order of the note is this, and it has two steps:**

| Step | Wording | What it says |
|---|---|---|
| **1** | *Wa mā jaʿalahu'llāhu illā **bushrā** lakum wa **li-taṭmaʾinna** qulūbukum* | The work of what was given: **good news and being set at rest** |
| **2** | *Wa ma'n-naṣru **illā min ʿindi'llāh*** | The source of the help: **not the number** |

**And both sentences are built with *illā* — the pattern of restriction.**

**I record this as an observation about the word order:** immediately after giving a number, the verse says that the number is **not the cause.** **That is, the figure is given not as a reckoning of force but as a note about morale.**

**And the same note is at 8/10 in `008-enfal.md` as well** (*wa mā jaʿalahu'llāhu illā bushrā wa li-taṭmaʾinna bihī qulūbukum, wa ma'n-naṣru illā min ʿindi'llāh*) **and it is almost word for word the same.** **I rely on that treatment and record this correspondence between the two verses as a fact verifiable from the text.**

**Per the method (`USLUP.md`) I record this: no description whatever is entered in this section of how or in what form the angels were present.** The verse gives the adjectives *munzalīn* (sent down) and *musawwimīn* (marked out) and says nothing else. **This silence of the text is preserved.** **And there is disagreement over the sense of *musawwimīn*** — as with *al-khaylu'l-musawwama* in verse 14, the root **س-و-م** conveys both *to mark* and *to turn loose*; **I impose no preference.**

---

## 3/128-129 — لَيْسَ لَكَ مِنَ ٱلْأَمْرِ شَىْءٌ

*Laysa laka mina'l-amri shayʾun aw yatūba ʿalayhim aw yuʿadhdhibahum fa-innahum ẓālimūn · Wa li'llāhi mā fi's-samāwāti wa mā fi'l-arḍ, yaghfiru li-man yashāʾu wa yuʿadhdhibu man yashāʾ, wa'llāhu ghafūrun raḥīm*

"**Nothing of the matter falls to you:** either He turns to them in mercy or He punishes them, for they are wrongdoers. · **Whatever is in the heavens and the earth belongs to God; He forgives whom He wills and punishes whom He wills.** God is much-forgiving, most merciful."

---

**This verse is a link in the line of "the limit of responsibility" treated in many places in the series, and it is to be added to the table at 28/56 in `028-kasas.md`.**

### The sharpest expression of the limit

**The table set up at 28/56 in `028-kasas.md` was this, and I rely on it:**

| Place | Expression | What it limits |
|---|---|---|
| al-Aḥqāf 46/35 | *Balāgh* | **The task** — it is to convey |
| al-Zumar 39/41 | *Wa mā anta ʿalayhim bi-wakīl* | **Responsibility** |
| Qāf 50/45 | *Wa mā anta ʿalayhim bi-jabbār* | **The authority to compel** |
| Fāṭir 35/8 | *Fa-lā tadhhab nafsuka ʿalayhim ḥasarāt* | **Grief** |
| Luqmān 31/23 | *Fa-lā yaḥzunka kufruh* | **Grief** |
| al-Qaṣaṣ 28/56 | *Lā tahdī **man aḥbabta*** | **Love** — the closest bond |

**And in this commentary I add this verse to the table:**

| Place | Expression | What it limits |
|---|---|---|
| **Āl ʿImrān 3/128** | *Laysa laka **mina'l-amri shayʾun*** | **The matter itself** — no share at all in the outcome |

**And the difference of the added link must be recorded, because it stands apart from the others:**

**In the other six verses what is limited is a particular authority, a feeling or a task:** giving guidance, being a trustee, compelling, grieving. **Here, on the other hand, what is limited is *al-amr* — the matter itself.** And the negation is made with the most general word: *shayʾun* — "nothing at all".

**I record this as my own reading, and its ground is the breadth of the word:** the others were closing off a field; **this verse leaves no field.** *Mina'l-amri shayʾun* — "nothing of the matter".

### The context of the sentence and a difficulty of word order

**And the lexicographers differ over the syntactic analysis (*iʿrāb*) of the sentence, and the disagreement is not to be concealed. The difficulty is this: what is the expression *aw yatūba ʿalayhim* attached to?**

| Reading | The attachment | Sense |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | *Laysa laka mina'l-amri shayʾun* is **an interposed clause**; *aw yatūba* attaches to *li-yaqṭaʿa* in the preceding verse | "Either He cuts off a part of them, or crushes them, **or turns to them in mercy, or punishes them**" |
| 2 | *Aw yatūba* attaches directly to *laysa laka* | "Nothing of this matter falls to you — **neither the acceptance of their repentance nor their punishment**" |

**Both are transmitted; I do not impose a preference.**

**But there is a conclusion common to both readings, and I record it: on either reading the deciding party is not the one addressed.** And the continuation of the verse confirms it: *yaghfiru li-man yashāʾu wa yuʿadhdhibu man yashāʾ* (129).

### The verse's place in the block

**And *where the verse is placed* must be recorded, because it cannot be separated from its content.**

| Verse | Subject |
|---|---|
| **123-127** | **Help** — Badr, the angels, the good news |
| **128** | ***Laysa laka mina'l-amri shayʾun*** |
| **130-136** | **Ribā, spending, anger, pardon** |

**That is, the sentence of the limit of responsibility has been placed *immediately after* the verses in which the help is described.**

**I record this as my own reading, and it is not binding:** in the place where the help is described, it is said once more that the one who asks for help and the one who gives it are distinct. **And this is the note of verse 126 (*wa ma'n-naṣru illā min ʿindi'llāh*) seen from the human side.** One states the source of the help, the other the owner of the decision.

---

## 3/130-132 — يَٰٓأَيُّهَا ٱلَّذِينَ ءَامَنُوا۟ لَا تَأْكُلُوا۟ ٱلرِّبَوٰٓا۟ أَضْعَٰفًا مُّضَٰعَفَةً

*Yā ayyuha'lladhīna āmanū lā taʾkulu'r-ribā aḍʿāfan muḍāʿafatan wa'ttaqu'llāha laʿallakum tufliḥūn · Wa'ttaqu'n-nāra'llatī uʿiddat li'l-kāfirīn · Wa aṭīʿu'llāha wa'r-rasūla laʿallakum turḥamūn*

"**You who believe! Do not consume *ribā*, doubled and multiplied, and guard yourselves before God, that you may prosper.** · **Guard yourselves against the fire prepared for those who deny.** · **Obey God and the Messenger, that you may be shown mercy.**"

### Relying on al-Baqara

*Ribā* was treated in detail at 2/275-281 in `002-bakara.md` — the sense of the root, the definition of the transaction, its difference from trade, the drawing of the limit. **I rely on that treatment and do not repeat it here.**

**Only two things are to be recorded here, and both are proper to this verse.**

**One. The length of the verse.** In al-Baqara the subject runs for **seven verses**; here it is **a single verse.** **And this is an instance of the principle recorded in the introduction to the sūra:** Āl ʿImrān names what has been treated in al-Baqara in the form of a reminder.

**Two. The note أَضْعَٰفًا مُّضَٰعَفَةً and the disagreement it gives rise to.**

The root **ض-ع-ف**: **a fold, a multiple.** *Ḍiʿf* — the multiple of a thing. **And the construction is a noun and an adjective from the same root: *aḍʿāfan muḍāʿafatan* — "multiplied multiples".** **The same form of emphasis was in verse 14 as well: *al-qanāṭīru'l-muqanṭara*.** **Two occurrences, in a single sūra.**

**And there is a divergence over the function of the construction in the ruling, and it is not to be concealed:**

| Reading | What *aḍʿāfan muḍāʿafatan* is |
|---|---|
| 1 | **A restrictive qualification (*iḥtirāzī*)** — the prohibition is only for the form that is multiplied over and over |
| 2 | **A description of the actual state** — it describes the common practice and does not narrow the ruling; al-Baqara 2/278-279 already sets the limit absolutely |

**Both are transmitted; I do not impose a preference and I issue no legal ruling.** **This is the same limit recorded in `002-bakara.md`.**

**And I record an observation about the word order:** the three verses end with three separate sentences of consequence — *tufliḥūn* (130), *li'l-kāfirīn* (131), *turḥamūn* (132). **And *tufliḥūn* will come once more in the last verse of the sūra (200).**

**وَأَطِيعُوا۟ ٱللَّهَ وَٱلرَّسُولَ** — root **ط-و-ع**. **And the same command occurs twice in the sūra: at 32 and at 132.** **And at verse 152 its breach will be recorded: *wa ʿaṣaytum*.** **Command, command, breach — this is a weave verifiable within the sūra and was recorded in the table in the introduction.**

---

## 3/133-136 — وَسَارِعُوٓا۟ إِلَىٰ مَغْفِرَةٍ مِّن رَّبِّكُمْ · ٱلَّذِينَ يُنفِقُونَ فِى ٱلسَّرَّآءِ وَٱلضَّرَّآءِ وَٱلْكَٰظِمِينَ ٱلْغَيْظَ وَٱلْعَافِينَ عَنِ ٱلنَّاسِ

*Wa sāriʿū ilā maghfiratin min rabbikum wa jannatin ʿarḍuha's-samāwātu wa'l-arḍu uʿiddat li'l-muttaqīn · Alladhīna yunfiqūna fi's-sarrāʾi wa'ḍ-ḍarrāʾi wa'l-kāẓimīna'l-ghayẓa wa'l-ʿāfīna ʿani'n-nās, wa'llāhu yuḥibbu'l-muḥsinīn · Wa'lladhīna idhā faʿalū fāḥishatan aw ẓalamū anfusahum dhakaru'llāha fa'staghfarū li-dhunūbihim wa man yaghfiru'dh-dhunūba illa'llāh, wa lam yuṣirrū ʿalā mā faʿalū wa hum yaʿlamūn*

"**Race towards forgiveness from your Lord and towards a garden whose breadth is the heavens and the earth, prepared for those who guard themselves.** · **They are those who spend in ease and in hardship, who swallow their anger and who pardon people. God loves those who do good.** · **And those who, when they commit an indecency or wrong themselves, remember God and ask forgiveness for their sins** — and who forgives sins but God? — **and who do not persist in what they did, knowing what it was.**"

### وَسَارِعُوٓا۟ — the pattern of the root

The root **س-ر-ع**: **speed.** **And the verb is in Form III (*mufāʿala*, *sāraʿa*): in Arabic this form conveys *reciprocity and competition*.**

**I record this as an observation about the word:** the command means not "hurry" but **"race"**. And racing requires the presence of another.

**And the same root was used at verse 114 of a community from among the People of the Book: *wa yusāriʿūna fi'l-khayrāt*.** **Two occurrences, the same form:**

| Verse | Wording | Who | Mood |
|---|---|---|---|
| **114** | *Yusāriʿūna fi'l-**khayrāt*** | **A community from among the People of the Book** | **A statement** — they do it |
| **133** | *Wa **sāriʿū** ilā maghfiratin* | **The community addressed** | **A command** |

**This is a repetition verifiable within the sūra. And I record this as my own reading, and it is not binding:** an act described as what others do is, nineteen verses later, commanded to the one addressed.

**And the measure used in describing the garden must be recorded: *ʿarḍuha's-samāwātu wa'l-arḍ* — "its breadth is the heavens and the earth".** **And the word *ʿarḍ* (breadth) has been chosen; not *ṭūl* (length).** **The same measure is at 57/21 in `057-hadid.md` as well** (*ʿarḍuhā ka-ʿarḍi's-samāʾi wa'l-arḍ*). **Two sūras use the same measure.**

### 3/134 — four attributes

| # | Attribute | Field |
|---|---|---|
| **1** | *Yunfiqūna fi's-**sarrāʾi** wa'ḍ-**ḍarrāʾi*** | **Wealth** — in two opposite conditions |
| **2** | *Wa'l-**kāẓimīna'l-ghayẓ*** | **Anger** — holding in |
| **3** | *Wa'l-**ʿāfīna ʿani'n-nās*** | **Pardon** — letting go |
| **4** | *Wa'llāhu yuḥibbu'l-**muḥsinīn*** | **Doing good** — the sentence that gives the ruling |

**And the word order of the first attribute must be recorded: *fi's-sarrāʾi wa'ḍ-ḍarrāʾi* — two opposite conditions.** **س-ر-ر** (joy, ease) and **ض-ر-ر** (harm, hardship). **The two words are on the same measure (*faʿlāʾ*) and are alike in sound as well.** **That is, by putting the two extremes into the same pattern the sentence makes spending independent of circumstance.**

### ٱلْكَٰظِمِينَ ٱلْغَيْظَ — root: ك-ظ-م

**The root was analysed in detail at 68/48 in `068-kalem.md` (*makẓūm*), tabulated by difference of pattern at 43/17-18 in `043-zuhruf.md`, and treated at 12/84 in `012-yusuf.md` (*kaẓīm*) and at 40/18 in `040-gafir.md` (*kāẓimīn*). I rely on those treatments and do not repeat them.** A summary of what was recorded there:

> **The concrete sense of the root: *kaẓama'l-qirba* — he filled a waterskin and tied its mouth.** That is, **a vessel filled and stopped at the mouth.** The pressure inside is there; but it cannot get out.
>
> **The difference of pattern:** *makẓūm* is a **passive participle** — stopped from outside; *kaẓīm* is the **intensive on *faʿīl*** — one who ties his own mouth shut.

**And there are three notes belonging here.**

**One. The pattern here is *kāẓim* — an active participle.** That is, the word conveys **the one who is doing the work.** *Makẓūm* is passive, *kaẓīm* is intensive; **kāẓim** is **the one performing the act.**

| Pattern | Place | Who |
|---|---|---|
| **مَكْظُوم** — passive participle | al-Qalam 68/48 | **Stopped from outside** |
| **كَظِيم** — *faʿīl* (intensive) | Yūsuf 12/84, al-Zukhruf 43/17 | **One who stops his own inside** |
| **كَاظِم** — active participle | **Āl ʿImrān 3/134**, Ghāfir 40/18 | **The one doing the holding in** |

**Three patterns, one root — and this distinction was tabulated in `068-kalem.md` and `043-zuhruf.md`.**

**Two. And the object of the word is *al-ghayẓ*.** **It was recorded in `067-mulk.md`: *ghayẓ* is swallowed anger.** **And at 68/48 in `068-kalem.md` the tension between these two was dealt with honestly** — in one place the Qurʾān praises the swallowing of anger (this verse), and in another a prophet "calling out while his anger was swallowed" is named as a negative example. **I rely on the analysis there and do not repeat it.**

**Three. And the real note belonging here is the *order* in the list, and I give it as my own reading:**

**The second attribute is holding in (*kāẓim*); the third attribute is letting go (*ʿāfī*).**

The root **ع-ف-و**: **to wipe away, to leave no trace; and from this, to pardon.** The lexicographers give as an instance the wind's wiping away a trace: *ʿafati'r-rīḥu'l-athar*.

| Attribute | The operation | Where |
|---|---|---|
| *Kāẓim* | **Holding in** — not letting out | **Inside** |
| *ʿĀfī* | **Wiping** — leaving no record | **Inside** |

**And the two verbs do not look in the same direction: one blocks the exit, the other does away with what is inside.**

**I record this as my own reading, and its ground is the picture of the two roots:** holding anger in does not mean that the anger has ended — the skin is still full. **Pardoning, on the other hand, empties what is full.** **The order is meaningful for this reason: first not letting it out, then not keeping it in.**

**And the note *ʿani'n-nās*: the pardon is opened to everyone, without its being said from whom.** **The same note of breadth is in the sūra as *li'n-nās* at verses 96 and 110 as well.**

### 3/135 — not persisting

*Wa lam yuṣirrū ʿalā mā faʿalū **wa hum yaʿlamūn***

The root **ص-ر-ر**: **to bind oneself to a thing and keep at it, to hold tight.** **And the same root occurred at verse 117 as *ṣirr* (a searing cold)** — and there too there was an idea of **pressing** in the root. **Two occurrences, the same root, two different derivatives.**

**And the note itself must be recorded: the list sets no condition of *sinlessness*.** The attribute is not not committing an indecency; it is **not persisting after having committed one**.

**I record this as an observation about the word order:** the conditional sentence begins with *idhā faʿalū* — "when they have done it". **That is, the act is assumed to have taken place.** The measure lies not in whether the event happens but in **what is done after it.**

---

## 3/137-138 — قَدْ خَلَتْ مِن قَبْلِكُمْ سُنَنٌ فَسِيرُوا۟ فِى ٱلْأَرْضِ

*Qad khalat min qablikum sunanun fa-sīrū fi'l-arḍi fa'nẓurū kayfa kāna ʿāqibatu'l-mukadhdhibīn · Hādhā bayānun li'n-nāsi wa hudan wa mawʿiẓatun li'l-muttaqīn*

"**Ways have passed before you. So travel in the land and see what the end of the deniers was.** · **This is a clear statement for people, and a guidance and an admonition for those who guard themselves.**"

### سُنَن — root: س-ن-ن

The root **س-ن-ن**: **to make a thing flow evenly and continuously, to open a way.** The lexicographers explain the root by **continuous flow**: *sanna'l-māʾa* — he made the water flow. *Sinān* — a spear-point (levelled by whetting). *Sunna* — a way opened and travelled.

**And the word is here plural and indefinite: *sunanun* — "certain ways".**

**I record this as an observation about the word order:** the sentence conveys not a single law but **more than one way.** **And the verb is *khalat* — "have passed": it conveys what has been left behind.**

**And the form of the command must be recorded, and this is the verse's own distinctive side: *fa-sīrū fi'l-arḍi fa'nẓurū*.**

| Verb | The operation |
|---|---|
| *Sīrū* | **Travel** — the body |
| *Unẓurū* | **Look** — the eye |

**Both commands are concrete.** **That is, the verse does not transmit a piece of knowledge; it gives a method: go and see.**

**I record this as my own reading, and its ground is the concreteness of the two verbs:** the conclusion to be drawn is not stated. **What is stated is *where* the conclusion is to be found.** And that place is outside the text — in the land.

**And this is what the verse says to the present: the past is set down not as a narrative handed on but as a field to be gone to and looked at.** **I record this as a point of view and do not force it:** the work the verse commands is a method that coincides with the work done today by fields such as history and archaeology — **going to the remains and drawing a conclusion from them.** **This is recorded not as a claim of a "miracle" but as a direct consequence of the wording of the verse.**

**هَٰذَا بَيَانٌ لِّلنَّاسِ وَهُدًى وَمَوْعِظَةٌ لِّلْمُتَّقِينَ** — **and the word order must be recorded: three words, two addressees.**

| Word | To whom |
|---|---|
| *Bayān* | ***Li'n-nās*** — **to people** |
| *Hudan* + *mawʿiẓa* | ***Li'l-muttaqīn*** — **to those who guard themselves** |

**That is, the clear statement to everyone; the guidance and the admonition to the one who profits by it.** **This has the same structure as the note *hudan li'l-muttaqīn* at 2/2 in `002-bakara.md`, and I rely on that treatment.**

---

## 3/139-141 — وَلَا تَهِنُوا۟ وَلَا تَحْزَنُوا۟ وَأَنتُمُ ٱلْأَعْلَوْنَ إِن كُنتُم مُّؤْمِنِينَ

*Wa lā tahinū wa lā taḥzanū wa antumu'l-aʿlawna in kuntum muʾminīn · In yamsaskum qarḥun fa-qad massa'l-qawma qarḥun mithluh, wa tilka'l-ayyāmu nudāwiluhā bayna'n-nās, wa li-yaʿlama'llāhu'lladhīna āmanū wa yattakhidha minkum shuhadāʾ, wa'llāhu lā yuḥibbu'ẓ-ẓālimīn · Wa li-yumaḥḥiṣa'llāhu'lladhīna āmanū wa yamḥaqa'l-kāfirīn*

"**Do not slacken and do not grieve: you are the higher, if you are believers.** · **If a wound has touched you, a like wound touched that people too. Those days We turn about among people** — so that God may know those who believe and take witnesses from among you. God does not love the wrongdoers. · **And so that God may refine those who believe and wear away those who deny.**"

### و-ه-ن — on the basis of the series

The root **و-ه-ن**: **the coming undone of strength, the failing of capacity.** **The root was treated at 31/14 in `031-lokman.md` (*wahnan ʿalā wahn* — the mother's labour of carrying), at 29/41 in `029-ankebut.md` (*awhanu'l-buyūt* — the spider's house), at 8/18 in `008-enfal.md` (*mūhinu kaydi'l-kāfirīn*) and at 47/35 in `047-muhammed.md` (*fa-lā tahinū*). I rely on those treatments and do not repeat them.**

**And the root occurs twice in the sūra, and the relation of the two occurrences must be recorded:**

| Verse | Wording | Mood |
|---|---|---|
| **139** | *Wa lā **tahinū*** | **A prohibition** — "do not slacken" |
| **146** | *Fa-mā **wahanū** limā aṣābahum fī sabīli'llāh* | **A statement** — "they did not slacken" |

**A prohibition and an instance, seven verses apart.** This is a repetition verifiable within the sūra and was recorded in the table in the introduction.

**I record this as an observation about the word order:** having forbidden a thing, the sūra tells of those who kept to that prohibition. **And in verse 146 three verbs are counted: *mā wahanū*, *wa mā ḍaʿufū*, *wa ma'stakānū*.** **That is, the prohibition is given with one word, the instance with three.**

### وَأَنتُمُ ٱلْأَعْلَوْنَ — the keeping of the limit

**And this construction was treated at 47/35 in `047-muhammed.md`, and a limit was set there. I keep that limit here exactly.**

**I set the two verses side by side:**

| | **Muḥammad 47/35** | **Āl ʿImrān 3/139** |
|---|---|---|
| **Wording** | *Fa-lā tahinū wa tadʿū ila's-salmi **wa antumu'l-aʿlawn*** | *Wa lā tahinū wa lā taḥzanū **wa antumu'l-aʿlawn*** |
| **The same root** | و-ه-ن | و-ه-ن |
| **The note** | *Wa'llāhu **maʿakum*** | ***In kuntum muʾminīn*** |
| **Context** | A moment of an offer | After a defeat |

**And in both verses the construction does not stand *alone*: in one *wa'llāhu maʿakum* is added, in the other *in kuntum muʾminīn*.**

**Per the method (`USLUP.md`) I record this plainly and keep the limit set at 47/35 in `047-muhammed.md`:**

**One. The construction is qualified by a *conditional clause*: *in kuntum muʾminīn*.** **In Arabic the particle *in* conveys a condition.** **That is, the sentence does not convey an unconditional superiority.**

**Two. The construction stands in a context of *consolation*.** The sentence comes after two prohibitions: do not slacken, do not grieve. **That is, what is said is not a claim of superiority but a sentence of morale.**

**Three. And the very next verse says that this is not a permanent position: *wa tilka'l-ayyāmu nudāwiluhā bayna'n-nās*.** **That is, the construction blocks the drawing of a lasting superiority from it *one verse later*.**

**Four. In this commentary no claim of superiority for any community, people or state is drawn from this construction, and present-day politics is not entered.**

### وَتِلْكَ ٱلْأَيَّامُ نُدَاوِلُهَا بَيْنَ ٱلنَّاسِ — root: د-و-ل

**And this is one of the most important concepts of the block and must be treated in detail.**

The root **د-و-ل**. **The root was analysed at 59/7 in `059-hasr.md` (*kay lā yakūna dūlatan bayna'l-aghniyāʾi minkum*) and reference was made there to this verse. I rely on that treatment and take the description given there:**

> **The original sense of the root is a thing's *passing from hand to hand, coming round by turns, the turn changing*.** *Dāla yadūlu*: it came round, the turn changed. *Mudāwala*: to pass a thing round from hand to hand. **And the word we use in Turkish today, *devlet* (state), is from this root; within the word there is an idea of *taking turns*.** From the same root, in Turkish, *tedavül* — the circulation of money.

**And I spell out what belongs here.**

**One. The form of the verb must be recorded: *nudāwiluhā* — from Form III (*mufāʿala*).** **This form conveys in Arabic *reciprocity* and *doing by turns*.** *Qātala* — he fought back and forth. *Sāraʿa* — he raced. **And *dāwala* — he turned about by turns.**

**That is, the pattern of the verb says the sense of the root a second time: both the root and the form convey taking turns.**

**Two. The object of the verb must be recorded: *al-ayyām* — "the days".**

In Arabic the word *ayyām* conveys not only calendar days but also **the events a people has been through.** **And this usage is old in the Arabic language.** **That is, what is turned about is not time but *states*.**

**Three. And the subject of the verb must be recorded: *nudāwilu* — first person plural, that is, God.** **The turning about is not tied to a chance or to a law of nature.**

**Four. And the field of the circulation must be recorded: *bayna'n-nās* — "among people".**

**This note is the widest word of the verse. The sentence does not say "between the two sides" or "between you and them".**

**Now I record this as my own reading, and it is not binding; its ground is the picture of the root and of the form:**

**The concrete picture of the root is a *passing from hand to hand* — an object's not staying in one hand.** And the verse uses this picture for superiority. **That is, superiority is named not as a possession but as something that passes from hand to hand.**

**And this has two consequences, and both are in the verse's own context:**

| Consequence | Whom it looks to | Its ground in the verse |
|---|---|---|
| **For the defeated** | This state is not permanent | *Nudāwiluhā* — the turn comes round |
| **For the victor** | This state is not your property | *Bayna'n-nās* — among everyone |

**And the verse gives both consequences at once; it does not choose one.**

**And the placing of the sentence in the middle of an account of defeat is a fact of word order:** the preceding sentence says that the wound was mutual (*fa-qad massa'l-qawma qarḥun mithluh*), and the sentences after it count the purpose of the testing. **That is, the note of "taking turns" stands between the consolation and the purpose.**

**قَرْح** — root **ق-ر-ح**: **a wound.** **The word is read in the variant readings both as *qarḥ* and as *qurḥ*; the lexicographers distinguish one as the wound itself and the other as the pain of the wound, and some count them synonymous. I record the disagreement, impose no preference, and give no reciter's name.**

### يُمَحِّصَ / يَمْحَقَ — two verbs

The root **م-ح-ص**: **to pick a thing clean of its dross, to purify.** The lexicographers give as an instance the refining of gold in fire.

The root **م-ح-ق**: **to destroy by diminishing, to efface.** **And the same root occurred at 2/276 in `002-bakara.md`: *yamḥaqu'llāhu'r-ribā*.** **Two occurrences, the same verb.**

**And the setting of the two verbs side by side must be recorded:**

| Verb | The operation | To whom |
|---|---|---|
| *Yumaḥḥiṣ* | **Refining** — separating out what is foreign within | To those who believe |
| *Yamḥaq* | **Wearing away** — finishing by diminishing | To those who deny |

**Both verbs convey a *diminishing*; but one purifies and the other consumes.** **I record this as an observation about the words: the same operation (taking away) gives two different outcomes.**

---

## 3/142-143 — أَمْ حَسِبْتُمْ أَن تَدْخُلُوا۟ ٱلْجَنَّةَ وَلَمَّا يَعْلَمِ ٱللَّهُ ٱلَّذِينَ جَٰهَدُوا۟ مِنكُمْ

*Am ḥasibtum an tadkhulu'l-jannata wa lammā yaʿlami'llāhu'lladhīna jāhadū minkum wa yaʿlama'ṣ-ṣābirīn · Wa la-qad kuntum tamannawna'l-mawta min qabli an talqawhu fa-qad raʾaytumūhu wa antum tanẓurūn*

"**Or did you suppose that you would enter the garden before God had marked out those of you who strive and those who are steadfast?** · **You were indeed wishing for death before you met it. Now you have seen it — and you were looking on.**"

### حَسِبْتُمْ — the line of the root in the sūra

The root **ح-س-ب** occurs six times in the sūra, and four of the occurrences were recorded in the table in the introduction (169, 178, 180, 188). **Two further occurrences must be added here: 78 (*li-taḥsabūhu*) and 142 (*am ḥasibtum*).**

| Verse | Wording | Who supposes |
|---|---|---|
| **78** | *Li-**taḥsabū**hu mina'l-kitāb* | **The one addressed** — another makes him suppose |
| **142** | *Am **ḥasibtum** an tadkhulu'l-jannata* | **The one addressed** — he supposes it himself |
| **169** | *Wa lā **taḥsabanna**'lladhīna qutilū… amwātā* | **The one addressed** — a false supposition is forbidden |
| **178** | *Wa lā **yaḥsabanna**'lladhīna kafarū* | **The other side** |
| **180** | *Wa lā **yaḥsabanna**'lladhīna yabkhalūn* | **The miserly** |
| **188** | *Lā **taḥsabanna**'lladhīna yafraḥūn* | **The one addressed** — about others |

**Six occurrences, in a single sūra.** This is a fact verifiable within the sūra and is the completed form of the table in the introduction.

**I record this as my own reading, and it is not binding:** the sūra uses one verb of "supposing" in six separate places, and each time it corrects a false reckoning. **And all the reckonings corrected are of the same kind: drawing a conclusion from what is seen.**

### وَلَمَّا يَعْلَمِ ٱللَّهُ — the sense of the verb of knowing

**There is a difficulty in the sentence, and the classical sources take it up: does an event have to occur for God to know?**

| Reading | How *yaʿlam* is understood |
|---|---|
| 1 | **The coming to pass of the knowledge in fact** — that is, the realising of what the knowledge requires |
| 2 | In the sense of ***marking out*** — the knowledge is already there, the separating comes about |
| 3 | **Making visible** — the knowledge comes out for others |

**All three are transmitted; I do not impose a preference.**

**But one datum of the text can be recorded:** in the same sūra verse 179 devotes a sentence directly to this question — *mā kāna'llāhu li-yadhara'l-muʾminīna ʿalā mā antum ʿalayhi ḥattā **yamīza**'l-khabītha mina'ṭ-ṭayyib*. **And the verb used there is *yamīza*: to separate.** **That is, the sūra supplies its own word.** This is a link verifiable within the sūra.

### 3/143 — what was wished for and what was seen

**And the word order of the verse must be recorded: *fa-qad raʾaytumūhu wa antum tanẓurūn*.**

**Both verbs convey seeing, but they are not the same:**

| Verb | Root | What it conveys |
|---|---|---|
| *Raʾaytumūhu* | **ر-أ-ي** | **You saw** — a seeing accomplished |
| *Tanẓurūn* | **ن-ظ-ر** | **You were looking** — a looking still going on |

**The lexicographers distinguish the two: *naẓar* is looking, *ruʾya* is seeing.** A person may look and not see.

**And the verse uses both at once.** **I record this as an observation about the words:** the sentence says "you saw" and adds to it "you were looking". **Two verbs together set up a record of witnessing that leaves no excuse.**

---

## 3/144 — وَمَا مُحَمَّدٌ إِلَّا رَسُولٌ قَدْ خَلَتْ مِن قَبْلِهِ ٱلرُّسُلُ

*Wa mā Muḥammadun illā rasūlun qad khalat min qablihi'r-rusul, a-fa-in māta aw qutila'nqalabtum ʿalā aʿqābikum, wa man yanqalib ʿalā ʿaqibayhi fa-lan yaḍurra'llāha shayʾā, wa sa-yajzi'llāhu'sh-shākirīn*

"**Muḥammad is only a messenger; messengers have passed away before him.** If then he dies or is killed, **will you turn back on your heels?** Whoever turns back on his heels does God no harm at all. **God will reward the thankful.**"

---

**This verse will be treated in detail. The reason is this: it is the only verse in the sūra in which the Prophet's name occurs, and that name occurs inside a sentence of limit.**

### Where the name occurs

**The name *Muḥammad* occurs in four places in the Qurʾān. And the sole occurrence in this sūra is here.**

**And the pattern of the sentence must be recorded: *mā … illā* — negation and exception, that is, *ḥaṣr*.** **This pattern was used at verse 65 as well** (*wa mā unzilati't-Tawrātu wa'l-Injīlu illā min baʿdih*). **Two occurrences, at two different pieces of work; and both set up a limitation.**

**I record this as an observation about the word order:** immediately after naming the name, the sentence uses a particle of limitation. ***Mā Muḥammadun illā rasūl*** — "only a messenger". **And *rasūlun* is indefinite: "a messenger", not the messenger.**

### قَدْ خَلَتْ مِن قَبْلِهِ ٱلرُّسُلُ — a qualifying clause

**And the qualifier of the indefinite is a clause: *qad khalat min qablihi'r-rusul* — "messengers have passed away before him".**

The root **خ-ل-و**: **to pass away, to become empty.** **And the same verb had occurred seven verses earlier in the sūra: *qad khalat min qablikum sunan* (137).** **Two occurrences, the same verb:**

| Verse | What has passed away |
|---|---|
| **137** | ***Sunanun*** — ways, laws |
| **144** | ***Al-rusul*** — the messengers |

**This is a repetition verifiable within the sūra. And I record this as my own reading, and it is not binding:** the sūra uses the same verb twice, and in both it says **that an ongoing order has been at work before as well.** **One for events, the other for persons.**

### أَفَإِي۟ن مَّاتَ أَوْ قُتِلَ — the counting of two possibilities together

**And the sentence names two possibilities at once: *māta aw qutila* — "if he dies or is killed".**

**I record this as an observation about the word order:** the sentence does not report an event; **it sets up a condition.** And the condition has two branches. **And both are tied to the same consequence** — that is, the distinction does not change the answer given.

**And the same pair will occur twice more in the sūra: *wa la-in qutiltum fī sabīli'llāhi aw muttum* (157) and *wa la-in muttum aw qutiltum* (158).** **Three occurrences, and in the last two the order is reversed.** This is a repetition verifiable within the sūra.

### ٱنقَلَبْتُمْ عَلَىٰٓ أَعْقَٰبِكُمْ — the description itself

The root **ق-ل-ب**: **to turn, to turn upside down.** The root **ع-ق-ب**: **heel; and from this, the one who comes after.**

**And the description is concrete and was treated at 2/143 in `002-bakara.md`** (*man yanqalibu ʿalā ʿaqibayh*) **and this was recorded there:**

> "**A concrete picture: a person walking turns first on his heel in order to turn back. The expression conveys a change of direction by a movement of the body.**"

**I rely on that treatment.** **And I compare the two verses:**

| | **al-Baqara 2/143** | **Āl ʿImrān 3/144** |
|---|---|---|
| **Context** | **The change of the qibla** | **The possibility of the messenger's death** |
| **Attachment to what is tested** | Habit or the command | **The person or the message** |
| **Mood** | *Man yanqalibu* — **third person**, a statement | *Inqalabtum* — **second person plural**, a question |

**And the two verses use the same description in two different testings.** **I record this as my own reading, and its ground is the comparison of the two contexts:** in both places the question asked is the same — **is what one is attached to the means or the thing itself?** In al-Baqara the means is a direction, here a person.

### The work the verse does

**And now I record what the verse does as a whole.**

**The verse does three things in succession:**

| Step | Wording | Its work |
|---|---|---|
| **1** | *Mā Muḥammadun illā rasūl* | **It defines the position** — a messenger |
| **2** | *Qad khalat min qablihi'r-rusul* | **It says that he is not unique** |
| **3** | *A-fa-in māta aw qutila'nqalabtum* | **It asks where the attachment has been set** |

**And the third step is in the interrogative — not a ruling.** **That is, the verse makes no accusation; it voices a possibility and puts it to the one addressed.**

**I record this as my own reading, and it is not binding:** the distinction the verse sets up is between **the messenger and the one who sends.** A messenger is temporary — messengers passed away before him too. **And the validity of a message cannot be tied to the lifetime of the one who brings it.**

**And the last half of the sentence completes this: *fa-lan yaḍurra'llāha shayʾā*** — "**he does God no harm at all.**" **That is, the turning back of the one who turns back affects not the message but himself.**

**The same note will come twice more in the sūra: *lā yaḍurrūna'llāha shayʾā* (176) and *lan yaḍurrū'llāha shayʾā* (177).** **Three occurrences, in a single sūra.** This is a repetition verifiable within the sūra.

**Per the method (`USLUP.md`) I record this: I transmit no report about the event and the words said in connection with this verse as related in the sources, and I give no personal names.** The only thing that can be recorded is this: **the classical sources place this verse in the context of Uḥud, and this identification is widespread.** **The verse itself gives no event and no personal name, and this silence is preserved.**

### What this says to the present

**The distinction the verse sets up is one that works in every age: the tying of an idea or a work to the person who carries it.**

When a work is built on one person, the work goes when that person goes. **The verse's question (*a-fa-in māta… inqalabtum*) probes exactly this point.**

**And the measure the verse gives does not belittle a person.** What it says is that the messenger **is a messenger** — that is, that what he brought is greater than himself. **This is a sentence that does not devalue the carrier; it puts what is carried first.**

---

## 3/145-148 — وَمَا كَانَ لِنَفْسٍ أَن تَمُوتَ إِلَّا بِإِذْنِ ٱللَّهِ كِتَٰبًا مُّؤَجَّلًا

*Wa mā kāna li-nafsin an tamūta illā bi-idhni'llāhi kitāban muʾajjalā, wa man yurid thawāba'd-dunyā nuʾtihī minhā wa man yurid thawāba'l-ākhirati nuʾtihī minhā, wa sa-najzi'sh-shākirīn · Wa ka-ayyin min nabiyyin qātala maʿahū ribbiyyūna kathīrun fa-mā wahanū limā aṣābahum fī sabīli'llāhi wa mā ḍaʿufū wa ma'stakānū, wa'llāhu yuḥibbu'ṣ-ṣābirīn · Wa mā kāna qawlahum illā an qālū rabbana'ghfir lanā dhunūbanā wa isrāfanā fī amrinā wa thabbit aqdāmanā wa'nṣurnā ʿala'l-qawmi'l-kāfirīn · Fa-ātāhumu'llāhu thawāba'd-dunyā wa ḥusna thawābi'l-ākhira, wa'llāhu yuḥibbu'l-muḥsinīn*

"**No soul dies except by God's leave: it is a writ whose term is set.** Whoever wants the reward of this world, We give him of it; whoever wants the reward of the hereafter, We give him of it. **And We shall reward the thankful.** · **How many a prophet has fought, and with him many *rabbānī*s; they did not slacken at what befell them in the way of God, nor did they weaken, nor did they submit.** God loves the steadfast. · **Their only word was: 'Our Lord! Forgive us our sins and our excess in our affair, make our feet firm, and help us against the people who deny.'** · **So God gave them the reward of this world and the good reward of the hereafter.** God loves those who do good."

### كِتَٰبًا مُّؤَجَّلًا — the work of two words

**ك-ت-ب** — a writ. **أ-ج-ل** — with a term set. **And the construction: "a writ whose term is set".**

**I record this as an observation about the word order, and its being placed inside a block about a defeat is significant:** the sentence says that death is **not tied to battle.** **And this is the first form of a note that will be repeated at verses 156 and 168** — there the saying "had they been with us they would not have died" will be transmitted and refuted.

### 3/146 — three verbs

*Fa-mā **wahanū**… wa mā **ḍaʿufū**… wa ma'**stakānū***

| Verb | Root | What it conveys |
|---|---|---|
| *Wahanū* | **و-ه-ن** | **The coming undone of strength** — the counterpart of the prohibition at 139 |
| *Ḍaʿufū* | **ض-ع-ف** | **Weakening** |
| *Istakānū* | **س-ك-ن** | **Submitting, cowering** — the picture of the root is *stillness* |

**All three verbs are negated. And the field of the three differs: the first is *capacity*, the second *strength*, the third *bearing*.**

**I record this as an observation about the words:** the list moves from inside outward — first the coming undone within, then the weakness in the body, then the submission shown outwardly.

**And the root of the verb *istakānū* is س-ك-ن, the same root as *maskana* in verse 112.** **Two occurrences, the same root: one in a ruling, the other in a negation.**

**رِبِّيُّون** — there is disagreement over the reading and the sense of the word, and it is not to be concealed:

| Reading | What *ribbiyyūn* is |
|---|---|
| 1 | From the root **ر-ب-ب** — those attached to the Lord; close to the *rabbāniyyīn* of verse 79 |
| 2 | **Numerous companies** — *ribba*: a large company |
| 3 | **Scholars, those who know** |

**All three are transmitted; I do not impose a preference. The word is also read with different vowels in the variant readings; I give no reciter's name.**

### 3/147 — the structure of the prayer

**And the word order of the prayer must be recorded, because its sequence is striking:**

| # | Request | Field |
|---|---|---|
| **1** | *Ighfir lanā **dhunūbanā*** | **Sin** |
| **2** | *Wa **isrāfanā fī amrinā*** | **Excess in the affair** |
| **3** | *Wa **thabbit aqdāmanā*** | **Steadfastness** |
| **4** | *Wa'**nṣurnā** ʿala'l-qawmi'l-kāfirīn* | **Help** |

**And the real note is this: the prayer does not *begin* by asking for help. Help is fourth in order.**

The first two requests concern their own failings. And the second must be recorded separately: *isrāfanā **fī amrinā*** — "**our excess in our affair**".

The root **س-ر-ف**: **to overstep the bound, to lose the measure.** **And the note is *fī amrinā* — that is, not in worship but in *the affair*.**

**I record this as my own reading, and its ground is the order of the prayer:** the prayer names **its own share** before asking for help from outside. **And this coincides with the axis recorded in the introduction to the sūra** — in three separate places (7, 152, 165) the sūra asked that a person see his own share. **This prayer is that share spoken in the person's own mouth.**

**And *fa-ātāhumu'llāhu thawāba'd-dunyā wa ḥusna thawābi'l-ākhira* (148) — the answer gives both of the two choices counted at verse 145 at once.** **That is, the two recompenses counted separately at 145 are given together at 148.** This is a link verifiable from the text.

---

## 3/149-151 — إِن تُطِيعُوا۟ ٱلَّذِينَ كَفَرُوا۟ يَرُدُّوكُمْ عَلَىٰٓ أَعْقَٰبِكُمْ

*Yā ayyuha'lladhīna āmanū in tuṭīʿu'lladhīna kafarū yaruddūkum ʿalā aʿqābikum fa-tanqalibū khāsirīn · Bali'llāhu mawlākum wa huwa khayru'n-nāṣirīn · Sa-nulqī fī qulūbi'lladhīna kafaru'r-ruʿba bimā ashrakū bi'llāhi mā lam yunazzil bihī sulṭānā, wa maʾwāhumu'n-nār, wa biʾsa mathwa'ẓ-ẓālimīn*

"**You who believe! If you obey those who deny, they will turn you back on your heels; then you will turn back as losers.** · **No: God is your patron, and He is the best of helpers.** · **We shall cast terror into the hearts of those who deny** — because they associated with God what He sent down no warrant for."

### أَعْقَٰبِكُمْ — the third occurrence in five verses

**And the description of *aʿqāb* occurs three times at short intervals in the sūra:**

| Verse | Wording | The cause |
|---|---|---|
| **144** | *Inqalabtum ʿalā **aʿqābikum*** | The possibility of **the messenger's going** |
| **144** | *Wa man yanqalib ʿalā **ʿaqibayh*** | **A general ruling** |
| **149** | *Yaruddūkum ʿalā **aʿqābikum*** | **Obeying another** |

**Three occurrences, within six verses.** This is a repetition verifiable within the sūra.

**And the difference must be recorded: at 144 the one who turns back turns of himself; at 149 another turns him back (*yaruddūkum*).** **The same movement, two different agents.**

**And the same structure was at verse 100: *yaruddūkum baʿda īmānikum kāfirīn* — there the subject was a group from among the People of the Book, here those who deny.** **Two verses use the same verb with two different subjects.** This is a repetition verifiable within the sūra.

**بَلِ ٱللَّهُ مَوْلَىٰكُمْ** — **the particle *bal* leaves the preceding sentence and puts another in its place.** **And the word *mawlā* is from the root و-ل-ي** — at verse 68 it occurred as *waliyyu'l-muʾminīn*. **Two occurrences, the same root.**

---

## 3/152-153 — حَتَّىٰٓ إِذَا فَشِلْتُمْ وَتَنَٰزَعْتُمْ فِى ٱلْأَمْرِ وَعَصَيْتُم مِّنۢ بَعْدِ مَآ أَرَىٰكُم مَّا تُحِبُّونَ

*Wa la-qad ṣadaqakumu'llāhu waʿdahū idh taḥussūnahum bi-idhnihī ḥattā idhā fashiltum wa tanāzaʿtum fi'l-amri wa ʿaṣaytum min baʿdi mā arākum mā tuḥibbūn, minkum man yurīdu'd-dunyā wa minkum man yurīdu'l-ākhira, thumma ṣarafakum ʿanhum li-yabtaliyakum, wa la-qad ʿafā ʿankum, wa'llāhu dhū faḍlin ʿala'l-muʾminīn · Idh tuṣʿidūna wa lā talwūna ʿalā aḥadin wa'r-rasūlu yadʿūkum fī ukhrākum fa-athābakum ghamman bi-ghammin li-kaylā taḥzanū ʿalā mā fātakum wa lā mā aṣābakum, wa'llāhu khabīrun bimā taʿmalūn*

"**God had indeed made good His promise to you: by His leave you were cutting them down. Until you slackened, and quarrelled over the affair, and disobeyed after He had shown you what you love.** Among you were those who wanted this world and those who wanted the hereafter. **Then He turned you away from them, to test you; and He has indeed pardoned you.** God is bountiful to the believers. · **When you were climbing away, turning back for no one, while the messenger was calling you from behind you.** So He repaid you with grief upon grief — **that you might not sorrow over what you had missed nor over what befell you.** God is aware of what you do."

---

**This verse is the second of the three sentences recorded in the introduction as the axis of the sūra, and it carries the densest textual data in the block. It will be treated in detail.**

### Three verbs, in one sentence

**At the centre of the verse stand three verbs, and all three are in the second person plural:**

| # | Verb | Root | What |
|---|---|---|---|
| **1** | *Fashiltum* | **ف-ش-ل** | **You slackened** |
| **2** | *Tanāzaʿtum fi'l-amr* | **ن-ز-ع** | **You quarrelled** |
| **3** | *ʿAṣaytum* | **ع-ص-ي** | **You disobeyed** |

**And the subject of all three is the one addressed. This was recorded in the introduction to the sūra.**

### A comparison with 8/46 — one of the strongest textual data of the sūra

**At 8/45-47 in `008-enfal.md` this chain was treated, and I rely on it:**

> *Wa lā **tanāzaʿū** fa-**tafshalū** wa **tadhhaba rīḥukum***
>
> "**Do not quarrel with one another; or you will slacken and your wind will go.**"
>
> **And the chain was recorded there:** *tanāzuʿ* (quarrelling) → *fashal* (slackening) → *dhahābu'r-rīḥ* (the going of strength). **Three steps, in one sentence.**

**And now I set the two verses side by side. This is a datum verifiable from the text and must be given in a table:**

| | **al-Anfāl 8/46** | **Āl ʿImrān 3/152** |
|---|---|---|
| **Mood** | ***A prohibition*** — *lā tanāzaʿū* | ***A statement*** — *tanāzaʿtum* |
| **Time** | **Future** — before it happens | **Past** — after it has happened |
| **ن-ز-ع** | *Tanāzaʿū* — **forbidden** | *Tanāzaʿtum* — **come to pass** |
| **ف-ش-ل** | *Tafshalū* — warned of **as a consequence** | *Fashiltum* — **come to pass** |
| **Order** | Quarrelling **first**, slackening **after** | Slackening **first**, quarrelling **after** |
| **The third element** | *Wa tadhhaba **rīḥukum*** — the going of strength | *Wa **ʿaṣaytum*** — disobeying |
| **The closing sentence** | *Wa'ṣbirū inna'llāha maʿa'ṣ-ṣābirīn* | *Wa la-qad **ʿafā ʿankum*** |

**I draw four observations from the table, and all four can be verified from the two texts.**

**One. The two verses use the same two roots: ن-ز-ع and ف-ش-ل.** **And the places in the Qurʾān where these two roots stand together are few.** **In al-Anfāl in the imperative mood, in Āl ʿImrān as an accomplished fact.**

**Two. Al-Anfāl is a *warning*; Āl ʿImrān is a *record*.** One says "if you do this, that will happen"; the other says "you did this".

**Three. The order is reversed in the two verses, and this must be recorded.** In al-Anfāl the quarrelling is the cause and the slackening the consequence (*lā tanāzaʿū **fa**-tafshalū* — the *fāʾ* conveys consequence). In Āl ʿImrān, on the other hand, slackening is counted first and quarrelling after.

**I record this as my own reading, and it is not binding:** the two orders do not conflict. Al-Anfāl sets up a **rule** — quarrelling leads to slackening. Āl ʿImrān tells of an **event**, and in the event the two happened together. **The sequence of a rule and the sequence of an event need not be the same.**

**Four. And the most important difference is at the end.** Al-Anfāl ends with a command: *wa'ṣbirū*. **Āl ʿImrān, by contrast, ends with a sentence of pardon: *wa la-qad ʿafā ʿankum*.**

**I record this as my own reading, and its ground is the close of the two verses:** a command is put at the end of a warning, because there is still something to be done. **A pardon is put at the end of a record, because what has been done cannot be taken back.**

**And this is a structure repeated throughout the sūra's Uḥud block, and it is seen in three places:**

| Verse | The failing recorded | Immediately after |
|---|---|---|
| **122** | *Hammat ṭāʾifatāni minkum an tafshalā* | ***Wa'llāhu waliyyuhumā*** |
| **152** | *Fashiltum wa tanāzaʿtum wa ʿaṣaytum* | ***Wa la-qad ʿafā ʿankum*** |
| **155** | *Alladhīna tawallaw minkum* | ***Wa la-qad ʿafa'llāhu ʿanhum*** |

**Three occurrences, in a single block.** This is a weave verifiable within the sūra.

### فِى ٱلْأَمْرِ — the subject of the quarrel

**And the subject of the quarrel is named: *fi'l-amr* — "over the affair".**

**And the word *al-amr* occurs four times in this block, and each time at a different piece of work:**

| Verse | Wording | What |
|---|---|---|
| **128** | *Laysa laka **mina'l-amri** shayʾ* | **The affair** — the decision |
| **152** | *Wa tanāzaʿtum **fi'l-amr*** | **The affair** — the subject of the quarrel |
| **154** | *Hal lanā **mina'l-amri** min shayʾ* | **The affair** — as a question |
| **154** | *Qul inna'l-**amra** kullahū li'llāh* | **The affair** — the answer |
| **159** | *Wa shāwirhum **fi'l-amr*** | **The affair** — the subject of consultation |

**Five occurrences, within thirty-two verses.** This is a fact verifiable within the sūra.

**And I record this as my own reading, and it is not binding:** the same word conveys three separate things in the block — the affair quarrelled over, the owner of the decision, and the affair to be consulted about. **And the block sets these in an order:** the affair was quarrelled over (152), it was asked and answered whose the affair is (154), and consultation about the affair was commanded (159).

### مِن بَعْدِ مَآ أَرَىٰكُم مَّا تُحِبُّونَ

**And the pattern of the note is familiar: *min baʿdi mā…* — for the fourth time in the sūra.**

| Verse | *Min baʿdi mā* — after what |
|---|---|
| **19** | *Jāʾahumu'l-**ʿilm*** |
| **61** | *Jāʾaka mina'l-**ʿilm*** |
| **105** | *Jāʾahumu'l-**bayyināt*** |
| **152** | ***Arākum mā tuḥibbūn*** |

**And the fourth stands apart from the others: what comes here is not a knowledge or a proof but the realising of what was wanted.**

**I record this as my own reading, and its ground is the four occurrences of the pattern:** the sūra uses the same pattern both for the coming of knowledge and for **the meeting of a wish.** **And after both the same thing happens: a breaking down.** **That is, the sūra says with the same pattern that both knowledge and success are a testing.**

### مِنكُم مَّن يُرِيدُ ٱلدُّنْيَا وَمِنكُم مَّن يُرِيدُ ٱلْءَاخِرَةَ

**And the division is set up this time within the community itself. And the particle is again *min*.**

**I record this as an observation about the word order, and it is the sūra's method:** the sūra had divided the People of the Book with *min* (69, 72, 75, 78, 100, 113, 199); **now it divides those it addresses with the same particle.**

**And this is the textual ground of the rule this commentary has kept throughout the sūra: the division is not a measure applied to outsiders; it is a measure the text applies to every community.**

### 3/153 — the scene of the flight

*Idh **tuṣʿidūna** wa lā **talwūna** ʿalā aḥadin wa'r-rasūlu **yadʿūkum** fī **ukhrākum***

| Verb / word | Root | What it conveys |
|---|---|---|
| *Tuṣʿidūn* | **ص-ع-د** | **To go up** — to climb the slope |
| *Lā talwūna ʿalā aḥad* | **ل-و-ي** | **Turning to look at no one** |
| *Yadʿūkum* | **د-ع-و** | **Calling** |
| *Ukhrākum* | **أ-خ-ر** | **Your rear** — the hindmost |

**And the root *ل-و-ي* must be recorded: the root occurred at verse 78 (*yalwūna alsinatahum*).** **Two occurrences, the same root, two different pieces of work:**

| Verse | Wording | What is twisted |
|---|---|---|
| **78** | *Yalwūna **alsinatahum** bi'l-kitāb* | **The tongue** — bending the recitation |
| **153** | *Wa lā **talwūna** ʿalā aḥad* | **The neck** — turning to look back |

**The concrete sense of the root is at work in both: to twist, to turn.** **In one the tongue is twisted, in the other the neck is not turned.** This is a repetition verifiable within the sūra.

**غَمًّۢا بِغَمٍّ** — root **غ-م-م**: **to cover, to overspread.** *Ghamām* — cloud (what covers the sky). **And *ghamm* is the grief that covers a person's inside.**

**And the construction sets two griefs one upon the other: *ghamman bi-ghamm*.** **And there is disagreement over its syntax, and it is not to be concealed:**

| Reading | How *ghamman bi-ghamm* is taken |
|---|---|
| 1 | **Grief upon grief** — two griefs one after the other |
| 2 | **A grief in return for a grief** — the *bāʾ* conveys requital |
| 3 | The grief's **making the earlier grief forgotten** |

**All three are transmitted; I do not impose a preference.**

**And the ground of the sentence must be recorded: *li-kaylā taḥzanū ʿalā mā fātakum wa lā mā aṣābakum*** — "**that you might not sorrow over what you missed nor over what befell you**".

**I record this as an observation about the word order:** the sentence separates two distinct sorrows — over **what was not obtained** and over **what came upon them**. **And both are grammatically negated.**

---

## 3/154-155 — ثُمَّ أَنزَلَ عَلَيْكُم مِّنۢ بَعْدِ ٱلْغَمِّ أَمَنَةً نُّعَاسًا

*Thumma anzala ʿalaykum min baʿdi'l-ghammi amanatan nuʿāsan yaghshā ṭāʾifatan minkum, wa ṭāʾifatun qad ahammat'hum anfusuhum yaẓunnūna bi'llāhi ghayra'l-ḥaqqi ẓanna'l-jāhiliyya, yaqūlūna hal lanā mina'l-amri min shayʾ, qul inna'l-amra kullahū li'llāh… · Inna'lladhīna tawallaw minkum yawma'ltaqa'l-jamʿāni innama'stazallahumu'sh-shayṭānu bi-baʿḍi mā kasabū, wa la-qad ʿafa'llāhu ʿanhum, inna'llāha ghafūrun ḥalīm*

"**Then, after the grief, He sent down upon you a security, a drowsiness that overcame a party of you.** Another party had been made anxious by their own selves: **they thought of God what is not the truth, the thought of the age of ignorance. 'Have we any part in this affair?' they were saying.** Say: '**The affair belongs entirely to God.**' … · **As for those of you who turned away on the day the two hosts met, Satan sought to make them slip because of some of what they had earned. And God has indeed pardoned them.** God is much-forgiving, most forbearing."

### أَمَنَةً نُّعَاسًا — the setting of two words side by side

**أ-م-ن** — security. **ن-ع-س** — light sleep, drowsiness.

**And the word order must be recorded: *amanatan* first, *nuʿāsan* after.** The second is the explanation of the first (an appositive substitute). **That is, what was sent down is "security", and the visible form of that security is a drowsiness.**

**I record this as an observation:** the sentence tells an inward state by **a sign visible in the body.** Fear drives sleep away; the verse counts the coming of sleep as the mark of security.

**And the same event is told at 8/11 in `008-enfal.md` as well** (*idh yughashshīkumu'n-nuʿāsa amanatan minh*) **and there the order of the words is reversed: *nuʿās* first, *amana* after.** **The two sūras use the same two words in reverse order.** This is a fact verifiable from the text.

### ظَنَّ ٱلْجَٰهِلِيَّةِ — the construction itself

The root **ج-ه-ل**: **not to know; and for the lexicographers also *to act in anger, without measure*.** **That is, the opposite of the root is not only *ʿilm* but also *ḥilm*.**

**And the construction occurs rarely in the Qurʾān.** **And its use here is of a *thought*: *ẓanna'l-jāhiliyya*.**

**I record this as an observation about the word:** the construction is used not as the name of a period but as the name of **a way of thinking.** **And what it is about is stated in the verse: *yaẓunnūna bi'llāhi ghayra'l-ḥaqq* — about God.**

### هَلْ لَّنَا مِنَ ٱلْأَمْرِ مِن شَىْءٍ — question and answer

**And the wording of the question and the answer must be recorded, because they are built with the same words:**

| | Wording |
|---|---|
| **The question** | *Hal lanā **mina'l-amri min shayʾ*** |
| **The answer** | *Qul inna'l-**amra kullahū li'llāh*** |

**And the same construction (*mina'l-amri… shayʾ*) had occurred twenty-six verses earlier, at verse 128: *laysa laka mina'l-amri shayʾun*.**

**Two occurrences, the same construction, two different addressees:**

| Verse | To whom it is said | Mood |
|---|---|---|
| **128** | **To the Prophet** | **A declaration** |
| **154** | **To those who ask** — as an answer | **Question and answer** |

**This is a repetition verifiable within the sūra. And I record this as my own reading, and it is not binding:** the same limit is said first to the person in the highest position, then to those who ask. **That is, the answer rests not on who the questioner is but on no one's having any part in it.**

---

## 3/156-158 — لَا تَكُونُوا۟ كَٱلَّذِينَ كَفَرُوا۟ وَقَالُوا۟ لِإِخْوَٰنِهِمْ … لَّوْ كَانُوا۟ عِندَنَا مَا مَاتُوا۟ وَمَا قُتِلُوا۟

*Yā ayyuha'lladhīna āmanū lā takūnū ka'lladhīna kafarū wa qālū li-ikhwānihim idhā ḍarabū fi'l-arḍi aw kānū ghuzzan law kānū ʿindanā mā mātū wa mā qutilū, li-yajʿala'llāhu dhālika ḥasratan fī qulūbihim, wa'llāhu yuḥyī wa yumīt, wa'llāhu bimā taʿmalūna baṣīr · Wa la-in qutiltum fī sabīli'llāhi aw muttum la-maghfiratun mina'llāhi wa raḥmatun khayrun mimmā yajmaʿūn · Wa la-in muttum aw qutiltum la-ila'llāhi tuḥsharūn*

"**You who believe! Do not be like those who deny and who say of their brothers who travelled in the land or went out to fight: 'Had they been with us they would not have died and would not have been killed'** — that God may make this a regret in their hearts. **It is God who gives life and gives death**… · **If you are killed in the way of God, or die, a forgiveness and a mercy from God are better than all they gather.** · **Whether you die or are killed, it is to God that you will be gathered.**"

### The logic of the saying and its refutation

***Law kānū ʿindanā mā mātū wa mā qutilū*** — "**had they been with us they would not have died and would not have been killed**".

**And the saying binds two things together: the place one is in, and death.**

**And its refutation is inside the verse, and it has two steps:**

| Step | Wording | What it does |
|---|---|---|
| **1** | *Li-yajʿala'llāhu dhālika **ḥasratan fī qulūbihim*** | What the saying does **to them** |
| **2** | *Wa'llāhu **yuḥyī wa yumīt*** | **To whom** death belongs |

**And the first step is striking. I record this as an observation about the word order:** the verse does not first refute the saying logically; **it says what it does to the one who says it.** *Ḥasra* — the burning felt over what has been missed. **That is, the sentence of "if only" produces a burning instead of giving consolation.**

**And its logical refutation had already been set up at verse 145: *wa mā kāna li-nafsin an tamūta illā bi-idhni'llāhi kitāban muʾajjalā*.** **Two verses, eleven verses apart, take up the same matter from two directions: one the rule, the other the outcome of the saying.**

**And the same saying will come once more at verse 168, in another mouth: *law aṭāʿūnā mā qutilū*.** **Two occurrences, in a single block.** This is a repetition verifiable within the sūra.

### 157-158: the reversal of the order

| Verse | Order |
|---|---|
| **157** | *Qutiltum* **aw** *muttum* — **being killed first** |
| **158** | *Muttum* **aw** *qutiltum* — **dying first** |

**And the ends of the two verses differ as well:**

| Verse | The close | What it conveys |
|---|---|---|
| **157** | *La-maghfiratun mina'llāhi wa raḥmatun **khayrun mimmā yajmaʿūn*** | **A comparison** — with what is gathered |
