| **158** | *La-**ila'llāhi tuḥsharūn*** | **An arrival** — where to |

**I record this as an observation about the word order:** the two verses give the same two verbs in reverse order, and one states **the recompense**, the other **the destination**. **And the reversed order shows that the two sentences are not the same as each other.**

---

## 3/159 — فَبِمَا رَحْمَةٍ مِّنَ ٱللَّهِ لِنتَ لَهُمْ وَلَوْ كُنتَ فَظًّا غَلِيظَ ٱلْقَلْبِ لَٱنفَضُّوا۟ مِنْ حَوْلِكَ

*Fa-bimā raḥmatin mina'llāhi linta lahum, wa law kunta faẓẓan ghalīẓa'l-qalbi la'nfaḍḍū min ḥawlik, fa'fu ʿanhum wa'staghfir lahum wa shāwirhum fi'l-amr, fa-idhā ʿazamta fa-tawakkal ʿala'llāh, inna'llāha yuḥibbu'l-mutawakkilīn*

"**By a mercy from God you were gentle with them. Had you been harsh and hard of heart, they would have scattered from around you.** **So pardon them, ask forgiveness for them, and consult them in the affair.** And when you have decided, rely on God; God loves those who rely on Him."

---

**This verse will be treated in detail. The reason is this: immediately after an account of a defeat and a disobedience, it is a verse that tells the one it addresses to *pardon*, to *ask forgiveness* and to *consult*. And it is the only place where the command to consult occurs.**

### فَبِمَا رَحْمَةٍ — the opening of the sentence

**And the lexicographers differ over the *mā* in the opening, and the disagreement is not to be concealed:**

| Reading | What *mā* is |
|---|---|
| 1 | ***Redundant*** — for emphasis; "by a mercy from God" |
| 2 | **An indefinite qualified noun** — "by such a mercy that…" |

**Both are transmitted; I do not impose a preference.**

**And a fact of word order must be recorded: the sentence ties the gentleness first to its *cause*.** *Fa-bimā raḥmatin mina'llāh* — the cause stands at the head of the sentence; *linta lahum* comes after. **That is, the source is stated before the quality itself is named.**

### لِنتَ — root: ل-ي-ن

The root **ل-ي-ن**: **softness.** *Layyin* — soft. **And the lexicographers tie the concrete sense of the root to the sense of touch: not hard, soft to the hand.**

### فَظًّا غَلِيظَ ٱلْقَلْبِ — the two words taken apart

**And the two words must be taken separately, because they do not say the same thing.**

**فَظّ** — root **ف-ظ-ظ**. **The lexicographers explain the word as *coarse, harsh, rebuffing*, and for the concrete sense of the root they transmit a striking usage: *faẓẓ* — the water in a camel's rumen.** The water a traveller left without water drinks after slaughtering the camel — **foul to the mouth, repellent.** **From this an idea of *repulsion* has remained in the word.**

**I transmit this in the form the lexicographers give it; I make no firm claim about the derivation.**

And the field of *faẓẓ* must be recorded: **speech and conduct** — that is, **what shows outwardly.**

**غَلِيظ** — root **غ-ل-ظ**: **thick, coarse, heavy.** *Ghilẓa* — thickness. **The root was treated at 4/21 in `004-nisa.md` (*mīthāqan ghalīẓā*), at 48/29 in `048-fetih.md` (*fa'staghlaẓa*), in `011-hud.md` and in `033-ahzab.md`. I rely on those treatments.**

**And here the word comes in a construct: *ghalīẓa'l-qalb* — "thick of heart".** **That is, its field is within.**

**I put the two words in a table:**

| Word | Root | Its concrete picture | Its field |
|---|---|---|---|
| **فَظّ** | ف-ظ-ظ | **Repellent, foul to the mouth** | **Outward** — speech, conduct |
| **غَلِيظُ ٱلْقَلْبِ** | غ-ل-ظ | **Thick, not fine** | **Inward** — the heart |

**I record this as my own reading, and its ground is the fields of the two words:** the sentence counts two separate failings — the visible side of one, the invisible side of the other. **And it negates both at once.** **That is, gentleness is neither only in manner nor only within.**

### لَٱنفَضُّوا۟ مِنْ حَوْلِكَ — root: ف-ض-ض

The root **ف-ض-ض**. **The root was analysed in detail at 62/11 in `062-cuma.md`. I rely on that treatment and take the description given there:**

> **Its concrete sense: *to break a thing and scatter its pieces*, to part what is joined, to open what is sealed.** *Faḍḍa'l-khātama* — he broke the seal. **In Form VII (*infaḍḍa*) it becomes reflexive: to scatter of oneself, to fall apart.**

**And what belongs here is this: the picture of the root is a *breaking* — the falling apart of what was joined.**

**And the form of the verb must be recorded: *infaḍḍū* — Form VII, reflexive.** **That is, no one scatters them; they scatter of themselves.**

**I record this as an observation about the word:** the verse tells the consequence of harshness not as a driving off or a sending away but as **a scattering of itself.** **And this lies along the same line as the command *wa lā tafarraqū* at verse 103** — there scattering was forbidden; here what leads to scattering is named.

**And I record the link between 62/11 in `062-cuma.md` and this verse, and it is verifiable from the text:**

| Place | Verb | The cause of the scattering |
|---|---|---|
| **al-Jumuʿa 62/11** | *Infaḍḍū ilayhā* | **Trade and diversion** — a pull from outside |
| **Āl ʿImrān 3/159** | *La'nfaḍḍū min ḥawlik* | **Harshness** — a push from within |

**The same root, two different causes: one pulls from outside, the other pushes from within.**

### Three commands

**And after the conditional sentence three commands come, and their order must be recorded:**

| # | Command | Direction | Subject |
|---|---|---|---|
| **1** | *Fa'fu **ʿanhum*** | **To the past** | What has happened |
| **2** | *Wa'staghfir **lahum*** | **Upward** | On their behalf |
| **3** | *Wa **shāwirhum** fi'l-amr* | **To the future** | The work to be done |

**And the order sets up a sequence of time: past, intercession, future.**

**I record this as my own reading, and its ground is the order of the three commands:** the third command is the heaviest. **For the first two close what has happened; the third gives the same people a say once again.**

And its context must not be forgotten: at verse 152 it was said *tanāzaʿtum fi'l-amr* — "you quarrelled over the affair". And now at verse 159 it says *shāwirhum **fi'l-amr*** — **the same construction.**

**This is a fact verifiable within the sūra and worth recording:** the affair that was quarrelled over and the affair about which consultation is commanded are named **with the same word.**

**I record this as my own reading, and it is not binding:** the consequence of a quarrel is given not as the removal of consultation on the matter quarrelled over but **as the commanding of consultation.** **That is, the text does not draw from a consultation that turned out badly the conclusion that consultation should be abolished.**

### شُورَىٰ — a comparison with 42/38

The root **ش-و-ر** was analysed in detail at 42/38 in `042-sura.md`. **I rely on that treatment and do not repeat it; I summarise:**

> **The concrete sense of the root: *shāra'l-ʿasal* — he took the honey out of the hive.** The honey is inside the hive; it is unseen, closed in, and getting it out takes labour. **And all the branches of the root carry the same core** — *ashāra* (to point), *shawār* (goods put on display): **to bring a thing out of where it is and set it in the open.**
>
> **And this was recorded there: "Within a community there is opinion, but until it is brought out it does not exist."**

**And now I compare the two verses. This is a datum verifiable within the series:**

| | **al-Shūrā 42/38** | **Āl ʿImrān 3/159** |
|---|---|---|
| **The word** | *Shūrā* — **a noun** | *Shāwir* — **an imperative verb** |
| **Kind of sentence** | ***A nominal sentence*** — *wa amruhum shūrā baynahum* | ***A command*** — *wa shāwirhum fi'l-amr* |
| **What it conveys** | **An attribute** — this is their state | **A command** — do this |
| **To whom** | **To a community** — third person | **To one person** — second person |
| **Context** | **A list of attributes** — between prayer and spending | **The aftermath of a defeat** |
| **Where** | *Baynahum* — **among them** | *Fi'l-amr* — **in the affair** |
| **What follows** | *Wa mimmā razaqnāhum yunfiqūn* | *Fa-idhā ʿazamta fa-tawakkal ʿala'llāh* |

**I draw three observations from the table, and all three can be verified from the two texts.**

**One. One is an attribute, the other a command — and this is said by two different forms of the word.** In al-Shūrā a noun (*shūrā*), in Āl ʿImrān a verb (*shāwir*). **That is, the same root conveys in one place the state of a community and in the other a duty given to one person.**

**Two. In al-Shūrā a nominal sentence is used, and it was recorded in `042-sura.md` that this conveys continuance.** **Here, on the other hand, the imperative is used, and the command comes after an event.** **The two moods do two different pieces of work: one conveys a lasting quality, the other a thing to be done in a particular situation.**

**Three. And the two verses together give a whole.** The verse in al-Shūrā says that consultation is **the quality of a community**; the verse in Āl ʿImrān shows that it **is not abandoned even at the hardest moment.**

**I record this as my own reading, and it is not binding:** whether a quality is really a quality becomes clear at the moment when things go badly. **And Āl ʿImrān chooses exactly that moment.**

**Per the method (`USLUP.md`) I record this: no form of government and no political model is drawn from this verse.** **The same note was entered at 42/38 in `042-sura.md` and is kept here.** The verse commands consultation; it says nothing about the institutional form of consultation.

### فَإِذَا عَزَمْتَ فَتَوَكَّلْ

The root **ع-ز-م**: **to resolve firmly upon a matter, to be determined.** **And the subject of the verb is singular: *ʿazamta* — "when you have decided".**

**And this gives rise to a fact of word order that must be recorded:**

| Stage | Verb | Person |
|---|---|---|
| **Consultation** | *Shāwir**hum*** | **Plural** — with them |
| **Decision** | *ʿAzam**ta*** | **Singular** — you |
| **Reliance** | *Fa-tawakkal* | **Singular** — you |

**I record this as an observation about the word order:** the consultation is plural, the decision singular. **And in binding the two together the verse uses the particle *fa* — that is, the decision comes after the consultation.**

**And the command to rely must be recorded: reliance is set not in place of consultation but *after* it.** **The two are in the same sentence and neither cancels the other.**

**And this finding was already recorded at 42/36 in `042-sura.md`** — there a conclusion was drawn from reliance and consultation standing in the same list. **I rely on that treatment.**

### What this says to the present

**What the verse says to the present lies in two places, and both come out of the wording.**

**One. The verse sets manner down as a matter of consequence.** *La'nfaḍḍū min ḥawlik* — the consequence of harshness is scattering. **And this is not a moral counsel but a note about how things work: people leave the place from which they are pushed.**

**Two. The verse does not draw from a participation that turned out badly the conclusion that participation should be removed.** A quarrel occurred (152), and afterwards consultation was commanded (159). **This is a link set up in two verses with the same word (*al-amr*) and verifiable from the text.**

---

## 3/160-164 — إِن يَنصُرْكُمُ ٱللَّهُ فَلَا غَالِبَ لَكُمْ … لَقَدْ مَنَّ ٱللَّهُ عَلَى ٱلْمُؤْمِنِينَ إِذْ بَعَثَ فِيهِمْ رَسُولًا مِّنْ أَنفُسِهِمْ

*In yanṣurkumu'llāhu fa-lā ghāliba lakum, wa in yakhdhulkum fa-man dha'lladhī yanṣurukum min baʿdih, wa ʿala'llāhi fa'l-yatawakkali'l-muʾminūn · Wa mā kāna li-nabiyyin an yaghulla, wa man yaghlul yaʾti bimā ghalla yawma'l-qiyāma, thumma tuwaffā kullu nafsin mā kasabat wa hum lā yuẓlamūn · … · La-qad manna'llāhu ʿala'l-muʾminīna idh baʿatha fīhim rasūlan min anfusihim yatlū ʿalayhim āyātihī wa yuzakkīhim wa yuʿallimuhumu'l-kitāba wa'l-ḥikmata wa in kānū min qablu la-fī ḍalālin mubīn*

"**If God helps you, none can overcome you; and if He leaves you without help, who is there that could help you after Him?** Let the believers rely on God alone. · **It is not for a prophet to defraud regarding the spoils.** Whoever defrauds will come with what he took on the day of resurrection… · **God has favoured the believers by sending among them a messenger from among themselves:** he recites His signs to them, purifies them, and teaches them the Book and wisdom. **Before that they were in manifest straying.**"

### 3/160 — the opposition of two conditions

| Condition | Consequence | Word order |
|---|---|---|
| *In **yanṣurkum**u'llāh* | *Fa-lā **ghāliba** lakum* | **A negative nominal sentence** — absolute |
| *Wa in **yakhdhulkum*** | *Fa-man dha'lladhī **yanṣurukum** min baʿdih* | **A question** — left unanswered |

**And the kind of the two consequences differs: the first is a ruling, the second a question.**

**I record this as an observation about the word order:** the answer to the second branch is not written. **The question leaves the gap for the reader to fill** — and this is the same method as *a-fa-lā taʿqilūn* at verse 65.

The root **خ-ذ-ل**: **to withhold help, to leave alone.** **And *khidhlān* is not enmity; it is the withdrawal of support.**

### 3/161 — وَمَا كَانَ لِنَبِىٍّ أَن يَغُلَّ

**And the pattern is familiar: *mā kāna li-…* — it occurred at verse 79 as *mā kāna li-basharin*.**

| Verse | Pattern | What is refused |
|---|---|---|
| **79** | *Mā kāna **li-basharin** an yaqūla… kūnū ʿibādan lī* | **Making others servants of oneself** |
| **161** | *Mā kāna **li-nabiyyin** an yaghulla* | **Defrauding regarding the spoils** |

**Two occurrences, the same pattern.** This is a repetition verifiable within the sūra.

The root **غ-ل-ل**: **to slip a thing in secretly, to hide it.** *Ghulūl* — in particular the taking of something from the spoils in secret. **And from the same root *ghull* — the collar put round the neck; and *ghill* — the rancour hidden within.** **The shared core of the root: *a thing's coming in between, its getting inside.***

**And a difference in the reciting of the word is transmitted** — the verb is read both in the active (*yaghulla*) and in the passive (*yughalla*); on this the sense becomes either "that he should defraud" or "that he should be defrauded". **Both readings are transmitted; I impose no preference and give no reciter's name.**

### 3/164 — لَقَدْ مَنَّ ٱللَّهُ … رَسُولًا مِّنْ أَنفُسِهِمْ

**And four verbs are counted, and their order must be recorded:**

| # | Verb | The work |
|---|---|---|
| **1** | *Yatlū ʿalayhim **āyātih*** | **Reciting** |
| **2** | *Wa **yuzakkīhim*** | **Purifying** |
| **3** | *Wa yuʿallimuhumu'l-**kitāb*** | **Teaching the Book** |
| **4** | *Wa'l-**ḥikma*** | **Teaching wisdom** |

**And the purifying comes *before* the teaching.**

**I record this as an observation about the word order:** the same four verbs occur at 2/129 in `002-bakara.md`, in Abraham's prayer, and at 2/151. **And there the order differs: at 2/129 *yuzakkīhim* is put last, here it stands second.** **The difference between the two arrangements is a fact verifiable from the text; I draw no ruling from it.**

**مِّنْ أَنفُسِهِمْ** — "**from among themselves**". **And this note is to be read together with verse 144:** there it is said that the messenger is mortal, here that he is one of them. **The two notes look in the same direction.**

---

## 3/165-168 — قُلْ هُوَ مِنْ عِندِ أَنفُسِكُمْ

*A-wa-lammā aṣābatkum muṣībatun qad aṣabtum mithlayhā qultum annā hādhā, qul huwa min ʿindi anfusikum, inna'llāha ʿalā kulli shayʾin qadīr · Wa mā aṣābakum yawma'ltaqa'l-jamʿāni fa-bi-idhni'llāhi wa li-yaʿlama'l-muʾminīn · Wa li-yaʿlama'lladhīna nāfaqū… · Alladhīna qālū li-ikhwānihim wa qaʿadū law aṭāʿūnā mā qutilū, qul fa'draʾū ʿan anfusikumu'l-mawta in kuntum ṣādiqīn*

"**When an affliction touched you, though you had inflicted twice its like, you said: 'Where is this from?' Say: 'It is from yourselves.'** God has power over all things. · **What befell you on the day the two hosts met was by God's leave** — that He might know the believers. · **And that He might know those who played the hypocrite**… · **They are the ones who, sitting at home, said of their brothers: 'Had they obeyed us they would not have been killed.'** Say: '**Then ward off death from yourselves, if you are truthful.**'"

### قُلْ هُوَ مِنْ عِندِ أَنفُسِكُمْ — the third note of the sūra

**This sentence is the third of the three sentences recorded in the introduction to the sūra. And the table of the three was given there:**

| Verse | Sentence | To whom |
|---|---|---|
| **7** | *Fa-ammā'lladhīna fī qulūbihim **zayghun*** | **To the reader** |
| **152** | *Fashiltum wa tanāzaʿtum wa ʿaṣaytum* | **To the community** |
| **165** | *Qul **huwa min ʿindi anfusikum*** | **Directly** |

**And the third is the shortest and the plainest.**

**And the word order of the sentence must be recorded:**

| Element | Wording | Its work |
|---|---|---|
| **The question** | *Annā hādhā* — "where is this from?" | **The cause is sought outside** |
| **The command** | *Qul* | **The answer is given** |
| **The answer** | *Huwa **min ʿindi anfusikum*** | **The cause is turned inward** |

**And the construction *min ʿindi* must be recorded. It occurs several times in the sūra, and always conveys a source:**

| Verse | Construction | Source |
|---|---|---|
| **37** | *Huwa **min ʿindi'llāh*** | **From God** |
| **78** | *Huwa **min ʿindi'llāhi** wa mā huwa min ʿindi'llāh* | **A claim and its refutation** |
| **126** | *Wa ma'n-naṣru illā **min ʿindi'llāh*** | **The source of the help** |
| **165** | *Huwa **min ʿindi anfusikum*** | **From yourselves** |

**Four occurrences, the same construction.** This is a repetition verifiable within the sūra.

**I record this as my own reading, and its ground is the four occurrences of the construction:** the sūra uses the same pattern both for the source of the help and for the source of the affliction. **And the two are tied to different places: the help is from His presence, the cause of the affliction from yourselves.**

### 165 and 166 together

**And the two verses must be read together, because they say two different things about the same event:**

| Verse | Wording | What it says |
|---|---|---|
| **165** | *Huwa **min ʿindi anfusikum*** | **The cause** — the human share |
| **166** | *Fa-**bi-idhni'llāh*** | **The leave** — the event's coming to pass |

**And the two do not conflict.** **I record this as an observation about the word order, and its ground is the succession of the two verses:** one verse ties the cause to the human being, the next ties the coming to pass of the event to leave. **The two sentences answer two different questions: "why did it happen?" and "how did it happen?"**

**And the order must be recorded: the human share is stated *first*.**

### 168 — the second occurrence of the saying

***Law aṭāʿūnā mā qutilū*** — "**had they obeyed us they would not have been killed**".

**And this saying is the second form of the saying in verse 156:**

| Verse | The saying | The condition |
|---|---|---|
| **156** | *Law kānū **ʿindanā** mā mātū wa mā qutilū* | ***Place*** — had they been with us |
| **168** | *Law **aṭāʿūnā** mā qutilū* | ***Obedience*** — had they followed us |

**Two occurrences, the same structure, two different conditions.** This is a repetition verifiable within the sūra.

**And the form of the answer must be recorded: *qul fa'draʾū ʿan anfusikumu'l-mawta in kuntum ṣādiqīn*** — "**then ward off death from yourselves, if you are truthful**".

**I record this as an observation about the word order:** the answer is not a counter-claim; **it is the applying of the claim's own logic to itself.** If the place one is in or the decision one takes prevents death, the same must hold for the speakers. **And the verb is in the imperative: *idraʾū* — "ward off".**

---

## 3/169-171 — وَلَا تَحْسَبَنَّ ٱلَّذِينَ قُتِلُوا۟ فِى سَبِيلِ ٱللَّهِ أَمْوَٰتًۢا بَلْ أَحْيَآءٌ عِندَ رَبِّهِمْ يُرْزَقُونَ

*Wa lā taḥsabanna'lladhīna qutilū fī sabīli'llāhi amwātā, bal aḥyāʾun ʿinda rabbihim yurzaqūn · Fariḥīna bimā ātāhumu'llāhu min faḍlihī wa yastabshirūna bi'lladhīna lam yalḥaqū bihim min khalfihim allā khawfun ʿalayhim wa lā hum yaḥzanūn · Yastabshirūna bi-niʿmatin mina'llāhi wa faḍlin wa anna'llāha lā yuḍīʿu ajra'l-muʾminīn*

"**Do not suppose those killed in the way of God to be dead. Rather they are alive, provided for with their Lord.** · **They rejoice in what God has given them of His bounty, and they rejoice for those who have not yet joined them from behind: no fear shall be upon them, and they shall not grieve.** · **They rejoice in a favour and a bounty from God; and God does not let the reward of the believers go to waste.**"

---

**This verse must be read together with 2/154 in `002-bakara.md`, and the difference between the two verses is the point this commentary draws attention to.**

### A comparison with 2/154

**At 2/154 in `002-bakara.md` this verse was already named and a table was drawn up. I rely on that treatment and widen the table:**

| | **al-Baqara 2/154** | **Āl ʿImrān 3/169** |
|---|---|---|
| **Mood** | *Wa lā **taqūlū*** — "**do not say**" | *Wa lā **taḥsabanna*** — "**do not suppose**" |
| **What is intervened in** | ***Speech*** | ***Supposition*** |
| **The addressee** | **Plural** — the community | **Singular** — with the *nūn* of emphasis |
| **The verb** | *Li-man **yuqtalu*** — **imperfect**, ongoing | *Alladhīna **qutilū*** — **perfect**, accomplished |
| **The close** | *Wa lākin **lā tashʿurūn*** — "**you do not perceive**" | *ʿInda rabbihim **yurzaqūn*** — "**they are provided for**" |
| **What is given** | **The limit of knowledge** | **A part of the knowledge** |

**And it was recorded in `002-bakara.md`:**

> "**One states the limit of the knowledge, the other gives a part of the knowledge. The verse in al-Baqara breaks off without saying anything about the nature of that life.**"

**I rely on that treatment and now record what Āl ʿImrān adds.**

**One. The prohibition is laid not on speech but on supposition.** Al-Baqara says *lā taqūlū* (do not say); here it says *lā taḥsabanna* (do not suppose). **The two are separate fields: one language, the other judgement.**

**I record this as my own reading, and its ground is the difference between the two verbs:** al-Baqara was intervening in the naming; **Āl ʿImrān intervenes in the appraisal behind the naming.** And the order is meaningful — first the word was corrected, then the conviction.

**Two. And the verb *yurzaqūn* is added.**

The root **ر-ز-ق**: **what is brought regularly to someone.** **And the verb is passive: the giver is not spoken of, the state of receiving is conveyed.**

**I record this as an observation about the word:** al-Baqara says "they are alive" and stops. **Āl ʿImrān adds a verb** — and the verb added is the most everyday mark of life: **being fed.** That is, the presence of life is shown not by an adjective but by a **verb**.

**Three. And I keep the limit here as well.** **This was recorded in `002-bakara.md` and holds exactly:**

> "**I do not say what the text does not say about what kind of life this is, where it goes on, or in what form.**"

**Āl ʿImrān says *ʿinda rabbihim* (with their Lord) and *yurzaqūn* (they are provided for); and in verses 170-171 it adds two more verbs: *fariḥīn* (they rejoice) and *yastabshirūn* (they receive good news / they rejoice).** **I go no further than these.**

### 170-171: the direction of the rejoicing

**And the word order of the two verses must be recorded, because rejoicing is named twice and about two different things:**

| Verse | Verb | The object of the rejoicing |
|---|---|---|
| **170** | *Fariḥīna bimā **ātāhumu'llāh*** | **What has been given to them** |
| **170** | *Wa **yastabshirūna** bi'lladhīna lam yalḥaqū bihim* | **Those left behind** |
| **171** | *Yastabshirūna bi-**niʿmatin** mina'llāhi wa **faḍl*** | **Favour and bounty** |

**And the middle one is worth recording: part of the rejoicing is not for themselves but for *those who have not yet come*.**

**I record this as an observation about the word order:** while telling of a loss the verse turns the gaze to those left behind. **And the word spoken is given not in their mouths but about them: *allā khawfun ʿalayhim wa lā hum yaḥzanūn*.**

**And this construction is one that occurs often in the Qurʾān and was treated at 2/38, 2/62 and 2/112 in `002-bakara.md`. I rely on those treatments.**

**بشر** — root **ب-ش-ر**: **the outer surface of the skin; and from this, joy showing on the face.** **The root was treated in `019-meryem.md` and `011-hud.md`.** **And in Form X (*yastabshirūn*) the *seeking or the mutual showing* of joy is conveyed.**

---

## 3/172-175 — حَسْبُنَا ٱللَّهُ وَنِعْمَ ٱلْوَكِيلُ · إِنَّمَا ذَٰلِكُمُ ٱلشَّيْطَٰنُ يُخَوِّفُ أَوْلِيَآءَهُۥ

*Alladhīna'stajābū li'llāhi wa'r-rasūli min baʿdi mā aṣābahumu'l-qarḥ, li'lladhīna aḥsanū minhum wa'ttaqaw ajrun ʿaẓīm · Alladhīna qāla lahumu'n-nāsu inna'n-nāsa qad jamaʿū lakum fa'khshawhum fa-zādahum īmānan wa qālū ḥasbuna'llāhu wa niʿma'l-wakīl · Fa'nqalabū bi-niʿmatin mina'llāhi wa faḍlin lam yamsas'hum sūʾun wa'ttabaʿū riḍwāna'llāh, wa'llāhu dhū faḍlin ʿaẓīm · Innamā dhālikumu'sh-shayṭānu yukhawwifu awliyāʾah, fa-lā takhāfūhum wa khāfūni in kuntum muʾminīn*

"**Those who answered God and the messenger after the wound had reached them** — for those of them who did good and guarded themselves there is a great reward. · **People said to them: 'People have gathered against you, so fear them.' But this increased them in faith, and they said: 'God is enough for us; how excellent a trustee!'** · **So they returned with a favour and a bounty from God, untouched by any harm, and they followed the good pleasure of God.** … · **That is only Satan, making his friends fear. Do not fear them; fear Me, if you are believers.**"

### 3/173 — the sound structure of the sentence

**And the word order of the verse must be recorded: the word *al-nās* occurs twice, one after the other, and conveys two different sets.**

*Alladhīna qāla lahumu'**n-nāsu** inna'**n-nāsa** qad jamaʿū lakum*

| Occurrence | Who |
|---|---|
| The first *al-nās* | **Those who bring the news** |
| The second *al-nās* | **The side said to have gathered** |

**The same word, in one sentence, for two different sets.** **I record this as an observation about the word order:** the sentence shows how fear is carried — **one group brings news about another group.** **And the truth of the news is not discussed; the stance taken is recorded.**

**فَزَادَهُمْ إِيمَٰنًا** — "**this increased them in faith**". **And the subject of the verb must be recorded: what increased it was the attempt at intimidation itself.**

### حَسْبُنَا ٱللَّهُ وَنِعْمَ ٱلْوَكِيلُ

**حَسْب** — root **ح-س-ب**: **to count, to reckon; and to be enough.** *Ḥasb* — what suffices, what is enough.

**And its link with the other occurrences of the root in the sūra must be recorded:** the same root had occurred six times in the sūra in the sense of "to suppose" (78, 142, 169, 178, 180, 188). **Here it is in the sense of "to suffice".**

**I record this as an observation about the word and draw no ruling from it:** the same root conveys both reckoning and sufficing. **And the lexicographers explain it thus: a thing's *sufficing* is the reckoning's being complete.**

**وَكِيل** — root **و-ك-ل**: **to commit an affair to another.** *Wakīl* — the one who takes an affair upon himself. **And from the same root *tawakkul* occurred at verses 159 and 160.**

**And the construction occurs once more in the Qurʾān and, within this sūra itself, only here. And the pattern *niʿma* conveys praise.**

**I record this as an observation about the word order:** the sentence is in two parts — one of **sufficiency** (*ḥasbuna'llāh*), the other of **taking charge** (*niʿma'l-wakīl*). **That is, what is said is not only "He is enough for us" but also "He takes the affair upon Himself".**

### 3/175 — يُخَوِّفُ أَوْلِيَآءَهُۥ and the instrument of intimidation

The root **خ-و-ف**: **fear.** **And the verb is in Form II: *yukhawwif* — the *giving* of fear, intimidating.**

**And at 39/36 in `039-zumer.md` the same verb in the same form was treated, and this was recorded there:**

> ***Wa yukhawwifūnaka bi'lladhīna min dūnih*** — "**they make you afraid with those besides Him**". **The verb is Form II: the work of *giving* fear.** **And this was recorded there: "the verse names the instrument the other side uses — not proof but fear."**

**I rely on that treatment and bind the two verses into one line:**

| Place | Wording | Who frightens | Who is frightened | The instrument of fear |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **al-Zumar 39/36** | *Wa **yukhawwifūnaka** bi'lladhīna min dūnih* | **People** | **The Prophet** | *Alladhīna min dūnih* |
| **Āl ʿImrān 3/173** | *Inna'n-nāsa qad jamaʿū lakum fa'**khshawhum*** | **People** | **The believers** | The news of a gathering |
| **Āl ʿImrān 3/175** | *Innamā dhālikumu'sh-shayṭānu **yukhawwifu** awliyāʾah* | **Satan** | — | — |

**Three verses, the same root and the same operation: the use of fear as an instrument.** This is a weave verifiable within the series.

**And the finding recorded in `039-zumer.md` holds here too and can be widened: the sūra names the instrument the other side uses.** **And Āl ʿImrān takes one step further: it names the *source* of the instrument as well.**

### يُخَوِّفُ أَوْلِيَآءَهُۥ — the difficulty in the sentence

**And the lexicographers differ over the syntax of the sentence, and the disagreement is not to be concealed. The difficulty is this: is *awliyāʾah* the object, or does it come with an understood *bi-*?**

| Reading | Sentence | Sense |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | *Yukhawwifu **awliyāʾah*** — object | "**He makes his own friends afraid**" — those frightened are Satan's friends |
| 2 | *Yukhawwifukum **bi**-awliyāʾih* — an understood particle | "**He makes you afraid with his friends**" — the instrument is his friends |
| 3 | *Yukhawwifukum awliyāʾah* — two objects | "**He makes his friends seem frightening to you**" |

**All three are transmitted; I do not impose a preference.**

**And the second reading coincides exactly with 39/36 in `039-zumer.md`** (*yukhawwifūnaka **bi'lladhīna** min dūnih* — there the *bāʾ* is explicit). **I record this as an indication, not as a preference.**

**فَلَا تَخَافُوهُمْ وَخَافُونِ** — **and the answer does not remove the fear; it changes its *direction*.**

**I record this as an observation about the word order, and its ground is that the two verbs are from the same root:** the sentence does not say "do not fear". **The same verb occurs twice: one negated, the other an imperative.** **That is, fear is not rejected as a feeling; its object is changed.**

**And the continuation of 39/36 in `039-zumer.md` has the same structure** (*a-laysa'llāhu bi-kāfin ʿabdah* — "Is God not enough for His servant?"). **Two sūras answer the same matter with the same two steps: sufficiency, and a change of direction.**

---

## 3/176-179 — وَلَا يَحْسَبَنَّ ٱلَّذِينَ كَفَرُوٓا۟ أَنَّمَا نُمْلِى لَهُمْ خَيْرٌ لِّأَنفُسِهِمْ

*Wa lā yaḥzunka'lladhīna yusāriʿūna fi'l-kufr, innahum lan yaḍurru'llāha shayʾā… · Inna'lladhīna'shtarawu'l-kufra bi'l-īmāni lan yaḍurru'llāha shayʾan wa lahum ʿadhābun alīm · Wa lā yaḥsabanna'lladhīna kafarū annamā numlī lahum khayrun li-anfusihim, innamā numlī lahum li-yazdādū ithmā, wa lahum ʿadhābun muhīn · Mā kāna'llāhu li-yadhara'l-muʾminīna ʿalā mā antum ʿalayhi ḥattā yamīza'l-khabītha mina'ṭ-ṭayyib…*

"**Let those who race into denial not grieve you; they do God no harm at all**… · **Those who have bought denial at the price of faith do God no harm at all**… · **Let those who deny not suppose that Our giving them time is good for them. We give them time only that they may increase in sin**… · **God will not leave the believers in the state you are in — until He separates the foul from the good.**"

### نُمْلِى — root: م-ل-و

The root **م-ل-و**: **a long span of time; and to grant a respite.** *Malā* — a long time. *Imlāʾ* — the giving of a term, the granting of respite.

**The root was treated at 22/44 and 22/48 in `022-hac.md`. I rely on those treatments.**

**And what belongs here is this, and it is an observation about the word order:** the verse uses the same verb twice and puts an *innamā* between them.

| Sentence | Wording | What it does |
|---|---|---|
| **What is supposed** | *Annamā **numlī** lahum **khayrun** li-anfusihim* | **The supposition that the respite is in their favour** |
| **What is so** | *Innamā **numlī** lahum **li-yazdādū ithmā*** | **The function of the respite** |

**The same verb, two different readings.** **And the difference is not in the verb but in the sense loaded onto it.**

**I record this as my own reading, and its ground is the verb the two sentences share:** the verse does not dispute the fact itself — the respite has really been given. **What it disputes is how that fact is to be read.** **And this is the reason the sūra uses the root ح-س-ب six times: what is corrected throughout the sūra is not facts but the conclusions drawn from facts.**

### 3/179 — يَمِيزَ ٱلْخَبِيثَ مِنَ ٱلطَّيِّبِ

The root **م-ي-ز**: **to separate, to sort out what has been mixed together.**

**And this verb is the answer to the difficulty at verse 142.** **There it said *wa lammā yaʿlami'llāhu* and the disagreement over the sense of the verb "to know" was recorded.** **Here the sūra supplies its own word: *yamīza* — to separate.**

**I record this as a datum of the text:** two verses tell the same matter with two different verbs, and the second explains the first. **This is a link verifiable within the sūra.**

**خَبِيث / طَيِّب** — **خ-ب-ث**: bad, foul, useless. **ط-ي-ب**: pleasant, clean, good. **And the same pair had occurred at 2/267 in `002-bakara.md` on the subject of spending.** **Two occurrences, in two different fields.**

---

## 3/180-182 — وَلَا يَحْسَبَنَّ ٱلَّذِينَ يَبْخَلُونَ بِمَآ ءَاتَىٰهُمُ ٱللَّهُ مِن فَضْلِهِۦ هُوَ خَيْرًا لَّهُم

*Wa lā yaḥsabanna'lladhīna yabkhalūna bimā ātāhumu'llāhu min faḍlihī huwa khayran lahum, bal huwa sharrun lahum, sa-yuṭawwaqūna mā bakhilū bihī yawma'l-qiyāma, wa li'llāhi mīrāthu's-samāwāti wa'l-arḍ, wa'llāhu bimā taʿmalūna khabīr · La-qad samiʿa'llāhu qawla'lladhīna qālū inna'llāha faqīrun wa naḥnu aghniyāʾ, sa-naktubu mā qālū wa qatlahumu'l-anbiyāʾa bi-ghayri ḥaqq… · Dhālika bimā qaddamat aydīkum wa anna'llāha laysa bi-ẓallāmin li'l-ʿabīd*

"**Let those who are miserly with what God has given them of His bounty not suppose it good for them: it is bad for them. What they were miserly with will be hung round their necks on the day of resurrection.** The inheritance of the heavens and the earth belongs to God… · **God has indeed heard the word of those who said, 'God is poor and we are rich.' We shall write down what they said and their killing of the prophets without right**… · **This is because of what your hands have sent ahead; and God is never unjust to His servants.**"

### سَيُطَوَّقُونَ — root: ط-و-ق

The root **ط-و-ق**: **a ring, a hoop that goes round the neck.** *Ṭawq* — a collar, a yoke. **And the verb is passive in Form II: *yuṭawwaqūna* — "it will be put round their necks".**

**I record this as an observation about the word:** the description likens the thing hoarded not so much to **a load** as to **a ring.** **A ring cannot be taken off; it is carried.** **And the thing one wanted to keep in hand turns into a hoop round the neck.**

**And the same root is close in sense to *ghull* in verse 161** — there too there was a ring that goes round the neck. **Two different roots, in two different verses, the same picture.** **I record this as an observation and claim no derivational link between the two roots.**

**وَلِلَّهِ مِيرَٰثُ ٱلسَّمَٰوَٰتِ وَٱلْأَرْضِ** — **and the sentence enters a note of ownership.** The root **م-ي-ر-ث / و-ر-ث**: **the passing on of what is left behind.**

**I record this as an observation about the word order:** immediately after the verse on miserliness there is set a sentence saying that what is held will **be passed on anyway.** **That is, the end of the thing miserliness is about is stated in the same verse.**

**And the concept of *al-mulk* was treated at verse 26 of the sūra; *mīrāth* is its counterpart in time.**

### إِنَّ ٱللَّهَ فَقِيرٌ وَنَحْنُ أَغْنِيَآءُ — the place of the saying

**And where the saying is placed must be recorded: immediately after the verse on miserliness.**

**I record this as my own reading, and its ground is the succession of the two verses:** verse 180 tells of a conduct (miserliness), verse 181 of a saying. **And there is a link between the two verses: reading the call to spend as "neediness".** Immediately after telling of the conduct, the sūra names the reading behind that conduct.

**And the same matter was treated at 2/245 in `002-bakara.md`** (*man dha'lladhī yuqriḍu'llāha qarḍan ḥasanā*) **and the difficulty raised by the phrase "a goodly loan" was taken up there.** **I rely on that treatment.**

**Per the method (`USLUP.md`) I record this: I do not go into the reports about who spoke this saying, and I give no personal or group names.** **The verse gives its subject as *alladhīna qālū* — "those who said". The measure is not who says the word but the word itself.**

---

## 3/183-186 — كُلُّ نَفْسٍ ذَآئِقَةُ ٱلْمَوْتِ · لَتُبْلَوُنَّ فِىٓ أَمْوَٰلِكُمْ وَأَنفُسِكُمْ

*Alladhīna qālū inna'llāha ʿahida ilaynā allā nuʾmina li-rasūlin ḥattā yaʾtiyanā bi-qurbānin taʾkuluhu'n-nār, qul qad jāʾakum rusulun min qablī bi'l-bayyināti wa bi'lladhī qultum fa-lima qataltumūhum in kuntum ṣādiqīn · Fa-in kadhdhabūka fa-qad kudhdhiba rusulun min qablika jāʾū bi'l-bayyināti wa'z-zuburi wa'l-kitābi'l-munīr · Kullu nafsin dhāʾiqatu'l-mawt, wa innamā tuwaffawna ujūrakum yawma'l-qiyāma, fa-man zuḥziḥa ʿani'n-nāri wa udkhila'l-jannata fa-qad fāz, wa ma'l-ḥayātu'd-dunyā illā matāʿu'l-ghurūr · La-tublawunna fī amwālikum wa anfusikum wa la-tasmaʿunna mina'lladhīna ūtu'l-kitāba min qablikum wa mina'lladhīna ashrakū adhan kathīrā, wa in taṣbirū wa tattaqū fa-inna dhālika min ʿazmi'l-umūr*

"**Those who said: 'God has charged us to believe in no messenger until he brings us an offering that fire consumes'**… Say: '**Messengers came to you before me with clear proofs and with what you named; why then did you kill them, if you are truthful?**' · **If they have called you a liar, messengers before you were called liars too**… · **Every soul shall taste death. Your recompenses will be paid in full only on the day of resurrection. Whoever is drawn away from the fire and admitted to the garden has attained. The life of this world is nothing but the enjoyment of delusion.** · **You will certainly be tested in your wealth and in your persons; and you will certainly hear much that is hurtful from those given the Book before you and from those who associate. But if you are steadfast and guard yourselves — that is among the matters requiring resolve.**"

### 3/183 — the refuting of a claim from within itself

**And the form of the answer must be recorded: *fa-lima qataltumūhum in kuntum ṣādiqīn*.**

**I record this as an observation about the word order:** the answer does not refuse the claim; **it asks what was done to those who did fulfil its condition.** **That is, it is shown from the past that even when the condition was met the outcome did not change.**

**And the same method was used at verse 168: *fa'draʾū ʿan anfusikumu'l-mawt* — there too the logic of the claim was applied to the claimant.** **Two occurrences, the same method.**

### 3/185 — كُلُّ نَفْسٍ ذَآئِقَةُ ٱلْمَوْتِ

The root **ذ-و-ق**: **to taste.** **And not a verb but an active participle is used: *dhāʾiqa* — "taster".** In Arabic the active participle conveys the coming to pass of the act with certainty and continuance.

**I record this as an observation about the word:** death is named not as an **end** but as a **tasting.** **And to taste is to take not the whole of a thing but a part of it.**

**زُحْزِحَ** — root **ز-ح-ز-ح**: **to move away little by little, by pushing.** **The word occurs only here in the Qurʾān.** **And the verb is passive: the one who moves away is not named.**

**And what the lexicographers record must be recorded: there is a *repetition* in the root** — a four-letter, doubled form (*zaḥzaḥa*), which in Arabic generally conveys a repeated movement. **That is, the moving away is not in a single thrust but stage by stage.**

**مَتَٰعُ ٱلْغُرُور** — root **غ-ر-ر**: **to deceive; a thing's appearance misleading a person.** **And the word *matāʿ* had occurred at verse 14: *dhālika matāʿu'l-ḥayāti'd-dunyā*.**

**The difference between the two occurrences must be recorded:**

| Verse | Construction | What is added |
|---|---|---|
| **14** | *Matāʿu'l-ḥayāti'd-**dunyā*** | **No qualifier** — only "provision" |
| **185** | *Matāʿu'l-**ghurūr*** | **A qualifier** — "of delusion" |

**And it was recorded at verse 14: *matāʿ* is not a bad word; it means what is used, what is drawn benefit from.** **Here, on the other hand, an adjective is added to the word.**

**I record this as my own reading, and its ground is the difference between the two constructions:** what is bad is not the *matāʿ* but the wrong conclusion drawn from it. **And the sūra says this in two separate places in two separate ways: unqualified at the beginning, qualified at the end.**

### 3/186 — two fields of testing and two sources

| Field | Wording |
|---|---|
| **Wealth** | *Fī **amwālikum*** |
| **Persons** | *Wa **anfusikum*** |

| Source | Wording |
|---|---|
| **From those given the Book** | *Mina'lladhīna ūtu'l-kitāba min qablikum* |
| **From those who associate** | *Wa mina'lladhīna ashrakū* |

**And it must be recorded that both sources are given with the particle *min*.**

**عَزْمِ ٱلْأُمُور** — the root **ع-ز-م** occurred at verse 159 (*fa-idhā ʿazamta*). **Two occurrences, the same root:**

| Verse | Wording | Who / what |
|---|---|---|
| **159** | *Fa-idhā **ʿazamta*** | **A person's decision** |
| **186** | *Min **ʿazmi'l-umūr*** | **The character of the matters** |

**And the lexicographers differ over the sense of the construction, and the disagreement is not to be concealed:**

| Reading | What *ʿazmu'l-umūr* is |
|---|---|
| 1 | **Matters that require resolve** |
| 2 | **Matters commanded with finality** |
| 3 | **Matters to be dwelt on, serious matters** |

**All three are transmitted; I do not impose a preference.**

---

## 3/187-189 — وَإِذْ أَخَذَ ٱللَّهُ مِيثَٰقَ ٱلَّذِينَ أُوتُوا۟ ٱلْكِتَٰبَ لَتُبَيِّنُنَّهُۥ لِلنَّاسِ وَلَا تَكْتُمُونَهُۥ

*Wa idh akhadha'llāhu mīthāqa'lladhīna ūtu'l-kitāba la-tubayyinunnahū li'n-nāsi wa lā taktumūnah, fa-nabadhūhu warāʾa ẓuhūrihim wa'shtaraw bihī thamanan qalīlā, fa-biʾsa mā yashtarūn · Lā taḥsabanna'lladhīna yafraḥūna bimā ataw wa yuḥibbūna an yuḥmadū bimā lam yafʿalū fa-lā taḥsabannahum bi-mafāzatin mina'l-ʿadhāb, wa lahum ʿadhābun alīm · Wa li'llāhi mulku's-samāwāti wa'l-arḍ, wa'llāhu ʿalā kulli shayʾin qadīr*

"**And when God took a covenant from those given the Book: 'You shall surely make it plain to people and shall not conceal it.' But they cast it behind their backs and sold it for a small price. How evil a bargain!** · **Do not suppose those who rejoice in what they have done and love to be praised for what they have not done to have escaped the punishment; theirs is a painful punishment.** · **To God belongs the dominion of the heavens and the earth; and God has power over all things.**"

### The content of the covenant: two obligations

| # | Obligation | Mood |
|---|---|---|
| **1** | *La-**tubayyinunna**hū li'n-nās* | **Positive** — to make plain |
| **2** | *Wa lā **taktumū**nah* | **Negative** — not to conceal |

**And the two obligations are two faces of the same thing, but they are not the same: one commands a doing, the other a not-doing.**

**And the verb *taktumūn* occurred at verse 71: *wa taktumūna'l-ḥaqqa wa antum taʿlamūn*.** **Two occurrences, the same verb: one an actual state, the other a pledge taken.** This is a repetition verifiable within the sūra.

**And the note *li'n-nās* must be recorded:** the making plain is to be done not to a group but **to people.** **The same note was at verses 96, 110 and 138 as well.**

**نَبَذُوهُ وَرَآءَ ظُهُورِهِمْ** — root **ن-ب-ذ**: **to find a thing worthless and throw it away.** **And the description is concrete: *warāʾa ẓuhūrihim* — behind their backs.**

**I record this as an observation about the word:** the description conveys not a refusal but a **disregarding.** What is thrown behind the back is not destroyed; **it is put out of the field of vision.** **And this lies along the same line as the description *labs* (clothing over) at verse 71** — in both the object stays and becomes invisible.

### 3/188 — يُحِبُّونَ أَن يُحْمَدُوا۟ بِمَا لَمْ يَفْعَلُوا۟

**And what the verse describes is two separate kinds of conduct, and they must be counted separately:**

| # | Conduct | Wording |
|---|---|---|
| **1** | **Rejoicing in what one has done** | *Yafraḥūna bimā ataw* |
| **2** | **Loving to be praised for what one has not done** | *Yuḥibbūna an yuḥmadū **bimā lam yafʿalū*** |

**And the second is distinct from the other: what is at issue here is not an exaggeration but the taking of the recompense for *something that is not there*.**

**I record this as an observation about the word order:** the sentence takes as its subject not being praised but being praised **for what has not been done.** **And the verb is *yuḥibbūna* — "they love": that is, something at the level of desire is being named.**

**مَفَازَة** — root **ف-و-ز**: **deliverance, attaining what is sought.** **And the same root had occurred three verses earlier: *fa-qad fāza* (185).** **Two occurrences, three verses apart:**

| Verse | Wording | Who |
|---|---|---|
| **185** | *Fa-qad **fāza*** | **The one drawn away from the fire** |
| **188** | *Bi-**mafāzatin** mina'l-ʿadhāb* | **It is negated** |

**This is a repetition verifiable within the sūra.**

**And the verb *lā taḥsabanna* occurs twice in the verse** — this is the last occurrence of the root ح-س-ب in the sūra and the last of the six.

---

## 3/190-191 — إِنَّ فِى خَلْقِ ٱلسَّمَٰوَٰتِ وَٱلْأَرْضِ وَٱخْتِلَٰفِ ٱلَّيْلِ وَٱلنَّهَارِ لَءَايَٰتٍ لِّأُو۟لِى ٱلْأَلْبَٰبِ

*Inna fī khalqi's-samāwāti wa'l-arḍi wa'khtilāfi'l-layli wa'n-nahāri la-āyātin li-ūli'l-albāb · Alladhīna yadhkurūna'llāha qiyāman wa quʿūdan wa ʿalā junūbihim wa yatafakkarūna fī khalqi's-samāwāti wa'l-arḍ, rabbanā mā khalaqta hādhā bāṭilā, subḥānaka fa-qinā ʿadhāba'n-nār*

"**In the creation of the heavens and the earth and in the alternation of night and day there are signs for those who possess understanding.** · **They remember God standing, sitting and lying on their sides, and they reflect on the creation of the heavens and the earth: 'Our Lord! You did not create this in vain. Glory be to You; guard us from the punishment of the fire.'**"

---

**These two verses open the last block of the sūra. And the opening of the block makes a ring with the seventh verse of the sūra: the construct *ūlu'l-albāb* occurs twice in the sūra — at 7 and at 190. At the two ends of the sūra.** This was recorded in the table in the introduction.

### أُو۟لِى ٱلْأَلْبَٰب — root: ل-ب-ب

The root **ل-ب-ب**: **the kernel of a thing, its core.** *Lubb* — what is left of a fruit when the husk is thrown away.

**The root was treated at 12/111 in `012-yusuf.md`, at 14/52 in `014-ibrahim.md`, at 39/18 in `039-zumer.md`, at 13/19 in `013-rad.md` and at 89/5 in `089-fecr.md`. I rely on those treatments and do not repeat them.** The summary recorded there:

> **The edible part inside the shell of an almond or a walnut is called *lubb*.** **And the lexicographers record a difference between *ʿaql* and *lubb*: *lubb* is the intellect in its pure and working state.** **The intellect is what is inside the husk.**

**And I open out two of these supports separately, because both define *ūlu'l-albāb* by a *verb*:**

| Place | How *ūlu'l-albāb* is defined |
|---|---|
| **al-Zumar 39/18** | *Alladhīna **yastamiʿūna'l-qawla fa-yattabiʿūna aḥsanah*** — those who listen to the word and **follow the best of it** |
| **al-Raʿd 13/19** | *A-fa-man **yaʿlamu** annamā unzila ilayka min rabbika'l-ḥaqqu **ka-man huwa aʿmā*** — that the one who knows is not like the one who is blind |
| **Āl ʿImrān 3/190-191** | *Alladhīna **yadhkurūna**'llāha… **wa yatafakkarūna** fī khalqi's-samāwāti wa'l-arḍ* — **those who remember and reflect** |

**Three sūras, three separate definitions — and in all three the construct is tied to a verb.** This is a weave verifiable within the series.

**I record this as my own reading, and it is not binding:** *ūlu'l-albāb* occurs in the Qurʾān not as the name of a class but as **the name of those who do a certain work.** **And the pair of verbs Āl ʿImrān adds differs from the others: here the work is neither listening nor knowing — it is *remembering and reflecting*.**

### The standing of the two verbs together

| Verb | Root | Field |
|---|---|---|
| *Yadhkurūn* | **ذ-ك-ر** | **Remembering** — heart and tongue |
| *Yatafakkarūn* | **ف-ك-ر** | **Reflecting** — the mind |

**And the two are in one sentence. I record this as an observation about the word order:** the verse does not separate two distinct works; it gives both to the same person. **And the objects of the verbs differ: what is remembered is *God*, what is reflected on is *the creation*.**

**قِيَٰمًا وَقُعُودًا وَعَلَىٰ جُنُوبِهِمْ** — **three states: standing, sitting, lying on the side.**

**And this set of three is a count covering every posture a person can take.** **I record this as an observation about the word order:** the count does not tie remembering to any particular posture. **And the third (*ʿalā junūbihim*) conveys the weakest state — the state of one lying down.**

### مَا خَلَقْتَ هَٰذَا بَٰطِلًا

The root **ب-ط-ل**: **to go for nothing, to be void, to be unable to stand.** The lexicographers explain *bāṭil* as "what has no support to hold it up". **The root was treated at 38/27 in `038-sad.md`, at 21/16-18 in `021-enbiya.md`, at 22/62 in `022-hac.md` and at 47/3 in `047-muhammed.md`. I rely on those treatments.**

**And I open out two of these supports separately, because both give the same ruling *in God's own words*:**

| Place | Wording | Who says it |
|---|---|---|
| **al-Anbiyāʾ 21/16** | *Wa mā khalaqna's-samāʾa wa'l-arḍa wa mā baynahumā **lāʿibīn*** | **God** — "not in play" |
| **Ṣād 38/27** | *Wa mā khalaqna's-samāʾa wa'l-arḍa wa mā baynahumā **bāṭilā*** | **God** — "not in vain" |
| **Āl ʿImrān 3/191** | *Rabbanā **mā khalaqta hādhā bāṭilā*** | **A human being** — inside a prayer |

**And the difference is the point this commentary draws attention to, and it can be verified from the text.**

**In al-Anbiyāʾ and in Ṣād the ruling is a *declaration*: God qualifies His own act. In Āl ʿImrān the same ruling comes *from a human mouth*, and inside a prayer at that.**

**I record this as my own reading, and its ground is the subject of the three verses:** the sentence in Ṣād and the sentence in Āl ʿImrān are built with almost the same words (*bāṭilā*). **The one difference is who is speaking.** **And the sentence in Āl ʿImrān comes as the *result* of a reflecting** — because immediately before it *yatafakkarūna fī khalqi's-samāwāti wa'l-arḍ* has been said.

**That is, the sūra gives here, as a conclusion a human being reaches by reflecting, a ruling declared in the other two sūras.**

**And the distinction recorded at 38/27 in `038-sad.md` holds here too, and I rely on it:** *bāṭil* does not mean "false"; it means **what has no support, what falls of itself.** **That is, the sentence does not say "this creation is true"; it says "this creation is not without support".**

**سُبْحَٰنَكَ فَقِنَا عَذَابَ ٱلنَّارِ** — **and the prayer passes from the declaration of transcendence immediately to a request for protection.** **This transition will be taken up below, when the structure of the prayer is analysed.**

---

## 3/192-194 — رَبَّنَآ إِنَّكَ مَن تُدْخِلِ ٱلنَّارَ فَقَدْ أَخْزَيْتَهُۥ … رَبَّنَا وَءَاتِنَا مَا وَعَدتَّنَا عَلَىٰ رُسُلِكَ

*Rabbanā innaka man tudkhili'n-nāra fa-qad akhzaytah, wa mā li'ẓ-ẓālimīna min anṣār · Rabbanā innanā samiʿnā munādiyan yunādī li'l-īmāni an āminū bi-rabbikum fa-āmannā, rabbanā fa'ghfir lanā dhunūbanā wa kaffir ʿannā sayyiʾātinā wa tawaffanā maʿa'l-abrār · Rabbanā wa ātinā mā waʿadtanā ʿalā rusulika wa lā tukhzinā yawma'l-qiyāma, innaka lā tukhlifu'l-mīʿād*

"**Our Lord! Whomever You admit to the fire, You have disgraced him; and the wrongdoers have no helpers.** · **Our Lord! We heard a caller calling to faith: 'Believe in your Lord' — and we believed. Our Lord! Forgive us our sins, cover over our evil deeds, and take us with the righteous.** · **Our Lord! Give us what You promised us through Your messengers, and do not disgrace us on the day of resurrection. You do not fail Your appointment.**"

---

**This prayer is the longest in the sūra and does the same work as the closing prayer of al-Baqara (2/285-286). Its structure must be taken apart in a table.**

### The structure of the prayer: what is asked, in what order

**The prayer begins in the middle of verse 191 and ends at verse 194. The address *rabbanā* occurs four times, and each address opens a new section.**

| # | Address | What is asked / said | Kind |
|---|---|---|---|
| **1** | *Rabbanā* (191) | *Mā khalaqta hādhā bāṭilā* — **a statement** | **A statement** |
| | | *Subḥānaka* — **transcendence** | **Praise** |
| | | *Fa-qinā ʿadhāba'n-nār* — **protection from the fire** | **A request** |
| **2** | *Rabbanā* (192) | *Man tudkhili'n-nāra fa-qad akhzaytah* — **a statement** | **A statement** |
| **3** | *Rabbanā* (193) | *Innanā samiʿnā… fa-āmannā* — **a declaration** | **A declaration** |
| | *Rabbanā* (193) | *Fa'ghfir lanā dhunūbanā* — **forgiveness** | **A request** |
| | | *Wa kaffir ʿannā sayyiʾātinā* — **being covered** | **A request** |
| | | *Wa tawaffanā maʿa'l-abrār* — **the manner of the end** | **A request** |
| **4** | *Rabbanā* (194) | *Wa ātinā mā waʿadtanā ʿalā rusulik* — **the giving of the promise** | **A request** |
| | | *Wa lā tukhzinā yawma'l-qiyāma* — **not being disgraced** | **A request** |
| | | *Innaka lā tukhlifu'l-mīʿād* — **the ground** | **A ground** |

**And the structure that follows from the table must be recorded, and I give four observations. All four can be verified from the wording of the prayer.**

**One. The prayer does not begin with a *request*.** The first thing said is a **statement**: *mā khalaqta hādhā bāṭilā*. **And the statement is followed by a declaration of transcendence.** The request comes third.

**Two. The requests go from outside inward, then outward again.**

| Order | Request | Field |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | *Qinā ʿadhāba'n-nār* | **Protection from the outcome** |
| 2 | *Ighfir lanā **dhunūbanā*** | **Their own failings** |
| 3 | *Kaffir ʿannā **sayyiʾātinā*** | **Their own failings** |
| 4 | *Tawaffanā **maʿa'l-abrār*** | **The manner of the end** |
| 5 | *Ātinā **mā waʿadtanā*** | **What was promised** |
| 6 | *Wa lā **tukhzinā*** | **Not being disgraced** |

**That is, the prayer opens with protection, turns to its own failings, and then asks for the promise.** **And the promise is left to the last.**

**I record this as my own reading, and its ground is the order of the requests:** what was promised is asked for only **after** forgiveness of the failings has been asked. **And even when it is asked it is tied to a ground: *innaka lā tukhlifu'l-mīʿād* — that is, the ground of the request is not the asker's desert but the giver's word.**

**Three. Two verbs are asked separately: *ighfir* and *kaffir*.**

| Verb | Root | Its concrete picture | Object |
|---|---|---|---|
| *Ighfir* | **غ-ف-ر** | **To cover** — *mighfar*, what covers the head | *Dhunūbanā* — **sins** |
| *Kaffir* | **ك-ف-ر** | **To cover** — the root sense is to cover | *Sayyiʾātinā* — **evil deeds** |

**Both verbs convey covering, and their objects are two different words.** **The lexicographers transmit distinctions between *dhanb* and *sayyiʾa* and do not agree on them; I give no definite distinction.**

**I record this as an observation about the word order:** the prayer asks for the same work with two separate verbs and two separate objects. **And the concrete sense of both roots is to cover.**

**Four. In the prayer *being disgraced* (خ-ز-ي) occurs twice, and the two answer each other:**

| Verse | Wording | Who |
|---|---|---|
| **192** | *Man tudkhili'n-nāra fa-qad **akhzaytah*** | **Another** — a statement |
| **194** | *Wa lā **tukhzinā** yawma'l-qiyāma* | **Oneself** — a request |

**The same root, two verses apart: one a ruling, the other a prayer.** **I record this as an observation about the word order:** the prayer first accepts the ruling, then asks to remain outside its reach.

### مُنَادِيًا يُنَادِى لِلْإِيمَٰنِ

The root **ن-د-و/ي**: **to call out.** **And there are two words from the same root in the sentence: *munādiyan yunādī* — "a caller calling".** In Arabic this sets up **emphasis**.

**And the caller is not named: *munādiyan* is indefinite.** **I record this as an observation about the word order:** the prayer does not name the one who called; **it says that the call was heard.**

**And this part of the prayer conveys a *cause*: *samiʿnā… fa-āmannā* — we heard, and therefore we believed.** **And only after this does the request come: *rabbanā fa'ghfir lanā*.** **Two *fāʾ* particles one after the other set up a chain: hearing → faith → request.**

### A comparison with the close of al-Baqara

**It was recorded in the introduction to the sūra: both al-Baqara and Āl ʿImrān close with a prayer. Now I compare the two prayers.**

| | **al-Baqara 2/285-286** | **Āl ʿImrān 3/191-194** |
|---|---|---|
| **The opening** | *Āmana'r-rasūlu bimā unzila ilayh* — **a declaration** | *Mā khalaqta hādhā bāṭilā* — **a statement** |
| **The declaration of faith** | *Wa'l-muʾminūn… **āmannā wa aṭaʿnā*** | *Samiʿnā munādiyan… **fa-āmannā*** |
| **The first request** | *Ghufrānaka* | *Fa-**qinā** ʿadhāba'n-nār* |
| **The request about failings** | *Rabbanā lā tuʾākhidhnā in nasīnā aw akhṭaʾnā* | *Fa'**ghfir** lanā dhunūbanā wa **kaffir** ʿannā sayyiʾātinā* |
| **The request about burden** | *Wa lā taḥmil ʿalaynā **iṣran*** | (None) |
| **The close** | *Fa'nṣurnā ʿala'l-qawmi'l-kāfirīn* | *Innaka **lā tukhlifu'l-mīʿād*** |
| **The kind of the last sentence** | **A request** | **A ground** |

**And the last row is worth recording. I give this as my own reading, and it is not binding:** the prayer of al-Baqara ends with a request; the prayer of Āl ʿImrān ends with a **ground.** **That is, one leaves the last word with the one who asks, the other with the one who gives.**

**And the standing of the word *iṣr* in the two sūras must be recorded separately:** in the prayer of al-Baqara it was asked that an *iṣr* not be laid on; **while in verse 81 of Āl ʿImrān the taking up of the *iṣr* was affirmed.** **The two sūras use the same word in two different directions** — this was recorded above.

---

## 3/195 — أَنِّى لَآ أُضِيعُ عَمَلَ عَٰمِلٍ مِّنكُم مِّن ذَكَرٍ أَوْ أُنثَىٰ بَعْضُكُم مِّنۢ بَعْضٍ

*Fa'stajāba lahum rabbuhum annī lā uḍīʿu ʿamala ʿāmilin minkum min dhakarin aw unthā, baʿḍukum min baʿḍ, fa'lladhīna hājarū wa ukhrijū min diyārihim wa ūdhū fī sabīlī wa qātalū wa qutilū la-ukaffiranna ʿanhum sayyiʾātihim wa la-udkhilannahum jannātin tajrī min taḥtiha'l-anhār, thawāban min ʿindi'llāh, wa'llāhu ʿindahū ḥusnu'th-thawāb*

"**Their Lord answered them: 'I do not let the work of any worker among you go to waste, male or female; you are of one another.'** Those who migrated, who were driven from their homes, who were hurt in My way, who fought and were killed — **I shall surely cover over their evil deeds and shall surely admit them to gardens beneath which rivers flow.** A recompense from God; and with God is the best of recompense."

---

**This verse is the answer to the long prayer, and the first sentence of the answer enters a note. It will be treated in detail.**

### فَٱسْتَجَابَ لَهُمْ رَبُّهُمْ — the answer itself

**And the word order must be recorded: after a prayer running four verses the answer comes at once, with the particle *fa*.**

The root **ج-و-ب**: **to cut, to cleave; and from this, to answer.** **In Form X (*istajāba*) it conveys *responding*.** **And the same verb occurred at verse 172: *alladhīna'stajābū li'llāhi wa'r-rasūl*.**

| Verse | Verb | Who to whom |
|---|---|---|
| **172** | *Istajābū **li'llāhi wa'r-rasūl*** | **The human being** → to the call |
| **195** | *Fa'**stajāba** lahum rabbuhum* | **The Lord** → to the prayer |

**The same verb, two directions.** This is a repetition verifiable within the sūra.

### مِّن ذَكَرٍ أَوْ أُنثَىٰ — the removal of the distinction

**And the word order of the sentence must be recorded:**

*Lā uḍīʿu **ʿamala ʿāmilin** minkum **min dhakarin aw unthā***

| Element | Wording | Its work |
|---|---|---|
| **The object** | *ʿAmala **ʿāmilin*** | Two words from the same root — **the work and the worker** |
| **Note 1** | *Minkum* | **Among you** |
| **Note 2** | *Min dhakarin aw unthā* | **Male or female** |
| **The ground** | *Baʿḍukum min baʿḍ* | **You are of one another** |

**And the construction *ʿamala ʿāmilin* is a figure of *ishtiqāq* in Arabic (derivation from the same root) and reinforces: "the work of the worker".**

**And the place of the second note in the sentence must be recorded: *min dhakarin aw unthā* is an explanation of the word *ʿāmilin*.** **That is, the sentence does this: first a general subject is set down (*ʿāmil* — worker), then it is written separately that this subject covers both sexes.**

**I record this as an observation about the word order:** the word *ʿāmil* is masculine in form in Arabic, and in the ordinary working of the language it covers women as well. **The sentence does not content itself with this; it adds the note separately.**

### A comparison with 33/35

**`033-ahzab.md` 33/35 is the central text of this matter in the series, and I rely on it. What was recorded there:**

> **This verse is the longest parallel enumeration of man and woman in the Qurʾān: ten pairs, twenty words.**
>
> **And this was recorded there: an alternative was possible.** In Arabic the masculine plural can be used for a mixed company, and that is the ordinary working of the language. **Had the verse gone on *inna'l-muslimīna wa'l-muʾminīna…* it would grammatically have covered women too, and the verse would have been half the length. The Qurʾān does not do this; it raises ten words to twenty.**

**And now I compare the two verses:**

| | **al-Aḥzāb 33/35** | **Āl ʿImrān 3/195** |
|---|---|---|
| **Method** | **Counting every attribute twice** — ten pairs | **A note in a single sentence** — *min dhakarin aw unthā* |
| **Length** | **Twenty words** | **Four words** |
| **Subject** | **Attributes** — who one is | **Deed** — what one has done |
| **The ground added** | (None) | ***Baʿḍukum min baʿḍ*** |
| **The close** | *Aʿadda'llāhu lahum maghfiratan wa ajran ʿaẓīmā* | *La-ukaffiranna… wa la-udkhilannahum* |
| **Context** | **A list of attributes** | **The answer to a prayer** |

**The two verses do the same work by two different methods: one by repeating, the other by entering a note.**

**And what Āl ʿImrān adds is the ground. I open this out separately.**

### بَعْضُكُم مِّنۢ بَعْضٍ — the ground itself

**"Some of you are of others."**

**And the structure of the construction must be recorded: the word *baʿḍ* twice, with the particle *min* between them.**

**And the same construction had occurred twice more in the sūra:**

| Verse | Wording | Subject |
|---|---|---|
| **34** | *Dhurriyyatan **baʿḍuhā min baʿḍ*** | **Descent** — the chain of prophets |
| **64** | *Wa lā yattakhidha **baʿḍunā baʿḍan** arbābā* | **Hierarchy** — it is refused |
| **195** | ***Baʿḍukum min baʿḍ*** | **Sex** — the distinction is lifted |

**Three occurrences, in a single sūra.** This is a repetition verifiable within the sūra and worth recording.

**I record this as my own reading, and it is not binding:** the same construction describes three separate relations three times in the sūra — **the chain of descent, hierarchy among people, and sex.** **And in all three what it does is the same: to bind the two sides to one another and to prevent a step being set between them.**

**And I record its link with verse 64 separately:** there the construction stood inside the item forbidding people to *take one another as lords*. **Here it is the ground of the sentence saying that the work of man and of woman is weighed by the same measure.** **The same construction, against two different inequalities.**

### Five verbs

**And in the continuation of the verse five verbs are counted, and three of them are passive:**

| # | Verb | Voice |
|---|---|---|
| **1** | *Hājarū* | **Active** — they migrated |
| **2** | *Wa **ukhrijū** min diyārihim* | **Passive** — they were driven out |
| **3** | *Wa **ūdhū** fī sabīlī* | **Passive** — they were hurt |
| **4** | *Wa qātalū* | **Active** — they fought |
| **5** | *Wa **qutilū*** | **Passive** — they were killed |

**And the word order must be recorded: active and passive verbs run into one another.**

**I record this as an observation about the word order:** the list counts both what was done and what was suffered. **And no distinction is made between the two — all five are tied to the subject of the same sentence.**

And the note *fī sabīlī*: a first-person construct — "in **My** way". **This is its only occurrence in the verse; elsewhere in the sūra the construction is *fī sabīli'llāh*.**

---

## 3/196-198 — لَا يَغُرَّنَّكَ تَقَلُّبُ ٱلَّذِينَ كَفَرُوا۟ فِى ٱلْبِلَٰدِ

*Lā yaghurrannaka taqallubu'lladhīna kafarū fi'l-bilād · Matāʿun qalīlun thumma maʾwāhum jahannam, wa biʾsa'l-mihād · Lākini'lladhīna'ttaqaw rabbahum lahum jannātun tajrī min taḥtiha'l-anhāru khālidīna fīhā nuzulan min ʿindi'llāh, wa mā ʿinda'llāhi khayrun li'l-abrār*

"**Do not let the moving about of those who deny in the lands deceive you.** · **It is a small enjoyment; then their refuge is hell. What an evil bed!** · **But for those who guard themselves before their Lord there are gardens beneath which rivers flow; they abide there. A hospitality from God. And what is with God is better for the righteous.**"

### تَقَلُّب — root: ق-ل-ب

The root **ق-ل-ب**: **to turn, to revolve.** **In Form V (*taqallub*): to turn over and over, to pass from one state to another.**

**And the occurrences of the root in the sūra must be recorded:**

| Verse | Wording | What |
|---|---|---|
| **7, 8, 103, 126, 151, 154, 156, 159** | *Qulūb* | **Heart** |
| **127** | *Fa-**yanqalibū** khāʾibīn* | **To turn back** — in despair |
| **144** | *In**qalabtum** ʿalā aʿqābikum* | **To turn back** |
| **149** | *Fa-**tanqalibū** khāsirīn* | **To turn back** — in loss |
| **174** | *Fa'n**qalabū** bi-niʿmatin* | **To turn back** — with a favour |
| **196** | ***Taqallubu**'lladhīna kafarū fi'l-bilād* | **Moving about** |

**The root is one of the densest in the sūra and occurs both in the sense of *heart* and in the sense of *turning*.** **I record this as an observation and build no ruling on the two senses; the lexicographers already explain the heart by the sense of "what turns continually".**

**And the verb *lā yaghurrannaka* must be recorded: the root غ-ر-ر occurred at verse 185 as *matāʿu'l-ghurūr*.** **Two occurrences, eleven verses apart: one for the life of this world, the other for an appearance.**

**مِهَاد / نُزُل** — the opposition of the two words must be recorded:

| Word | Root | Sense |
|---|---|---|
| **مِهَاد** | **م-ه-د** | **A bed, a spread-out couch** — *mahd*: a cradle |
| **نُزُل** | **ن-ز-ل** | **What is prepared for a guest** — prepared for one who alights |

**Both words convey a *preparing*.** **I record this as an observation about the words:** the two opposite outcomes are given with two separate words for "a place prepared". **And *mihād* is a bed, *nuzul* a hospitality.**

---

## 3/199-200 — وَإِنَّ مِنْ أَهْلِ ٱلْكِتَٰبِ لَمَن يُؤْمِنُ بِٱللَّهِ · يَٰٓأَيُّهَا ٱلَّذِينَ ءَامَنُوا۟ ٱصْبِرُوا۟ وَصَابِرُوا۟ وَرَابِطُوا۟

*Wa inna min ahli'l-kitābi la-man yuʾminu bi'llāhi wa mā unzila ilaykum wa mā unzila ilayhim khāshiʿīna li'llāhi lā yashtarūna bi-āyāti'llāhi thamanan qalīlā, ulāʾika lahum ajruhum ʿinda rabbihim, inna'llāha sarīʿu'l-ḥisāb · Yā ayyuha'lladhīna āmanu'ṣbirū wa ṣābirū wa rābiṭū wa'ttaqu'llāha laʿallakum tufliḥūn*

"**Among the People of the Book there are those who believe in God, and in what has been sent down to you and in what has been sent down to them; humbling themselves before God, they do not sell the signs of God for a small price. Theirs is their reward with their Lord.** God is swift in reckoning. · **You who believe! Be steadfast, vie in steadfastness, hold fast together, and guard yourselves before God, that you may prosper.**"

### 3/199 — the close of the sūra's line on the People of the Book

**And this verse is the sūra's last word about the People of the Book. And it is a favourable word.**

**And the particle *min* once again: *wa inna min ahli'l-kitābi la-man yuʾmin*.** **And this is the eighth occurrence in the sūra; it was recorded in the table above.**

**And the word order of the verse must be recorded, because in counting what is believed in a sequence is followed:**

| # | What is believed in | Wording |
|---|---|---|
| **1** | **God** | *Bi'llāh* |
| **2** | **What has been sent down to you** | *Wa mā unzila **ilaykum*** |
| **3** | **What has been sent down to them** | *Wa mā unzila **ilayhim*** |

**And the order must be recorded: "what has been sent down to you" comes first, "what has been sent down to them" after.**

**I record this as an observation about the word order:** the sentence counts faith in their own books as well and does not negate it. **And at 29/46 in `029-ankebut.md` the same order is reversed** (*āmannā bi'lladhī unzila ilaynā **wa** unzila ilaykum* — there "what has been sent down to us" comes first). **In both verses the two revelations are named together; the order changes according to who is speaking.** **This is a fact verifiable within the series.**

**خَٰشِعِينَ لِلَّهِ** — root **خ-ش-ع**: **to be lowered, to bow, the sinking of the voice.** **The root was treated at 33/35 in `033-ahzab.md`, and it was recorded there: the lexicographers give the earth as an instance of the root's concrete sense — *khashaʿati'l-arḍ*, the ground sank, it was lowered.** **I rely on that treatment.**

**لَا يَشْتَرُونَ بِـَٔايَٰتِ ٱللَّهِ ثَمَنًا قَلِيلًا** — **and the construction occurs in the sūra for the third time, and this time negated:**

| Verse | Wording | Mood |
|---|---|---|
| **77** | *Yashtarūna bi-ʿahdi'llāhi wa aymānihim **thamanan qalīlā*** | **Affirmative** — they do it |
| **187** | *Wa'shtaraw bihī **thamanan qalīlā*** | **Affirmative** — they did it |
| **199** | *Lā yashtarūna bi-āyāti'llāhi **thamanan qalīlā*** | **Negative** — they do not do it |

**Three occurrences, and the third is the opposite of the first two.** This is a repetition verifiable within the sūra.

**I record this as my own reading, and it is not binding:** the sūra uses the same verb three times, and in the last occurrence it names those who do **not** do the thing named in the first two. **And the last occurrence is the sūra's last sentence about the People of the Book.** **That is, the sūra closes with the negative of the same word the line it opened with a severe statement.**

**أُو۟لَٰٓئِكَ لَهُمْ أَجْرُهُمْ عِندَ رَبِّهِمْ** — *ulāʾika lahum ajruhum ʿinda rabbihim*: **the reward is given without qualification.** **The same construction occurs at 2/62 and 2/112 in `002-bakara.md` and was treated there. I rely on those treatments.**

### 3/200 — a close with four commands

*Iṣbirū wa ṣābirū wa rābiṭū wa'ttaqu'llāh*

**And the sūra closes with four commands. Three from the same field, the fourth from the beginning of the sūra.**

| # | Command | Root | Form | What |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **1** | *Iṣbirū* | **ص-ب-ر** | **Form I** | **Be steadfast** |
| **2** | *Wa ṣābirū* | **ص-ب-ر** | **Form III (*mufāʿala*)** | **Vie with one another in steadfastness** |
| **3** | *Wa rābiṭū* | **ر-ب-ط** | **Form III (*mufāʿala*)** | **Hold fast together / stand on guard** |
| **4** | *Wa'ttaqu'llāh* | **و-ق-ي** | **Form VIII** | **Guard yourselves** |

**And the word order of the first three commands must be recorded, and this is the most important fact of the verse.**

**One. The first two commands are from the same root and their forms differ.**

***Iṣbirū* is Form I: a person's being steadfast on his own. *Ṣābirū* is Form III (*mufāʿala*), and in Arabic this form conveys *reciprocity*.**

**I record this as an observation about the words and rely on the lexicographers' account of the forms:** the same root, in two forms, conveys two different pieces of work. **The first is the work of one person, the second a mutual work.**

**And the lexicographers differ over with whom *ṣābirū* is mutual, and the disagreement is not to be concealed:**

| Reading | With whom *ṣābirū* is |
|---|---|
| 1 | **With the enemy** — to endure beyond their enduring |
| 2 | **With one another** — mutual steadfastness within the community |
| 3 | **In general** — to vie in steadfastness |

**All three are transmitted; I do not impose a preference.**

**Two. The third command is from a new root: ر-ب-ط.**

The root **ر-ب-ط**: **to bind.** *Ribāṭ* — a bond; and **the place where a horse is tethered.** *Rabaṭa ʿalā qalbih* — he bound his heart, he gave him firmness. **And *murābaṭa* is used in the sense of being tethered in a place and waiting, standing guard.**

**And the lexicographers differ over the sense of the verb, and the disagreement is not to be concealed:**

| Reading | What *rābiṭū* is |
|---|---|
| 1 | **To stand guard at a frontier** — to tether the horses and wait |
| 2 | **To wait from one prayer to the next** — a continuous attachment |
| 3 | **To bind yourselves to one another** — to stand together as a community |
| 4 | **To bind the heart** — firmness |

**All four are transmitted; I do not impose a preference and I draw no legal ruling.**

**But one datum of the text can be recorded, and I record it: *rābiṭū* too is in Form III — that is, the form conveying reciprocity.** **That the second and third commands are in the same form is a fact of word order.**

**Three. And the order of the four commands sets up a structure. I give this as my own reading, and it is not binding:**

| Command | Who | Field |
|---|---|---|
| *Iṣbirū* | **One person** | **Within himself** |
| *Ṣābirū* | **Mutual** | **With another** |
| *Rābiṭū* | **Mutual** | **Standing together** |
| *Ittaqu'llāh* | **Everyone** | **Before God** |

**The order goes from the singular to the plural, then upward.** **And the last command comes with a word that has run since the second verse of the sūra: *taqwā*.**

### The last word of the sūra: تُفْلِحُونَ

The root **ف-ل-ح**. **The lexicographers give the concrete sense of the root as *to cleave*: *falaḥa'l-arḍa* — he cleaved the ground, he ploughed it. And *fallāḥ* is a farmer.**

**And the word occurs twice in the sūra: at 130 and at 200.**

| Verse | Wording | Context |
|---|---|---|
| **130** | *Wa'ttaqu'llāha laʿallakum **tufliḥūn*** | **After the prohibition of *ribā*** |
| **200** | *Wa'ttaqu'llāha laʿallakum **tufliḥūn*** | **The last sentence of the sūra** |

**Two occurrences, and both are built with the same two words: *wa'ttaqu'llāha laʿallakum tufliḥūn*.** **The same sentence, twice in a single sūra.** This is a repetition verifiable within the sūra.

**And at verse 104 the same root had occurred once more, as a noun: *wa ulāʾika humu'l-mufliḥūn*.** **Three occurrences, in a single sūra.**

**I record this as an observation and rely on the concrete sense of the root:** the picture of the root is a **cleaving** — the opening of what is closed, the reaching of what is under the soil. **And the sūra, after proceeding for two hundred verses as a text that opens what is closed, ends with this word.** **I give this as an observation about the word; I make no claim about reckonings or numbers.**

---

## The sūra as a whole — looking back

### Repetitions within the sūra that can be verified

**The table below is not interpretation but an inventory of repetitions verifiable from the sūra's own text. It completes the table in the introduction.**

| Root / word / pattern | Where | What it does |
|---|---|---|
| **مِنْ (partitive) + the People of the Book** | 69, 72, 75, 78, 100, 110, 113, 199 | **A division in eight places** — in none of them is a blanket ruling built |
| **سَوَآء** | *Kalimatin **sawāʾin*** (64) — *laysū **sawāʾan*** (113) | **Common ground, and "they are not all alike"** |
| **بَعْضُكُم مِّنۢ بَعْضٍ** | 34 (descent), 64 (hierarchy), 195 (sex) | **The same construction against three separate inequalities** |
| **حَبْل** | *Ḥabli'llāh* (103) — *ḥablin mina'llāhi wa ḥablin mina'n-nās* (112) | **The rope held on to and the bond that protects** |
| **ع-ص-م** | *Yaʿtaṣim bi'llāh* (101) — *iʿtaṣimū bi-ḥabli'llāhi **jamīʿan*** (103) | **Singular and plural holding fast** |
| **ف-ر-ق** | *Lā tafarraqū* (103) — *tafarraqū* (105) | **The ban and the breach** |
| **ف-ش-ل** | *An **tafshalā*** (122) — *fashiltum* (152) | **An approach and a coming to pass** |
| **و-ه-ن** | *Lā tahinū* (139) — *mā wahanū* (146) | **The prohibition and the instance** |
| **ط-و-ع / ع-ص-ي** | *Aṭīʿu'llāh* (32, 132) — *ʿaṣaytum* (152) | **Command, command, breach** |
| **ح-س-ب** | 78, 142, 169, 178, 180, 188 — and *ḥasbuna'llāh* (173) | **Six "supposings", one "sufficing"** |
| **أَمْر** | 128, 152, 154 (twice), 159 | **The affair quarrelled over, the owner of the decision, the affair to consult on** |
| **مِنْ عِندِ** | 37, 78, 126, 165 | **The naming of the source** — the last occurrence "from yourselves" |
| **مِنۢ بَعْدِ مَا** | 19 (*ʿilm*), 61 (*ʿilm*), 105 (*bayyināt*), 152 (*mā tuḥibbūn*) | **Knowledge and success, in the same pattern** |
| **بَعْدَ إِيمَٰنِ-** | 86, 90, 100, 106 | **Turning back: statement, ruling, warning, question** |
| **أَعْقَاب** | 144 (twice), 149 | **Turning back on the heels** |
| **ثَمَنًا قَلِيلًا** | 77, 187 — and **negated** at 199 | **The contrast that closes the sūra's line on the People of the Book** |
| **مَا كَانَ لِ-** | *Li-basharin* (79) — *li-nabiyyin* (161) | **The pattern of impossibility, on two subjects** |
| **مَا … إِلَّا (restriction)** | 65, 126, 144, 185 | **The pattern of limitation** |
| **أُخْرِجَتْ / أُوتُوا۟ لِلنَّاسِ** | *Li'n-nās* — 96, 104 (indirectly), 110, 138, 187 | **The note of breadth** |
| **ل-و-ي** | *Yalwūna alsinatahum* (78) — *lā talwūna ʿalā aḥad* (153) | **The tongue is twisted, the neck is not turned** |
| **ق-و-م** | *Qāʾiman bi'l-qisṭ* (18), *qāʾimā* (75), *maqām* (97), *ummatun qāʾima* (113) | **The same root, four separate pieces of work** |
| **ص-ب-ر + ت-ق-ي** | *In taṣbirū wa tattaqū* — 120, 125, 186 | **Three times, the same pair** |
| **أ-ص-ر** | *Akhadhtum ʿalā dhālikum iṣrī* (81) | **The opposite of al-Baqara 2/286** |
| **ف-ل-ح** | *Al-mufliḥūn* (104) — *tufliḥūn* (130, 200) | **The last word of the sūra** |
| **ٱلْأَعْلَوْن** | 139 — with the note *in kuntum muʾminīn* | **Qualified**; one verse later limited by *nudāwiluhā* |
| **د-و-ل** | *Nudāwiluhā bayna'n-nās* (140) | **The key to the sūra's reading of defeat** |
