| **أَمْر بِٱلْمَعْرُوف** | 104, 110, 114 | **The third for a community from among the People of the Book** |
| **ٱلْأَمَانَة / أَدَاء** | *Yuʾaddihī ilayk* (75 — twice) | **The returning of a trust, with a division** |
| **خ-ز-ي** | *Akhzaytah* (192) — *lā tukhzinā* (194) | **A ruling and a prayer** |
| **غ-ر-ر** | *Matāʿu'l-ghurūr* (185) — *lā yaghurrannaka* (196) | **The two faces of being deceived** |
| **ق-ل-ب** | *Qulūb* (many) — *inqalabtum* (144), *tanqalibū* (149), *inqalabū* (174), *taqallub* (196) | **One of the densest roots in the sūra** |

### The points where the blocks join

| Link | Where | What |
|---|---|---|
| **I → III** | *Min baʿdi mā jāʾahumu'l-ʿilm* (19) → (61, 105) | **The same note in three blocks** |
| **III → IV** | *Arbāb* (64) → *arbāb* (80) → *iʿtaṣimū* (101, 103) | **Hierarchy and holding fast** |
| **IV → V** | *Lā tafarraqū* (103) → *tanāzaʿtum* (152) → *la'nfaḍḍū* (159) | **Three states of scattering** |
| **Within V** | *Tafshalā* (122) → *fashiltum* (152) → *ʿafā ʿankum* (152, 155) | **Failing and pardon, three times** |
| **V → VII** | *Shāwirhum fi'l-amr* (159) ← *tanāzaʿtum fi'l-amr* (152) | **The same construction, two separate works** |
| **I ↔ VII** | *Ūlu'l-albāb* (7) ↔ *ūlu'l-albāb* (190) | **The two ends of the sūra** |
| **III ↔ VII** | *Thamanan qalīlā* (77) ↔ *lā yashtarūna… thamanan qalīlā* (199) | **The close of the line** |

**In the places below more than one view has been transmitted, given in a table, and none of them preferred.**

| **73** | The attachment of the clause *an yuʾtā aḥadun* |

| **97** | Whether the sentence *wa man dakhalahū kāna āminā* is a statement or a performative |
| **97** | The extent of *istiṭāʿa* — **a legal disagreement, not entered** |
| **102** | The measure of the construction *ḥaqqa tuqātih* |
| **103** | What *ḥablu'llāh* is |
| **104** | Whether the *min* in *minkum* is partitive or explicative |
| **110** | The time conveyed by *kuntum* |

| **128** | What *aw yatūba ʿalayhim* attaches to |

| **200** | With whom *ṣābirū* is |
| **200** | The sense of *rābiṭū* — **no legal conclusion drawn** |

### The measure the sūra leaves

**The sūra is a text that does the same work in three separate fields, and this was recorded in the introduction: in reading the text (7), in speaking with the other (64), in reading defeat (152, 165) — in all three what is asked is that a person see his own share.**

**And at the end of two hundred verses two notes the sūra itself sets down stand out, and both are from the text itself:**

**One. No blanket ruling is built about any community.** Beside its heaviest statements the sūra each time places a *min*, an exception, a division or the note of the interrogative mood. **And in two places (113-115 and 199) it writes this plainly.**

**Two. Superiority is not given as a possession.** The sentence *wa antumu'l-aʿlawn* is tied to a condition (139), is limited one verse later by *nudāwiluhā bayna'n-nās* (140), and the sentence *kuntum khayra ummatin* is tied to three verbs (110).

**Across two hundred verses the sūra tells a community how it is to build itself; and beside everything it builds it places a caveat.**

---

## Where this section does not speak with certainty

**At many points throughout the text definite language was deliberately not used; the reader needs to know these.**

- **In the Uḥud block (121-179) no personal name was given, no detail of any report was transmitted, no unit or tribe was named.** The reason was written plainly in the text: **the verses are without names from beginning to end.** The only thing recorded is that the classical sources identify this block as Uḥud and that this identification is widespread. **No word-for-word ascription to any particular report was made.**

- **The same limit was kept in the discussion of the mutual imprecation (61) and was written plainly there.** That limit was not returned to in this section.

- **At 3/85 and at 3/19 the debate about excommunication was not entered.** In both places it was recorded that what the verses give is a **measure**, and that applying the measure to persons is not the work of this commentary.

- **At 3/110 no conclusion of superiority for any people, race or state was drawn**, and it was shown from three separate places that this is not a sensitivity brought in from outside but the verse's own word order. **Present-day politics was not entered.**

- **No unconditional superiority was drawn from the construction *wa antumu'l-aʿlawn* at 3/139;** the limit set at 47/35 in `047-muhammed.md` was kept exactly, and the verse's own conditional note (*in kuntum muʾminīn*) and the note of the following verse (*nudāwiluhā bayna'n-nās*) were shown.

- **At 3/75, 3/112 and 3/118 no blanket ruling was built about any religious or ethnic group.** In all three the verse's own note of extent (a division, an exception, a list of grounds) was shown from the text.

- **At 3/96-97 no legal ruling was issued.** The extent of *istiṭāʿa*, the detail of the rulings of pilgrimage, and the conclusions drawn from the sentence *man dakhalahū kāna āminā* were named **in the language of transmission**; `002-bakara.md` 2/196-203 and `022-hac.md` 22/26-33 were relied on.

- **At 3/130 no legal ruling was issued about *ribā*.** Whether the note *aḍʿāfan muḍāʿafatan* narrows the ruling was given as two views; `002-bakara.md` 2/275-281 was relied on.

- **At 3/200 no legal conclusion was drawn from the verb *rābiṭū*.** Four readings were given in a table and no preference was made.

- **At 3/124-125 no description whatever was entered of the form or the presence of the angels or of the sense of the attribute *musawwimīn*.** The two attributes the verse gives were taken as sufficient.

- **At 3/169-171 nothing the text does not say about the nature of the life of those slain was said.** The limit set at 2/154 in `002-bakara.md` was kept exactly; the four verbs the verse gives (*aḥyāʾ*, *yurzaqūn*, *fariḥīn*, *yastabshirūn*) were taken as sufficient.

- **The reports about who spoke the saying at 3/181 were not entered, and no personal or group name was given.**

- **Where differences of recitation were recorded (3/81, 3/140, 3/161) no reciter's name was written**, because it cannot be given with certainty.

- **In the discussion of the verse-endings no claim about numbers or reckoning was made;** this was recorded in the introduction to the sūra as well.

- **The link drawn between the root picture of the word *tufliḥūn* at 3/200 (to cleave) and the sūra as a whole was given as an observation about the word;** it was not claimed to be a deliberate choice.

- **My own inferences were marked off throughout the text with the phrases "I record this as my own reading" or "I record this as an observation", and it was stated that they are not binding.** None of them is a transmitted report.
